Such appeal shall be taken by giving a notice of the appeal in open court at the time of the rendition of judgment, or by the service and filing of a notice of appeal within twenty days from and after the entry of the judgment. 

The appellant shall have fifty days after the entry of judgment within which to serve and file his opening brief, and the respondent shall have thirty days after the service of the opening brief in which to answer. The appellant shall have twenty days after the service of respondent's brief in which to reply. After the filing of the briefs, or the expiration of the time for filing briefs, the cause shall be assigned for hearing at the earliest motion day of the court, or at such other time as the court shall fix, and the clerk of the court shall notify the attorneys for the respective parties of the date set for the hearing in time to permit them to participate in the hearing. 

The original transcript of the record and testimony filed in the superior court in any action to review an order of the commission,

together with a transcript of the proceedings in the superior court, shall constitute the record on appeal. 

No appeal shall be effective, when taken by a public service company or a complainant, unless a cost bond on appeal in the sum of two hundred dollars is filed within five days after the service of the notice of appeal.

The superior court may suspend its judgment pending the hearing in the supreme court, upon the filing of a bond, with a good and sufficient surety, conditioned as provided for bonds upon actions for review, or upon such other terms and conditions as it may deem proper.

The general laws relating to appeals to the supreme court shall, so far as applicable and not in conflict with the provisions of this chapter, apply to appeals taken under the provisions hereof. [1911 

c 117 § 88; RRS § 10430.] 

1. Cited in 42 Fed. (2d) 775.

Order requiring certain improvement plans to be approved is not appealable. State ex rel. Pacific L. & P. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 81 Wash. 24 (1914). 

2. Filing of briefs within 50 days is not mandatory and jurisdictional, and that time may be extended. United Truck Lines, Inc. v. Dept. of Public 

Works, 181 Wash. 318 (1935). 

3. Where an action for less than $200 does not involve the validity of a statute, but simply the construction of a tariff rule promulgated pursuant to statute, the supreme court has no jurisdiction, and the appeal will be dismissed. State ex rel. Home Tel. & Tel. Co. v. Hurn, 106 Wash. 362 (1919). 

4. The provisions of this section with regard to the taking of an appeal within 20 days are mandatory notwithstanding use of the term "may." State ex rel. Dept. of Public Service v. Northern Pac. Ry. Co., 200 Wash 663 (1939). 

5. The requirement of this section that appeal be taken within 20 days after entry of judgment is not dispensed with by Const. Article IV, sec. 4, giving the supreme court original jurisdiction in habeas corpus, quo warranto and mandamus as to all state officers, there being nothing in that article authorizing a review of a judgment of the superior court in a case in which the supreme court has no jurisdiction because of failure to give timely notice of appeal. Id. 

6. This section should be construed liberally in aid of the appellate jurisdiction of the supreme court. State ex rel. Pac. T. & T. Co. v. Dept. of Public 

Service, 19 Wn. (2d) 200 (1943). 

7. On appeal to the supreme court from an order of the superior court reversing an order of the Department suspending tariffs and effecting a general rate reduction, if a party deems matters which occurred before the superior court to be relevant and of importance to the proper determination of the questions to be decided, such matters may be made a part of the record on appeal by statement of facts or bill of exception. Id. 

8. A judgment of the superior court entered upon remittitur in obedience to an order of the supreme court becomes the law of the case. State ex rel. 

Seattle v. Dept. of Public Utilities, 33 Wn. (2d) 896 (1949). 

9. Denial by the supreme court of a petition for recall of a remittitur and correction of a judgment with regard to occupation, use-of-streets and other taxes, in effect approved the judgment entered by the superior court. Id. 

80.04.200 Rehearing before commission. Any public service company affected by any order of the commission, and deeming itself aggrieved, may, after the expiration of two years from the effective

date of the order, petition the commission for a rehearing upon the matters involved in the order, setting forth the grounds and reasons for a rehearing, which may consist of changed conditions since the issuance of the order, or the showing of a result injuriously affecting the petitioner which was not considered at the former hearing, or that the effect of the order has been such as was not contemplated by the commission or the petitioner, or for any good and sufficient cause which for any reason was not considered and determined in such former hearing.

Upon the filing of the petition, such proceedings shall be had thereon as are provided for hearings upon complaint, and the order may be reviewed as are other orders of the commission. No order superseding an order denying a rehearing shall be granted by the court pending the review.

In case any order shall not be reviewed, but shall be complied with by the company, a petition for rehearing may be filed within six months from the effective date of the order, and the proceedings thereon shall be as in this section provided. 

The commission may permit the filing of a petition for rehearing at any time. No order of the commission upon a rehearing shall affect any right of action or penalty accruing under the original order unless so ordered by the commission. [1911 c 117 § 89; RRS § 10431.] 

1. Rehearings intended only to correct defects and mistakes existing or inherent in the order at time of its entry and is not the the proper procedure to establish a tariff increasing rates which was filed since the hearing. Op. 

Atty. Gen. Nov. 4, 1920. 

2. Purpose of this section is to provide for changed conditions since the issuance of the order or injurious result not anticipated in the order. P. S. 

Elec. Co. v. Lee, 207 Fed. 860. 

3. Carrier secured modification of order requiring station without changed conditions. Dryden Commercial Club v. Dept. of Public Works, 142 Wash 317 

(1927). 

4. Order of an administrative tribunal is not invalidated by admission of matter not admissible under rules of evidence or for mere error in reasoning. 

Northern Pac. Ry. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 268 U. S. 39, 69 L. Ed. 837. 

Reversing (on a different point) 125 Wash. 584 (1923). 

80.04.210 Commission may change orders. The commission may at any time, upon notice to the company affected, and after opportunity to be heard as provided in the case of complaints, rescind, alter, or amend any order or rule made, issued, or promulgated by it, and any order or rule rescinding, altering, or amending any prior order or rule shall, when served upon the company affected, have the same effect as original orders and rules. [1911 c 117 § 90; RRS § 10432.] 

1. Department cannot reopen proceeding after final order of dismissal, when the injured party sought no writ of review, and when four years had passed and the statute of limitations for its presentment has run. N. Pac. R. 

Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 122 Wash. 673 (1923).

2. Department on its own motion changed former order and ordered telechronometers out of Everett. State ex rel. American Telechronometer Co. v. 

Baker, 164 Wash. 483 (1931). 

3. Reservation of jurisdiction for possible future action by the Department was immaterial in view of this section. State ex rel. O.-W. R. & N. Co. 

v. Walla Walla County, 5 Wn. (2d) 95 (1940). 

4. For other cases on this point, see: State ex rel. American Telechronometer Co. v. Baker, 164 Wash. 483 (1931); Bingen v. Dept. of Public Works, 164

Wash. 563 (1931); N. P. Railroad Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 122 Wash. 673 

(1923). 

5. Procedure to rescind order of Department. Op. Atty. Gen. February 4, 1933. 

6. Discussion of Department's right to rescind order. Id. 

80.04.220 Reparations. When complaint has been made to the commission concerning the reasonableness of any rate, toll, rental, or charge for any service performed by a public service company, and it has been investigated by the commission, and the commission has determined that the company has charged an excessive or exorbitant amount for the service, and that any party complainant is entitled to an award of damages, the commission shall order that the company pay to the complainant the excess amount found to have been charged, whether it was charged and collected before or after the filing of the complaint, with interest from the date of col lection of the excess amount. [1943 c 258 §1; 1937 c 29 § 1; Rem. 

Supp. 1943 § 10433.] 

1. Where a telephone company put into effect and collected a schedule of rates in excess of the rates fixed by its franchise, before the Commission had exercised its discretion or taken any steps to terminate the franchise contract by authorizing an increase of rates, the superior court has jurisdiction of any action to recover the excess charges. State ex rel. Home Tel. & Tel. Co. v. 

Superior Court, 110 Wash. 396 (1920). 

2. Excess charges for telephone service, paid without protest, cannot be recovered by a subscriber where they were not paid under a mistake of fact, nor induced by fraud, nor paid under the exigencies of business which would sustain loss if the payments were not made. Cavers v. Home Tel. & Tel. Co., 117 Wash. 299 (1921). 

3. Claims for refund of overcharges, validity of statute requiring submission of, to Public Service Commission. 3 A. L. R. 203. 

4. Power of Public Service Commission as to individual party's charge for telephone service in excess of that fixed by public utility. 73 A.L.R. 1194. 

80.04.230 Overcharges, refund of. When complaint has been made to the commission that a public service company has charged an amount for any service rendered in excess of the lawful rate in force at the time the charge was made, and it has been investigated and the commission has determined that the overcharge allegation is true, the commission may order that the company pay to the complainant the amount of the overcharge so found, whether made before or after the filing of the complaint, with interest from the date of collection of the overcharge. [1937 c 29 § 2; RRS § 10433-1.] 

1. Ordering of payment of interest on amount of refund determined is discretionary with Commission. Op. Atty. Gen. Feb. 24, 1925.

80.04.240 Action in court on reparations and overcharges. If the public service company does not comply with the order of the commission for the payment of the overcharge within the time limited in the order, action may be brought in any superior court where service may be had upon the company to recover the amount of the overcharge with interest. The commission shall certify and file its record in the case, including all exhibits, with the clerk of the court within thirty days after such action is started, and the action shall be heard on the evidence and exhibits introduced before the commission and certified to by it. 

If the complainant prevails in the action, the court shall enter judgment for the amount of the overcharge with interest and shall allow complainant a reasonable attorney's fee, and the cost of preparing and certifying the record for the benefit of and to be paid to the commission by complainant, and deposited by the commission in the public service revolving fund, said sums to be fixed and collected as a part of the costs of the action. 

If the order of the commission is found contrary to law or erroneous by reason of the rejection of testimony properly offered, the court shall remand the cause to the commission with instructions to receive the testimony so offered and rejected and enter a new order based upon the evidence theretofore taken and such as it is directed. to receive.

The court may remand any action which is reversed by it to the commission for further action. Appeals to the supreme court shall lie as in other civil cases. All complaints concerning overcharges shall be filed with the commission within six months, in cases involving the collection of unreasonable rates, and within two years, in cases involving the collection of more than lawful rates, from the time the cause of action accrues, and the action to recover the overcharge shall be filed in the superior court within one year from the date of the order of the commission.

The procedure provided in this section is exclusive, and neither the supreme court nor any superior court shall have jurisdiction save in the manner hereinbefore provided. [1943 c 258 § 2; 1937 c 29 

§ 3; Rem. Supp. 1943 § 10433-2.] 

1. Two-year period of limitation held valid. Hewitt Logging Co. v. 

Northern Pac. Ry. Co., 97 Wash. 597 (1917). 

2. Under this section certiorari lies to review the Commission's order for the repayment by a carrier of an overcharge; and the carrier need not first await the order in an action by the shipper to recover the overcharge. State ex rel. Tacoma Eastern R. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 102 Wash. 589 (1918). 

3. Assuming that the Department is not empowered to direct future refunds, it does not follow that it did not have authority to direct refunds in an order as actually entered. State ex rel. Puget Sound Navigation Co. v. Dept. of Transportation, 33 Wn. (2d) 448 (1949). 

4. It has been held that filing of a claim with the regulatory body is a condition precedent to right to sue in courts. Op. Atty. Gen. Feb. 1, 1922.

80.04.250 Valuation of public service property. The commission may, upon complaint or upon its own motion, ascertain and determine the fair value for rate making purposes of the property of any public service company used and useful for service in this state, and shall exercise such power whenever it deems such valuation or determination necessary or proper under any of the provisions of this title.

The commission may make revaluations of the property of any I public service company from time to time. Before any hearing is had, the commission shall notify the complainants and the public service company concerned, of the time and place of such hearing by giving at least thirty days' written notice thereof, specifying that at the time and place designated a hearing will be held for the purpose of ascertaining the value of the company's property, used and useful, which shall be sufficient to authorize the commission to inquire into and pass upon the matters designated in this section. [1933 

c 165 § 4, last am'ds 1911 c 117 § 92; RRS § 10441.] 

1. "Prudent Investment Theory" sustained.

Rates yielding return of 10% held not confiscatory. Department justified in excluding from rate base warehouses not in use and out of repair. Pac. Coast 

Elev. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 130 Wash. 620 (1924). 

2. Average working capital of public utility was properly taken into account by special master in determining rate basis. Pacific Tel. & Tel. Co. v. 

Whitcomb, 12 F. (2d) 279-affirmed: 276 U. S. 97, 72 L. Ed. 483 (1928). 

3. Public utilities outlays for reasonable future needs as to equipment is a matter of business management which may not be arbitrarily interfered with under guise of rate making. Id. 

4. Method of valuation of public utilities for rate making purposes which considers only original cost of properties on prudent investment theory held erroneous. Id. 

5. "Going value" of public utility is additional value that purchaser will give because it is a going concern with an established business. Id. 

6. Accrued depreciation of property of public utility must be ascertained by inspection and estimate based thereon. Id. 

7. Rate-making body for purpose of fixing rates cannot reduce what it considers excessive reserve fund for depreciation by making inadequate provision for future depreciation. Id. 

8. Valuation order that is merely a step in a rate controversy must be reviewed in Thurston County.

Valuation proceeding that is not connected with rates or service must be reviewed in the county in which the hearing is held. 

Department has authority to correct error in valuation without showing changed conditions.

Department in deducting property erroneously included in the valuation, was not making a revaluation. State ex rel. P. P. & L. Co. v. Dept. of Public 

Works, 143 Wash. 67 (1927). 

9. Failure of Department to allow item for "going concern" does not violate "due process" clause of constitution. Columbia River Tel. Co. v. Dept. of 

Public Works, 148 Wash. 395 (1928). 

10. Company is not entitled to theoretical cost of cutting and replacing pavement in computing reproduction cost. State ex rel. Grays Harbor R. & 

L. Co. v. Denney, 150 Wash. 690 (1929). 

11. One of the main purposes of the public service law is that rates shall at all times be non-discriminatory, non-preferential, and just, reasonable and sufficient to yield a reasonable compensation for the service rendered. Value

to customer or his ability to pay is not the prime factor which alone will warrant the reduction in rates. State ex rel. Pac. P. & L. Co., Puget Sound P. & 

L. Co., Wash. Water Power Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 179 Wash. 461 (1934). 

12. Department may refuse to include in rate base nine unproducing wells. 

In determining the fair value of the utility for rate-making purposes, it was proper for the Department to consider the original cost of construction, the cost of additions and betterments, and the cost of reproduction new. 

State ex rel. Winlock Water Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 180 Wash. 278 

(1934). 

13. "Fair value of the property" which is necessary basis for rate making is not necessarily synonymous with "reconstruction cost depreciated," particularly where reconstruction cost is based upon abnormally low prices. 

State ex rel. O. W. Water Service Co. v. Dept of Public Works, 184 Wash. 

451 (1935). 

14. Rate-making authority proceeding by first establishing original cost of utilities property was not required to accept fair value thereof without deduction therefrom to represent accrued depreciation or present depreciation of property. State ex rel. Pacific Tel. & Tel. Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 19 

Wn. (2d) 200 (1943). 

15. In determining the fair value of a telephone company's property, the 

Department may consider the original cost, whether such cost was prudently incurred, the cost of reproduction of the property at normal times, and shall then determine the present condition of the property in the light of existing depreciation. Id. 

16. In determining the amount of existing depreciation of a telephone company's property, the Department may consider the condition of the property as evidenced by a physical inspection thereof, in so far as practicable, and shall give consideration to obsolescence, inadequacy, changes in art, requirements of public authorities, and other pertinent matters. Id. (Wherein it was held error to determine fair value as amount arrived at by subtracting the company's depreciation reserve from original cost of property.) 

17. In determining fair value of company's property used and useful in public service, the Department improperly excluded certain parcels of real estate and considerable amount of underground conduit, though such property was not in use at the time of the entry of the orders fixing rates. Id. 

18. A licensing contract between a parent company and a subsidiary operating in this state as a public utility, is subject to close scrutiny by the Department in fixing the rates to be charged by the subsidiary, whenever the reasonableness of the terms of the contract is the subject of inquiry. Id. 

19. The Department erred in disallowing as operating expense payments made by the company to a trustee to provide for the cost of pensions to its employees. Id. 

20. Amortization of cost of road. 9 A.L.R. 1232. 

21. Valuations for rate making as affected by advance in price conditions.

20 A.L.R. 555. 

22. Street easements as a factor in fixing a rate base for a street railway company. 49 A.L.R. 1477. 

23. Capitalizing or funding bond discount in valuation for rate-making purposes. 72 A.L.R. 1232. 

24. Under law of 1911 where Light company owned and operated a short street railway, the Commission in prescribing rates was not required to hold hearing and could consider for valuation either the railway alone or the company's entire properties so long as rate established was reasonable. Op. Atty. 

Gen. April 21, 1915. 

25. Department may at any time make an investigation for the purposes of valuation and appraisement of utility properties and may do so for purpose of aiding utility in making its annual report or in securing adequate financing, so long as purpose of aiding the utility is not the primary reason for the valuation. Op. Atty. Gen. Sept. 25, 1941.

80.04.260 Summary proceedings. Whenever the commission shall be of opinion that any public service company is failing or omitting, or about to fail or omit, to do anything required of it by law, or by order, direction, or requirement of the commission, or is doing anything, or about to do anything, or permitting anything, or about to permit anything to be done, contrary to or in violation of law or of any order, direction, or requirement of the commission, it shall direct the attorney general to commence an action or proceeding in the superior court of Thurston county or in the superior court of any county in which the company may do business, in the name of the state on the relation of the commission for the purpose of having such violations or threatened violations stopped and prevented, either by mandamus or injunction.

The attorney general shall thereupon begin such action or proceeding by petition to such superior court, alleging the violation complained of, and praying for appropriate relief by way of mandamus or injunction. The court shall specify a time, not exceeding twenty days after the service of the copy of the petition, within which the public service company complained of must answer the petition.

In case of default in answer, or after answer, the court shall immediately inquire into the facts and circumstances in such manner as it shall direct, without other or formal pleadings, and without respect to any technical requirement. 

Such persons or corporations as the court deems necessary or proper to be joined as parties, in order to make its judgment, order, or writ effective, may be joined as parties. 

The final judgment in any such action or proceeding shall either dismiss the action or proceeding or direct that the writ of mandamus or injunction, or both, issue as prayed for in the petition, or in such other modified form as the court may determine will afford appropriate relief.

An appeal may be taken to the supreme court from such final judgment in the same manner and with the same effect as appeals from judgments of the superior court in actions to review orders of the commission. All provisions of this chapter relating to the time of appeal, the manner of perfecting the same, the filing of briefs, hearings, and supersedeas, shall apply to appeals to the supreme court under the provisions of this section. [1911 c 117 § 93; RRS § 10442.1 

1. Where electric and water company has published notice of abandonment of service, Commission can, on its own motion, hold hearing on the proposed discontinuance, and, if the facts justify it, order giving of adequate and efficient service. Op. Atty. Gen. June 3, 1919. 

2. Department has no authority to authorize affirmatively the abandonment of service by a public service company. Op. Atty. Gen. March 27, 1926; 

March 31, 1926.

80.04.270 Merchandise accounts to be kept separate. Any public service company engaging in the sale of merchandise or appliancest or equipment shall keep separate accounts, as prescribed by the commission, of its capital employed in such business and of its revenues therefrom and operating expenses thereof. The capital employed in such business shall not constitute a part of the fair value of the company's property for rate making purposes, nor shall the revenues or operating expenses of such business constitute a part of the operating revenues and expenses of the company as a public service company. [1933 c 165 § 8; RRS § 10458-2.] 

80.04.280 Purchase and sale of stock by employees. No public service company shall permit an employee to sell, offer for sale, or solicit the purchase of any security of any other person during the hours such employee is engaged to perform any duty of such company, nor shall a public service company by any device require an employee to purchase or contract to purchase any of its securities or those of any other person, or require an employee to permit the deduction from his wages or salary of any sum to be applied in payment of any purchase or contract to purchase any security of such company or of any other person. [1933 c 165 § 9; RRS § 10458-3.] 

80.04.290 Sales of stock to employees and customers. A public service company may sell to its employees and customers any increase of its capital stock, or part thereof, without first offering it to existing stockholders: Provided, That such sale is approved by the holders of a majority of the capital stock, at a regular or special meeting held after notice given as to the time, place, and object thereof as provided by law and the bylaws of the company. Such sales shall be at prices and in amounts for each purchaser and upon terms and conditions as set forth in the resolution passed at the stockholders' meeting, or in a resolution passed at a subsequent meeting of the board of trustees if the resolution passed at the stockholders' meeting shall authorize the board to determine prices, amounts, terms, and conditions, except that in either event a minimum price for the stock must be fixed in the resolution passed at the stockholders' meeting. [1923 c 110 § 1; RRS § 10344-1.] 

1. Constitutionality of statutes as to employee's purchase of stock. 63 

A.L.R. 841. 

80.04.300 Budgets to be filed by companies. The commission may regulate, restrict, and control the budgets of expenditures of public service companies. On or before the first day of November of each year each company shall prepare a budget showing the amount of money which, in its judgment, will be needed during the ensuing year for maintenance, operation, and construction, classified by accounts as prescribed by the commission, and shall file it with the commission for its investigation and approval or rejec-

tion. When a budget has been filed the commission shall examine into and investigate it to determine whether the expenditures therein proposed are fair and reasonable and not contrary to public interest.

Adjustments or additions to budget expenditures may be made from time to time during the year by filing a supplementary budget with the commission for its investigation and approval or rejection. 

[1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

80.04.310 Control over expenditures. The commission may, both as to original and supplementary budgets, prior to the making or contracting for the expenditure of any item therein, and after notice to the company and a hearing thereon, reject any item of the budget. The commission may require any company to furnish further information, data, or detail as to any proposed item of expenditure. 

Failure of the commission to object to any item of expenditure within sixty days of the filing of any original budget or within thirty days of the filing of any supplementary budget shall constitute authority to the company to proceed with the making of or contracting for such expenditure, but such authority may be terminated any time by objection made thereto by the commission prior to the making of or contracting for such expenditure.

Examination, investigation, and determination of the budget by the commission shall not bar or estop it from later determining whether any of the expenditures made thereunder are fair, reasonable, and commensurate with the service, material, supplies, or equipment received. [1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

80.04.320 Budget rules and regulations. The commission may prescribe the necessary rules and regulations to place RCW 80.04.300 and 80.04.310 in operation. It may, by general order, exempt in whole or in part from the operation thereof companies whose gross operating revenues are less than twenty-five thousand dollars a year. The commission may upon request of any company withhold from publication during such time as the commission may deem advisable any portion of any original or supplementary budget relating to proposed capital expenditures. [1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

80.04.330 Effect of unauthorized expenditure-Emergencies. Any public service company may make or contract for any rejected item of expenditure, but in such case the same shall not be allowed as an operating expense, or as to items of construction, as a part of the fair value of the company's property used and useful in serving the public: Provided, That such items of construction may at any time thereafter be so allowed in whole or in part upon proof that they are used and useful. Any company may upon the happening of any emergency caused by fire, flood, explosion, storm, earthquake, riot,

or insurrection, or for the immediate preservation or restoration to condition of usefulness of any of its property, the usefulness of which has been destroyed by accident, make the necessary expenditure therefor free from the operation of these sections. 

Any finding and order entered by the commission shall be in effect until vacated and set aside in proper proceedings for review thereof. [1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

80.04.340 Dividends, control of. No public service company engaged in intrastate business in this state shall pay any dividend upon its common stock until: (1) Its earnings and earned surplus are sufficient to declare and pay the same after provisions are made for reasonable and proper reserves; (2) The dividend proposed to be paid can reasonably be paid without impairing the ability of the company to render reasonable and adequate service at reasonable rates. 

Before any common stock dividend is paid, the company shall make application to the commission for approval and shall furnish to the commission such information and data relating thereto as the commission shall require. 

If the commission finds after notice and an opportunity to be heard that payment of the dividend will not be in violation of the provisions of this section it shall approve the declaration and payment thereof, otherwise it shall disapprove it. No dividend so disapproved shall be paid unless the findings and order of the commission with respect thereto, have been vacated and set aside in proper proceeding for review thereof.

If at any time the commission finds that the capital of any company is impaired, it may, after due notice, investigation, and hearing, issue an order directing the company to cease paying dividends on its common stock until reasonable proof has been made to the commission that such impairment has been made good, and the status of the company has become such that common stock dividends may reasonably and properly be paid in full compliance with this section. [1933 c 165 § 11; RRS § 10458-5.] 

1. This applies to corporations engaged "only in intrastate business in this state," excluding those engaged in both interstate and intrastate business, the word "only" being necessarily implied from other sections of the act. 

State ex rel. Wash. Water Power Co. v. Murray, 181 Wash. 27 (1935). 

80.04.350 Depreciation and retirement accounts. The commission, may after hearing, require any public service company to carry proper and adequate depreciation or retirement accounts in accordance with such rules, regulations, and forms of accounts as the commission may prescribe. The commission may from time to time ascertain and by order fix the proper and adequate rates of depreciation or retirement of the several classes of property of each company. Each company shall conform its depreciation or retire-

ment accounts to the rates so prescribed. In fixing the rate of the annual depreciation or retirement charge, the commission may consider the rate and amount theretofore charged by the company for depreciation or retirement.

The commission shall exercise like authority over all other reserve accounts of such companies. [1937 c 169 § 4; 1933 c 165 § 13; 

RRS § 10458-7.] 

80.04.360 Excessive earnings to reserve fund. If any public service company earns in any year a net utility operating income in excess of a reasonable rate of return upon the fair value of its property used and useful in the public service, such excess shall be placed in a reserve fund. Such reserve fund shall from time to time and under rules and regulations prescribed by the commission, and upon its order, be applied in whole or in part as earnings of the company in any subsequent year or years in which it does not earn a reasonable rate of return, or in establishing, replenishing, or maintaining amortization, depreciation, or other contingent funds, or for any other purpose beneficial to the consumers of the company. 

[1933 c 165 § 14; RRS § 10458-8.] 

80.04.370 Manner of serving papers. All notices, applications, complaints, findings of fact, opinions, and orders required by this title to be served may be served by mail and service shall be deemed complete when a true copy thereof is deposited in the post office properly addressed and stamped. [1933 c 165 § 7; RRS § 10458-1.] 

80.04.380 Penalty for violations by companies. Every public service company and every corporation, whether or not a public service company, which violates or fails to comply with any provision of this title or any order, rule, or requirement of the commission, shall be subject to a penalty of not to exceed one thousand dollars for each offense, and if the offense is a continuing one, each day's continuance thereof shall constitute a separate offense. [(i) 

1911 c 117 § 94; RRS § 10443. (ii) 1911 c 117 § 96; RRS § 10445.] 

80.04.390 Penalty for violations by individuals. Every officer, agent, or employee of a public service company and every person, who, as an individual or as an officer or agent of a corporation, violates or fails to comply with, or who procures, aids, or abets another, or his company, in the violation of, or noncompliance with, any provision of this title or any order, rule, or requirement of the commission shall be guilty of a gross misdemeanor. [(i) 1911 c 117 

§ 95; RRS § 10444. (ii) 1911 c 117 § 97; RRS § 10446.] 

80.04.400 Actions to recover penalties. Actions to recover penalties under this title shall be brought in the name of the state in the superior court of Thurston county, or the superior court of any county in which the public service company does business. In all such actions the procedure and rules of evidence shall be the same

as in ordinary civil actions, except as otherwise herein provided. 

All fines and penalties recovered shall be paid into the state trea sury. [1911 c 117 § 98; RRS § 10447.] 

80.04.410 Orders and rules conclusive. In all actions between private parties and public service companies involving any rule or order of the commission, and in all actions for the recovery of penalties, or for the enforcement of the orders or rules of the department, the orders and rules shall be conclusive unless set aside or annulled on review. [1911 c 117 § 99; RRS § 10448.] 

1. Action to set aside order will not lie, review being only remedy. Venue is presumed correct. Willapa Power Co. v. Public Service Comm., 110 Wash. 193 (1920). 

80.04.420 Intervention by commission where order or rule is involved. In all court actions involving any rule or order of the commission where it has not been made a party, the commission shall be served with a copy of all pleadings, and shall be entitled to intervene. Where the fact that the action involves a rule or order of the commission does not appear until the time of trial, the court shall immediately direct the clerk to notify the commission of the pendency of such action, and shall permit the commission to intervene therein.

The failure to comply with the provisions of this section shall render void and of no effect any judgment in such action, where the effect of such judgment is to modify or nullify any rule or order of the commission. [1943 c 67 § 1; Rem. Supp. 1943 § 10448-1.] 

80.04.430 Findings of commission prima facie correct. Whenever the commission has issued or promulgated any order or rule, in any writ of review brought by a public service company to determine the reasonableness of such order or rule, the findings of fact made by the commission shall be prima facie correct, and the burden shall be upon the public service company to establish the order or rule to be unreasonable or unlawful. [1911 c 117 § 100;

RRS § 10449.] 

1. Findings of Commission on reasonableness of rate should not be disturbed unless made arbitrarily and without a full consideration of facts. 

Puget Sound & Elec. R. R. Co. v. Railroad Comm., 65 Wash. 75 (1911). 

2. Upon review, the Commission will be presumed to have acted reasonably and lawfully, and such presumption must be clearly overthrown before the order will be set aside. State ex rel. Great Northern Ry. Co. v. Public 

Service Comm., 81 Wash. 275 (1914). 

3. Findings of Department are entitled to same presumption of verity accorded to findings of regular judicial tribunal and will be sustained unless against the clear weight of the evidence. 

The fact that complaint is filed by Department does not change the rule. 

Department may make its order retroactive to the date jurisdiction was acquired. Pac. Coast Elev. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 130 Wash. 620 (1924). 

4. Courts in review proceedings consider questions of law and only incidentally pass on questions of fact. Department is the tribunal for determining questions that involve business, engineering, mechanical, social, and economic questions and require expert investigations, careful study and con-

sideration of engineering and mechanical problems. State ex rel. Am. Telechronometer Co. v. Baker, 164 Wash. 483 (1931). 

5. Findings of Department on question of fact will not be disturbed by court unless arbitrarily reached and without full and due consideration of all controlling facts. State ex rel. Winlock Water Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 180 Wash. 278 (1934). Accord: Olympic Peninsula Motor Freight v. Valley Milk Transportation, Inc., 183 Wash. 329 (1935). 

6. Facts found in rate case held not sufficient to justify order made even if prima facie correct. State ex rel. Puget Sound Power & Light Co. v. Dept. 

of Public Works, 181 Wash. 105 (1935). 

7. Even if this section did not direct it, findings of the Department would be taken as prima facie correct on review where made by experts with long familiarity and great personal knowledge of the subject. State ex rel. West 

Side Improvement Club v. Dept. of Public Service, 186 Wash. 378 (1936). 

8. In determining whether a rate fixed by a regulatory body is fair or confiscatory, neither findings nor conclusions are binding upon the courts. 

State ex rel. Pac. Tel. & Tel. Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 19 Wn. (2d) 200 (1943). 

80.04.440 Liability for damages. Any public service company which does or causes or permits to be done or which fails to do any act prohibited or required by law, or order or rule of the commission, shall be liable for all loss, damage or injury resulting therefrom. In case of recovery, if the court finds that the act or omission was wilful, it may allow a reasonable attorney's fee as a part of the costs. [1911 c 117 § 102; RRS § 10451.] 

80.04.450 Certified copies of orders, rules, etc.-Evidentiary effect. Upon the application of any persons, the commission shall furnish certified copies of any classification, rate, rule, regulation, or order established by it, and the printed copies published by its authority or any certified copy of any such classification, rate, rule, regulation, or order, with seal affixed, shall be admissible in evidence in any action or proceeding, and shall be sufficient to establish the fact that the charge, rate, rule, order, or classification therein contained is the official act of the commission. 

When copies of any classification, rate, rule, regulation, or order not contained in the printed reports, or copies of papers, accounts, or records of public service companies filed with the commission are demanded for proper use, the department shall charge a reasonable compensation therefor. [1911 c 117 § 103; RRS § 10452.] 

1. Employees of Commission may not keep money received for certified copies of documents made by said employee after working hours. Dept. Memo. 

Aug. 14, 1924. 

2. Department has no power or duty to furnish a private individual a copy of a labor agreement between a public service company and a union. Op. 

Atty. Gen. Sept. 10, 1941. 

80.04.460 Investigation of accidents. Every public service company shall give immediate notice to the commission of every accident resulting in death or injury to any person occurring in its plant or system, in such manner as the commission may prescribe. 

The commission may investigate any accident resulting in death or injury to any person occurring in connection with the plant or

system of any public service company. Notice of the investigation shall be given in all cases for a sufficient length of time to enable the company affected to participate in the hearing and may be given orally or in writing, in such manner as the commission may prescribe.

Such witnesses may be examined as the commission deems necessary and proper to thoroughly ascertain the cause of the accident and fix the responsibility therefor. The examination and investigation may be conducted by an inspector or deputy inspector, and they may administer oaths, issue subpoenas, and compel the attendance of witnesses, and when the examination is conducted by an inspector or deputy inspector, he shall make a full and complete report thereof to the commission. [1911 c 117 § 63, part; RRS 

§ 10399, part.] 

Inspection of utility plants for defective machinery, etc.: RCW 81.44- .080. 80.04.470

Commission to enforce public service laws. The commission shall enforce the provisions of this title and all other acts affecting public service companies, the enforcement of which is not specifically vested in some other officer or tribunal. [1911 c 117 § 101; 

RRS § 10450.] 

1. Regulation of municipally owned water system denied to Commission.

State ex rel. Country Club of Seattle v. Dept. of Public Service, 198 Wash. 

37 (1939). 

2. Statute making it incumbent on school busses and explosives trucks to stop at railroad crossings and providing that failure so to do is a misdemeanor is not for the Department to enforce with regard to intracity bus operations. 

Op. Atty. Gen. Jan. 14, 1946. 

80.04.480 Rights of action not released. This title shall not release or waive any right of action by the state or any person for any right, penalty, or forfeiture under any law. [1911 c 117 § 104, part; 

RRS § 10453, part.] 

80.04.490 Penalties cumulative. All penalties arising under this title shall be cumulative and an action to recover one penalty is no bar to the recovery of any other penalty. [1911 c 117 § 104, part; 

RRS § 10453, part.] 

80.04.500 Application to municipal utilities. The commission shall make no order affecting the rates, tolls, rentals, charges, contracts, or services, or the safety or adequacy of the facilities of, or the reasonableness of the rules affecting, any telephone, gas, or electrical plant, or water system owned and operated by a city. All other provisions herein shall apply to public utilities so owned and operated. [1911 c 117 § 105; RRS § 10454.] 

1. Applicability of public utility acts to municipal corporations owning or operating a public utility. 18 A.L.R. 946. 

2. Power of state or Public Service Commission to regulate rates of municipally owned or operated public utility. 76 A.L.R. 851.

3. City of Tacoma may conduct its light and water business without interference by the Department. McCormacks, Inc. v. Tacoma, 170 Wash. 103 (1932). 

4. This section specifically excludes municipally-owned water systems from jurisdiction of the Department. State ex rel. West Side Improvement Club v. Dept. of Public Service, 186 Wash. 378 (1936). 

5. Laws of 1933 repealed by implication law putting that portion of a municipal water system lying outside its corporate limits under jurisdiction of the Department. New law allows cities to fix rates of such portions and to operate them without interference. Id.

SECURITIES
