80.08.010 Definition. The term "public service company," as used in this chapter, shall mean every company now or hereafter engaged in business in this state as a public utility and subject to regulation as to rates and service by the public service commission. 

[1933 c 151 § 1, part; RRS § 10439-1, part.] 

Severability: "If any section or provision of this act shall be adjudged to be invalid or unconstitutional, such adjudication shall not affect the validity of the act as a whole or any section, provision or part thereof not adjudged to be invalid or unconstitutional." [1933 c 

151 15.] This applies to RCW 80- .08.010-80.08.140. 

80.08.020 Control vested in state. The power of public service companies to issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, and bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness and to create liens on their property situated within this state is a special privilege, the right of supervision, regulation, restriction, and control of which is vested in the state, and such power shall be exercised as provided by law and under such rules

and regulations as the commission may prescribe. [1933 c 151 §2;

RRS § 10439-2.] 

1. Regulating issuance of securities by public utilities through Public 

Service Commissions. 41 A.L.R. 889; 39 A.L.R. 1517; 5 A.L.R. 36. 

2. State has control of issuance of stock certificates to stockholders of a public service company who subscribed before this act became effective. Op. 

Atty. Gen. Dec. 13, 1938. 

80.08.030 Authority to issue. A public service company may issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness payable on demand or at periods of more than twelve months after the date thereof, for the following purposes only: The acquisition of property, or the construction, completion, extension, or improvement of its facilities, or the improvement or maintenance of its service, or the discharge or refunding of its obligations, or the reimbursement of moneys actually expended from income or from any other moneys in the treasury of the company not secured by or obtained from the issue of stock or stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness of the company for any of the aforesaid purposes except maintenance of service, in cases where the applicant keeps its accounts and vouchers for such expenditures in such manner as to enable the commission to ascertain the amount of money so expended and the purpose for which the expenditure was made. 

[1937 c 30 § 1; 1933 c 151 § 3; RRS § 10439-3.] 

1. Issuance of stock dividend by public service company should be treated as any other issuance of securities and application must be made to Department containing full information on proposal. If Department is not satisfied with application, it should hold a hearing on the matter. Op. Atty. Gen. Oct. 

18, 1941. This opinion subsequently modified. Op. Atty. Gen. May 20, 1952. 

2. Electric company may not issue securities for purpose of obtaining money to purchase wiring or plumbing fixtures and/or installation of same for customers since such purposes are not enumerated in this section. Op. 

Atty. Gen. Jan. 26, 1928. 

3. Conditional sales contract for purchase of equipment entered into by common carrier with bank is not "Other evidence of indebtedness," and carrier is not required to apply to the Commission for permission to make such a contract. Op. Atty. Gen. May 10, 1950. 

80.08.040 Application for authority-Hearing-Joint action. Application for authority to issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness shall be made to the commission, stating the amount, character, terms, and purpose of each proposed issue, and stating such other pertinent details as the commission may require. 

To enable it to determine whether it will grant the application, the commission may hold a hearing and may make such additional investigation, and examine such witnesses, books, papers, documents, and contracts, and require the filing of such data as it may deem of assistance. The commission may by its order grant permission for the issuance of the stock or stock certificates or other

evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, in the amount applied for, or in a lesser amount, or not at all, and may attach to the exercise of its permission such conditions as it may deem reasonable and necessary. 

If an agency is empowered by another state to regulate and control the amount and character of securities to be issued by any public service company within such other state, the commission may agree with such agency on the issuance of stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, by a public service company owning or operating a public utility both in such state and in this state, and may approve such issue jointly with such other agency and issue a joint order of approval: Provided, That no joint approval shall be required in order to express the approval of such issue by this state if the issue is separately approved by the commission. 

The company making the application may have the order of the commission reviewed in the courts in the same manner and by the same procedure as any other order of the commission. [1933 c 151 

§ 4; RRS § 10439-4.] 

80.08.050 Use of proceeds limited. No public service company shall, without the consent of the commission, apply the issue of any stock or stock certificate or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bond, note or other evidence of indebtedness, or any part thereof, or any proceeds thereof, to any purpose not specified in the commission's order, or to any purpose specified in the commission's order in excess of the amount authorized for such purpose, or issue or dispose of the same on any terms less favorable than those specified in the order, or a modification thereof. [1933 c 151 § 5; RRS § 10439-5.] 

80.08.060 Short term notes excepted. A public service company may issue notes, except demand notes, for proper purposes and not in violation of any provision of this chapter, or any other law, payable at periods of not more than twelve months after the date of issuance, without the consent of the commission, but no such note shall, in whole or in part, be refunded by any issue of stock or stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, without the consent of the commission. [1937 c 30 § 2, part; 1933 c 151 § 6; RRS § 10439-6, part.] 

80.08.070 Fee schedule. Each public service company making application to the commission for authority to issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, shall pay to the commission the following fees: For each order authorizing an issue of bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, one dollar for each one thousand dollars of the principal amount of the authorized issue or fraction thereof up to one million dollars, and fifty cents for

each one thousand dollars over one million dollars and up to ten million dollars, and ten cents for each one thousand dollars over ten million dollars, with a minimum fee in any case of ten dollars; for each order authorizing an issue of stock, stock certificates, or other evidence of interest or ownership, one dollar for each one thousand dollars of the par or stated value of the authorized issue or fraction thereof up to one million dollars, and fifty cents for each one thousand dollars over one million dollars and up to ten million dollars, and ten cents for each one thousand dollars over ten million dollars, with a minimum fee in any case of ten dollars: Provided, That only twenty-five percent of the specified fees need be paid on any issue or on such portion thereof as may be used to guarantee, take over, refund, or discharge any stock issue or stock certificates, bonds, notes, or other evidence of interest, ownership, or indebtedness on which a fee has theretofore been paid: Provided further, That if the property of the public utility subject to the provisions of this title, proposing to issue such securities shall be located in part in the state of Washington and in part in some other state or states, the fees payable to the public service commission of Washington under this section shall be computed only on such amount of such securities as shall bear the same proportion to the total amount so authorized, as the book value of such property located within the state of Washington shall bear to the total book value of the property of such public utility proposing to issue such securities; for the purpose of computing such fees the book value of the property shall be determined as of the close of business of the last quarter preceding the application: And provided further, That if the commission modifies the amount of the issues requested and the applicant elects not to avail itself of the authorization, no fee need be paid. All fees collected under this section shall be paid at least one each month to the state treasurer and deposited in the public service revolving fund. [1951 c 227 § 1, last am'ds 1933 c 151 § 6; formerly RRS § 10439-6, part.] 

1. Loans from agency of Federal Government (REA) are covered by this act if public service company gives notes for the indebtedness. Op. Atty. Gen. 

July 1, 1937. 

2. Public service company which paid fee prescribed on application for authority to refund securities and was then unable to do so, cannot be credited on subsequent application with fees paid on former application—yet company need pay only 25% of fee in second application on all securities for which authority was given in prior application and fee paid. Op. Atty. Gen. 

Jan. 4, 1940. 

3. Where a public service company received authorization for issuance of bonds and then secured a supplemental order authorizing change in interest, and finally an order canceling the original order and authorizing issuance of notes to banks, the Department should not charge the statutory fee again for the supplemental order. Department also need not refund any of the fee because of the 25% proviso as to notes issued to banks. Op. Atty. Gen. 

April 5, 1943. 

4. Where a public service company, with the approval of the Department, issues a note to an insurance company in return for a loan, and the insurance

company's business is such that it may be considered a "loaning institution," the proviso of this section allowing payment of only 25% of the fee specified in the schedule applies. Op. Atty. Gen. Aug. 29, 1949. 

5. Loan to a public service company by a mutual life insurance company comes within the first proviso, and public service company need pay only 25% of the fee prescribed. Op. Atty. Gen. Aug. 29, 1949. 

80.08.080 Capitalization of franchises or merger contracts prohibited. The commission shall not authorize the capitalization of the right to be a corporation, or the capitalization of any franchise or permit whatsoever, or the right to own, operate, or enjoy any such franchise or permit, in excess of the amount, exclusive of any tax or annual charge, actually paid to the state or to a political subdivision thereof as the consideration for the grant of the franchise, permit, or right; nor shall any contract for consolidation or lease be capitalized, nor shall any public service company hereafter issue any bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness against or as a lien upon any contract for consolidation or merger. [1933 c 151 §7; 

RRS § 10439-7.] 

1. This section does not prohibit mortgaging of bus certificate of convenience and necessity for value in excess of $25 (amount of fee paid for certificate application) even though mortgage includes no other property. Op. 

Atty. Gen. July 7, 1942. 

80.08.090 Accounting for disposition of proceeds. The commission may require public service companies to account for the disposition of the proceeds of all sales of evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, in such form and detail as it deems advisable, and establish such rules and regulations as it deems reasonable and necessary to insure the disposition of the proceeds for the purposes specified in its order. [1933 c 151 § 8; RRS § 10439-8.] 

80.08.100 Unauthorized and nonconforming issues void. Every evidence of interest or ownership, and every evidence of indebtedness, of a public service company, issued without an authorizing order of the commission shall be void, and every evidence of interest or ownership and every evidence of indebtedness, of a company, issued with the authorization of the commission, but not conforming in substance in its provisions to the requirements of the order of authorization, shall be void; but no failure in any other respect to comply with the terms of the order and no defect in, or in connection with, the application for or issuance of such order shall render void any such evidence of interest or ownership, or any such evidence of indebtedness, except as to a person taking it otherwise than in good faith and for value and without actual notice. [1933 c 151 § 9; 

RRS § 10439-9.] 

80.08.110 Penalty against companies. Every public service company which issues, or causes to be issued, any evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, in nonconformity with

the order of the commission authorizing it, or contrary to the provisions of this chapter, or which applies any part of the proceeds from the sale thereof to any purpose other than the purposes specified in the order, or to any purpose specified in the order in excess of the amount authorized for such purpose, shall be subject to a penalty of not more than one thousand dollars for each offense. 

Every violation of any order, rule, or requirement of the commission, or of any provision of this chapter, shall be a separate offense and in a case of continuing violation every day's continuance thereof shall be a separate offense.

The act, omission, or failure of any officer, agent, or employee of any company acting within the scope of his official duties or employment, shall be the act, omission or failure of the company. 

[1933 c 151 § 11; RRS § 10439-11.] 

80.08.120 Penalty against individuals. Every officer, agent, or employee of a public service company, and every other person, who knowingly authorizes, directs, aids in, issues, or executes, or causes to be issued or executed, any evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, in nonconformity with the order of the commission authorizing it, or contrary to the provisions of this chapter, or who, in any proceedings before the commission, knowingly makes any false statement or representation or with knowledge of its falsity files or causes to be filed with the commission any false statement or representation, which may tend to influence the commission to make an order authorizing the issuance of any such evidence of interest or ownership, or such evidence of indebtedness, or which results in procuring from the commission any such order, or who, with knowledge that any false statement or representation was made to the commission in any proceedings tending in any way to influence the commission to make such order, issues or executes or negotiates, or causes to be issued, executed, or negotiated any such evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, or who, directly or indirectly, knowingly applies, or causes or assists to be applied, the proceeds or any part thereof, from the sale of any such evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, to any purpose not specified in the commission's order, or to any purpose specified in the commission's order in excess of the amount authorized for such purpose, or who, with knowledge that any such evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, has been issued or executed in violation of any of the provisions of this chapter, negotiates, or causes it to be negotiated, shall be guilty of a gross misdemeanor. [1933 c 151 § 12; RRS § 10439-12.] 

80.08.130 Permit to assume liability as guarantor, etc. No public service company shall henceforth assume any obligation or liability as guarantor, indorser, surety, or otherwise, in respect to the securities of any other person when such securities are payable at periods

of more than twelve months after the date thereof, without having first secured from the commission an order authorizing it so to do. 

Every such assumption made other than in accordance with the order of the commission is void. [1933 c 151 § 13; RRS § 10439-13.] 

80.08.140 State not obligated. No provision of this chapter, and no deed or act done or performed under or in connection therewith, shall obligate the state to pay or guarantee any stock or stock certificate or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bond, note, or other evidence of indebtedness, authorized, issued, or executed under the provisions of this chapter. [1933 c 151 § 14; RRS § 10439-14.] 

Chapter 80.12

TRANSFERS OF PROPERTY Sections 

80.12.010 Definition. 

80.12.020 Order required to sell, merge, etc. 

80.12.030 Disposal without authorization void. 

80.12.040 Authority required to acquire property or securities of utility. 

80.12.050 Rules and regulations. 

80.12.060 Penalty. 

80.12.010 Definition. The term "public service company," as used in this chapter, shall mean every company now or hereafter engaged in business in this state as a public utility and subject to regulation as to rates and service by the public service commission. 

[1941 c 159 § 1, part; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440a.] 

1. Public service company purchasing all capital stock of a non-public service company need not apply for permission of Department. Op. Atty. Gen. 

Nov. 6, 1942. 

2. Public service company, all of whose properties are being acquired by a public utility district in condemnation proceedings, need not apply to Commission for order authorizing such acquisition. Op. Atty. Gen. Dec. 21, 1942. 

3. Stockholder of public service company wishing to transfer his stock to another person is not required to obtain authorization of the Commission to make the transfer. Op. Atty. Gen. Nov. 25, 1949. 

80.12.020 Order required to sell, merge, etc. No public service company shall sell, lease, assign, or otherwise dispose of the whole or any part of its franchises, properties, or facilities, which are necessary or useful in the performance of its duties to the public, and no public service company shall, by any means whatsoever, directly or indirectly, merge or consolidate any of its franchises, properties, or facilities with any other public service company, without having secured from the commission an order authorizing it so to do: Provided, That this section shall not apply to any sale,

lease, assignment, or other disposal of such franchises, properties, or facilities to a public utility district. [1945 c 75 § 1; 1941 c 159 § 2; 

Rem. Supp. 1945 § 10440b.] 

80.12.030 Disposal without authorization void. Any such sale, lease, assignment, or other disposal, merger, or consolidation made without authority of the commission is void. [1941 c 159 § 3; Rem. 

Supp. 1941 § 10440c.] 

80.12.040 Authority required to acquire property or securities of utility. No public service company shall, directly or indirectly, purchase, acquire, or become the owner of any of the franchises, properties, facilities, capital stocks or bonds of any other public service company unless authorized so to do by the commission. 

Nothing contained in this chapter shall prevent the holding of stocks or other securities heretofore lawfully acquired or prohibit, upon the surrender or exchange of said stocks or other securities pursuant to a reorganization plan, the purchase, acquisition, taking, or holding by the owner of a proportionate amount of the stocks or other securities of any new corporation organized to take over at foreclosure or other sale, the property of the corporation the stocks or securities of which have been thus surrendered or exchanged. Any contract by any public service company for the purchase, acquisition, assignment, or transfer to it of any of the stocks or other securities of any other public service company, directly or indirectly, without the approval of the commission shall be void and of no effect. [1941 c 159 § 4; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440d.] 

80.12.050 Rules and regulations. The commission may promulgate rules and regulations to make effective the provisions of this chapter. [1941 c 159 § 5; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440e.] 

80.12.060 Penalty. A violation of, or failure to comply with, any provision of this chapter, or any order or rule issued by the commission hereunder, by any public service company or its officers, agents, or employees is a gross misdemeanor and each day's continuance of such violation or failure to comply is a separate offense. 

[1941 c 159 § 6; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440f.]

Sections Chapter 80.16 AFFILIATED INTERESTS 

80.16.010 Definitions. 

80.16.020 Dealings with affiliated interests must be approved. 

80.16.030 Payments to affiliated interest disallowed if not reasonable. 

80.16.040 Satisfactory proof, what constitutes. 

80.16.050 Commission's control is continuing. 

80.16.060 Summary order on nonapproved payments. 

80.16.070 Summary order on payments after disallowance. 

80.16.080 Court action to enforce orders. 80.16.090 

Review of orders. 

80.16.010 Definitions. As used in this chapter, the term "public service company" shall include every corporation engaged in business as a public utility and subject to regulation as to rates and service by the public service commission. 

As used in this chapter, the term "affiliated interest" means: 

Every corporation and person owning or holding directly or indirectly five percent or more of the voting securities of any public service company engaged in any intrastate business in this state; 

Every corporation and person, other than those above specified, in any chain of successive ownership of five percent or more of voting securities, the chain beginning with the holder of the voting securities of such public service company; 

Every corporation five percent or more of whose voting securities are owned by any person or corporation owning five percent or more of the voting securities of such public service company or by any person or corporation in any such chain of successive ownership of five percent or more of voting securities;

Every corporation or person with which the public service company has a management or service contract; and 

Every person who is an officer or director of such public service company or of any corporation in any chain of successive ownership of five percent or more of voting securities. [1933 c 152 § 1, part;

RRS § 10440-1, part.] 

Severability: "If any part of this act is held to be unconstitutional or void, such decision shall not affect the validity of the remaining parts of this act." [1933 c 152 § 10.] 

80.16.020 Dealings with affiliated interests must be approved.

No contract or arrangement providing for the furnishing of management, supervisory, construction, engineering, accounting, legal, financial, or similar services, and no contract or arrangement for the purchase, sale, lease, or exchange of any property, right, or

thing, or for the furnishing of any service, property, right, or thing, other than those above enumerated, made or entered into between a public service company and any affiliated interest, including open account advances from or to such affiliated interest, shall be valid until the contract or arrangement has received the approval of the commission. Every public service company shall file with the commission, a verified copy or a verified summary of any such unwritten contract or arrangement. The commission shall approve such contract or arrangement only if it clearly appears upon investigation that it is reasonable and consistent with the public interest. The commission shall not be required to approve any such contract or arrangement unless satisfactory proof is submitted to it of the cost to the affiliated interest of rendering the services or of furnishing the property or service described herein. [1941 c 160 § 1, part; 1933 

c 152 §2; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440-2, part.] 

80.16.030 Payments to affiliated interest disallowed if not reasonable. In any proceeding involving the rates or practices of any public service company, the commission may exclude from the accounts of the company any payment to an affiliated interest for any services rendered or property or service furnished, as above described, under existing contracts or arrangements with the affiliated interest, unless the company establishes the reasonableness of such payment. The commission shall disallow such payment, in whole or in part, in the absence of satisfactory proof that it is reasonable in amount, or unless satisfactory proof is submitted of the cost to the affiliated interest of rendering the service or furnishing the property or service. [1933 c 152 § 3; RRS § 10440-3.] 

1. In fixing a telephone company's rates, the Department acted arbitrarily in disallowing all payments by the company under a license contract with an affiliated company, and as a matter of law, committed error. While the Department was not required to approve the company's agreement to pay a certain percentage to its parent company in license fees, it should have allowed such portion thereof as appears to be reasonable payment for the services rendered. State ex rel. Pacific Tel. & Tel. Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 19 

Wn. (2d) 200 (1943). 

80.16.040 Satisfactory proof, what constitutes. No proof shall be satisfactory, within the meaning of RCW 80.16.020 and 80.16.030, unless it includes the original or verified copies of the relevant cost records and other relevant accounts of the affiliated interest, or such abstract thereof as the commission may deem adequate, properly identified and authenticated: Provided, That the commission may, where reasonable, approve or disapprove such contracts or arrangements without the submission of cost records or accounts. 

[1933 c 152 § 4; RRS § 10440-4.] 

80.16.050 Commission's control is continuing. The commission shall have continuing supervisory control over the terms and conditions of such contracts and arrangements in so far as necessary to

protect and promote the public interest. It shall have the same jurisdiction over the modification or amendment of such contracts or arrangements as it has over original contracts or arrangements. 

The fact that the commission has approved entry into a contract or arrangement shall not preclude disallowance of payments made pursuant thereto, if upon actual experience under such contract or arrangement, it appears that the payments provided for or made are unreasonable. Every order of the commission approving any such contract or arrangement shall be conditioned upon the reserved power of the commission to revise and amend the terms thereof, if and when necessary to protect and promote the public interest. 

[1933 c 152 § 5; RRS § 10440-5.] 

80.16.060 Summary order on nonapproved payments. When the commission finds that any public service company is giving effect to any such contract or arrangement without its having received the commission's approval, the commission may issue a summary order prohibiting the company from treating any payments made under the terms of such contract or arrangement as operating expenses or as capital expenditures for rate or valuation purposes, until the payments have received the approval of the commission. 

[1933 c 152 § 6; RRS § 10440-6.] 

80.16.070 Summary order on payments after disallowance. When the commission finds that any public service company is making payments to an affiliated interest notwithstanding such payments have been disallowed by the commission in a proceeding involving the company's rates or practices, the commission shall issue a summary order prohibiting the company from treating the payments as operating expenses or capital expenditures for rate or valuation. purposes, until they have received the approval of the commission. 

[1933 c 152 §7; RRS § 10440-7.] 

80.16.080 Court action to enforce orders. The superior court of 

Thurston county may enforce such orders to cease and desist by appropriate process, including the issuance of a preliminary injunction, upon the suit of the commission. [1933 c 152 § 8; RRS § 10440-8.] 

80.16.090 Review of orders. Any public service company or affiliated interest deeming any decision or order of the commission to be in any respect improper, unjust, or unreasonable may have the same reviewed in court in the same manner and by the same procedure as provided for review of any other order of the commission. 

[1933 c 152 § 9; RRS § 10440-9.]

Chapter 80.20 INVESTIGATION OF PUBLIC SERVICE COMPANIES Sections 

80.20.010

Definition. 

80.20.020 Cost of investigation may be assessed against company. 

80.20.030 Interest on unpaid assessment-Action to collect. 

80.20.040 Commission's determination of necessity as evidence. 

80.20.050 Order of commission not subject to review. 

80.20.060 Limitation on frequency of investigation. 

80.20.010 Definition. As used in this chapter, the term "public service company" means any person, firm, association, or corporation, whether public or private, operating a utility or public service enterprise subject in any respect to regulation by the commission. 

[1939 c 203 § 1; RRS § 10458-6.] 

80.20.020 Cost of investigation may be assessed against company.

Whenever the commission in any proceeding deems it necessary in order to carry out the duties imposed upon it by law to investigate the books, accounts, practices, and activities, or make any valuation or appraisal of the property, of any public service company, or to investigate or appraise any phase of its operations, or to render any engineering or accounting service to or in connection with any such company, and the cost thereof to the commission exceeds in amount the ordinary regulatory fees paid by such public service company during the preceding calendar year or estimated to be paid during the current year, whichever is more, such company shall pay the expenses reasonably attributable and allocable to such investigation, valuation, appraisal, or services. The commission shall ascertain such expenses, and, after giving notice and an opportunity to be heard, shall render a bill therefor by registered mail to the company, either at the conclusion of the investigation, valuation, appraisal, or services, or from time to time during its progress. Within thirty days after a bill has been mailed, the company shall pay to the commission the amount of the bill, and the commission shall transmit the payment to the state treasurer who shall credit it to the public service revolving fund. The total amount which any public service company shall be required to pay in any calendar year shall not exceed one percent of the gross operating revenues derived by such company from its intrastate operations during the last preceding year. If the company did not operate during all of the preceding year the calculations shall be based upon estimated gross revenue for the current year. [1939 c 203 § 2(a); 

RRS § 10458-6a(a).]

1. Equal protection clauses of both federal and state constitutions are violated by section 12, Chapter 165, Laws of 1933 which provides for investigation of public service companies by the Department with power to charge the cost of investigation in whole or in part to the companies without any fixed standards as to limits on amounts chargeable or frequency of investigation, thus permitting and inviting discrimination and inequality. State v. 

Northwestern Electric Co., 183 Wash. 184 (1935). 

2. This section relates only to expenses of investigation by the Department and not to costs of defending the Department in court proceedings resulting from a Departmental order; hence, legal fees and other expenses of the Attorney General cannot be recovered from the public service company. 

State v. Pacific Tel. & Tel. Co., 27 Wn. (2d) 893 (1947). 

3. Department has authority to conduct investigation to see if donations by public service company have been improperly recorded as operating expenses, although otherwise what such company does with its profit is immaterial to the Department. Op. Atty. Gen. Sept. 20, 1940. 

4. Whenever there is a complete and extraordinary investigation of a public service company as contemplated by this chapter, and the cost of that investigation exceeds the amount of fees paid by the company during the preceding year under Chapter 80.24 RCW, or estimated to be paid during the current year, the entire cost of such investigation should be charged directly against the company and the amount of annual fees paid by that company cannot be deducted from the charges accruing under this section. Op. Atty. 

Gen. May 6, 1941. 

5. An investigation by the Department of the books and plant of a public service company, unconnected with “any proceeding" and unaccompanied by any formal notice to the company, cannot be recovered from the utility under this section. Op. Atty. Gen. April 28, 1942. 

6. Cost of hiring special attorney to assist Department in litigation is properly payable out of Public Service Revolving Fund. Op. Atty. Gen. July 

7, 1944. 

80.20.030 Interest on unpaid assessment Action to collect.

Amounts so assessed against any public service company not paid within thirty days after mailing of the bill therefor, shall draw interest at the rate of six percent per year from the date of mailing the bill. Upon failure of the company to pay the bill, the attorney general shall proceed against the company by civil action in the name of the state in the superior court of Thurston county to collect the amount due, together with interest and costs of suit. [1939 c 203 

§ 2(b); RRS § 10458-6a (b).] 

80.20.040 Commission's determination of necessity as evidence. 

In such action the commission's determination of the necessity of the investigation, valuation, appraisal, or services shall be conclusive evidence of such necessity, and its findings and determination of facts expressed in bills rendered pursuant to this chapter or in any proceedings determinative of such bills shall be prima facie evidence of such facts. [1939 c 203 § 2(c); RRS § 10458-6a (c).] 

80.20.050 Order of commission not subject to review. In view of the civil action provided for in this chapter any order made by the commission in determining the amount of such bill shall not be reviewable in court, but the mere absence of such right of review shall not prejudice the rights of defendants in the civil action. [1939 

c 203 § 2(d); RRS § 10458-6a (d).]

80.20.060 Limitation on frequency of investigation. Expenses of a complete valuation, rate, and service investigation shall not be assessed against a public service company if it has been subjected to and has paid the expenses of a complete valuation, rate, and service investigation during the preceding five years, unless the properties or operations of the company have materially changed or there has been a substantial change in its value for rate making purposes or in other circumstances and conditions affecting rates and services. [1939 c 203 § 2(e); RRS § 10458-6a (e).] 

Chapter 80.24

REGULATORY FEES Sections 

80.24.010 Companies to file reports of gross revenue and pay fees. 

80.24.020 Fees to approximate reasonable cost of regulation. 

80.24.030 Regulatory cost records to be kept by commission. 

80.24.040 Disposition of fees-Public service revolving fund created. 

80.24.050 Penalty for failure to pay fees.

Annual license fee of public service companies: RCW 23.28.050. 

Assessment of public utilities for property tax purposes: Chapter 84.12.

Easements of public service companies taxable as personalty: RCW 84.20.010.

Public utility tax: Chapter 82.16. 

80.24.010 Companies to file reports of gross revenue and pay fees. Every public service company subject to regulation by the commission shall, on or before the first day of April of each year, file with the commission a statement on oath showing its gross operating revenue from intrastate operations for the preceding calendar year or portion thereof and pay to the commission a fee equal to one-tenth of one percent of the first fifty thousand dollars of gross operating revenue, plus two-tenths of one percent of any gross operating revenue in excess of fifty thousand dollars: Provided, That the fee shall in no case be less than one dollar. 

The percentage rates of gross operating revenue to be paid in any year may be decreased by the commission for any class of companies subject to the payment of such fees, by general order entered before March 1st of such year, and for such purpose such companies shall be classified as follows:

Electrical, gas, water, telephone, telegraph, and irrigation companies shall constitute class one. Every other company subject to regulation by the commission, for which regulatory fees are not otherwise fixed by law shall pay fees as herein provided and shall

constitute additional classes according to kinds of businesses en gaged in. [1939 c 123 § 1, part; 1937 c 158 § 1; RRS § 10417, part.] 

or 

Severability: "If any section part of a section, clause or sentence of this act should for any reason be declared unconstitutional and invalid such adjudication shall not affect the validity of any of the remaining portions of the act or if the application of the act to any person or circumstance be held unconstitutional or invalid such adjudication shall not affect the application of the act to any other persons or circumstances not directly involved in the action wherein such adjudication was made." [1937 c 158 § 8.] 

1. Fees based on gross operating revenues not an unconstitutional burden on interstate commerce provided amount of fees reasonably agrees with cost of supervision, but where charges against fund were not properly segregated, burden of proving reasonableness shifts to State. Great Northern Ry. Co. v. 

State, 184 Wash. 648. Reversed on facts in 300 U. S. 154, 57 Sup. Ct. 397, 81 

L. Ed. 573 (1935). 

2. Overpayment of filing fee cannot be credited upon next year's payment. 

Op. Atty. Gen. April 1, 1933. 

3. For purpose of computing gross revenue fees, telephone company may deduct revenues derived from business sent over lines partly within and partly without the state. Sums derived from messages sent in intrastate business by the Federal Government must be included for gross revenue purposes. Sums derived from activities not connected with actual message transmission need not be included. Op. Atty. Gen. July 26, 1939. 

4. Where one group of utilities out of a class established by law for purposes of regulation does not pay fees sufficient to cover cost of regulation, the Department must continue its statutory duties of regulation, and may in its discretion either await reduction of the deficit by future years' fees or apply to the next legislature for an increase in the fees. Op. Atty. Gen. April 15, 1940. 

5. Operator of small telephone company leasing it from owner must pay gross revenue fees. Op. Atty. Gen. Aug. 18, 1941. 

6. This section was not intended to operate retroactively. The fee paid by a company based on its gross revenue the preceding year is for the Department's expenses during the current year. So where a company went out of business in May, it was not required to pay in the next year any fees based on revenues up to May. The Department is not required to refund any portion of the last fee paid. Op. Atty. Gen. June 28, 1945. 

80.24.020 Fees to approximate reasonable cost of regulation. In fixing the percentage rates of gross operating revenue to be paid in any year, the commission shall consider all moneys then in the public service revolving fund and the fees currently to be paid into such fund, to the end that the fees collected from the several classes of companies shall be approximately the same as the reasonable cost of supervising and regulating such classes of companies. [1939 c 123 

§ 1, part; 1937 c 158 § 1; RRS § 10417, part.] 

80.24.030 Regulatory cost records to be kept by commission. 

The commission shall keep accurate records of the costs incurred in regulating and supervising the several groups of companies subject to regulation and supervision and such records shall be open to inspection by all interested parties. The records and data upon which the commission's determination is made shall be considered prima facie correct in any proceeding instituted to challenge the reasonableness or correctness of any order of the commission fixing

fees and distributing regulatory expenses. [1937 c 158 §7;

RRS § 10417-5.] 

1. Where one kind of utility out of a class established by law for purposes of regulation does not pay fees sufficient to cover cost of regulation, the Department must continue its statutory duties of regulation and may, in its discretion, either await reduction of the particular deficit by future years' fees or apply to next legislature for increase in amount of fees. Op. Atty. Gen. 

April 15, 1940. 

2. If the allocation of general departmental administrative overhead costs on the basis of gross operating revenues derived from intrastate operation produces a result which, in the language of the statute, is reasonable and does, in fact, approximate the cost of regulating any given utility or class of utilities, such basis would be proper and legal. Op. Atty. Gen. June 29, 1943. 

80.24.040 Disposition of fees-Public service revolving fund created. All moneys collected under the provisions of this chapter shall within thirty days after their receipt be paid to the state treasurer, who shall deposit them in a special fund in the state treasury, hereby created, to be designated the public service revolv ing fund. [(i) 1937 c 158 § 6; RRS § 10417-4. (ii) 1945 c 267 § 10, part; Rem. Supp. 1945 § 10459-10, part.] 

80.24.050 Penalty for failure to pay fees. Every company, or the officers, agents, or employees thereof, failing to pay the fees herein required shall be guilty of a misdemeanor, and in addition thereto shall be subject to a penalty of twenty-five dollars for each and every day that the fee remains unpaid after it becomes due; the penalty to be collected by the commission in a civil action. All fines and penalties collected under the provisions of this chapter shall be deposited in the public service revolving fund. [1923 c 107 

§ 2; 1921 c 113 § 3; RRS § 10419.] Sections 

Chapter 80.28

GAS, ELECTRICAL AND WATER COMPANIES 

80.28.010 Duties as to rates, services and facilities.

80.28.140 Inspection of gas and water meters.

Chapter 47.44.

80.28.010 Duties as to rates, services and facilities. All charges made, demanded, or received by any gas, electrical, or water company for gas, electricity, or water, or for any service rendered or to be rendered, in connection therewith, shall be just, fair, reasonable, and sufficient.

Every such company shall furnish such service, instrumentalities, and facilities as shall be safe, adequate, and efficient, and in all respects just and reasonable. 

All rules and regulations issued by any such company, pertaining to the sale or distribution of its product, shall be just and reasonable.

Every such company shall construct and maintain such facilities in connection with the manufacture and distribution of its product as will be efficient and safe to its employees and the public. [1911 

c 117 § 26; RRS § 10362.] 

1. Water company may enforce rule that users shall not place tap accessible to neighbors. State ex rel. Fogarty v. Everett Water Co., 38 Wash. 609 (1905). 

2. Gas franchise throughout city includes new additions as city limits are extended. Seattle Ltg. Co. v. Seattle, 54 Wash. 9 (1909). 

3. Cited in 76 Wash. 497. 

4. Under statutes providing for reasonable rates for electrical companies and change of the same upon hearings before the Commission, the law contemplates that the commission may by direct order terminate contracts which were outstanding at the effective date of the law; but as to contracts entered into subsequent to the going into effect of the law, it is not necessary to first obtain the affirmative consent of the Commission to the filing of a new schedule increasing the rates to meet the situation where changed conditions make the increase necessary. North Coast Power Co. v. Public Service Comm., 

114 Wash. 102 (1921). 

5. A tariff providing for net earnings of 4.12 per cent is not unreasonable, where the evidence shows the service is worth its cost. 

Rule that company may be compelled to furnish certain service at loss is not applicable, where there is no evidence that system as whole earns adequate return.

Reasonableness of rate not affected by fact that consumers made contributions toward construction of plant. North Coast Power Co. v. Kuykendall, 117 Wash. 563 (1921). 

6. Water furnished in accordance with a contract running with the land is not subject to regulation as to charges by the department. Ament v. Bickford, 139 Wash. 494 (1926).

7. Electricity generated for manufacture and other industrial uses is devoted to the public use.

State ex rel. Public Service Comm. v. Spokane & I. E. Ry., 89 Wash. 599, 

(1916), impliedly overruled.

Provisions of Article 21 of Constitution declaring, "the use of the waters of this state for irrigation, mining and manufacturing purposes shall be deemed a public use," require holding that use of water to generate electricity for manufacturing is a public use.

Right of condemnation allowed to an electrical company which proposes to generate electricity for manufacturing purposes. State ex rel. Chelan Electric Co. v. Superior Court, 142 Wash. 270 (1927). 

8. See: 170 Wash. 103. 

9. Supplying water to a few neighbors does not make a water system a public utility. There is no dedication to public use. Clark v. Olson, 177 Wash. 

237; 93 A.L.R. 240 (1934). 

10. A mere rate difference does not constitute unlawful discrimination. 

State ex rel. Puget Sound Power & Light Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 181 

Wash. 105 (1935). 

11. The burden of proving effective rates unreasonable ordinarily rests upon the party attacking them. A rate difference does not necessarily in dicate discrimination. State ex rel. Model Water and Light Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 199 Wash. 24 (1939).

13. Under this section, where a water company undertakes to furnish water for fire protection purposes, it is under obligation to render adequate and sufficient service. Op. Atty. Gen. July 27, 1918. 

14. Department has no power to affirmatively authorize abandonment of public service, yet it can refuse, after investigation, to order continuing service; nor can the Department force public service company to continue operating at a loss. Op. Atty. Gen. March 27, 1926.

15. Company furnishing water at wholesale rates to city of Asotin for distribution through city's own system is subject to regulation by the Department. Op. Atty. Gen. January 18, 1927. 

16. Department does not have jurisdiction over any of the rates, services and facilities of a water district. Op. Atty. Gen. July 18, 1927. 

17. Electric service rendered tenants by owner of an apartment house does not constitute a service to the public for hire, and rates are not under jurisdiction of Department. Op. Atty. Gen. February 13, 1929. 

18. Water company may not discontinue service to one patron because of delinquent charges due from a previous occupant of the same premises. Op. 

Atty. Gen. June 6, 1929. 

19. Electric company must make extensions to serve those in its territory not already served where the return would reasonably justify the making of such extensions. Op. Atty. Gen. January 4, 1932. 

20. Department cannot cancel tax clauses in filed tariffs without hearing thereon. Dept. Memo. June 13, 1941. 

21. Department may, in its discretion, render engineering service to utilities customers and prospective customers informing them as to lawful charges of utility company and means whereby such charges could be reduced. Op. 

Atty. Gen. September 25, 1941. 

22. Anti, duplication contract between privately-owned electrical company and an electrical cooperative association is, insofar as the private utility is concerned, subject to the approval or disapproval of the Department. Dept. 

Memo. July 22, 1946. 

23.

Public utility districts are governmental agencies within the purview of Chapter 238, L. 1947. Op. Atty. Gen. December 23, 1947. 

80.28.020 Commission to fix just, reasonable and compensatory rates. Whenever the commission finds, after hearing had upon its own motion or upon complaint, that the rates or charges demanded or collected by any gas, electrical, or water company for gas, electricity, or water, or in connection therewith, or that the rules, regulations, practices, or contracts affecting such rates or charges are unjust, unreasonable, unjustly discriminatory, or unduly preferential, or in any wise in violation of law, or that such rates or charges are insufficient to yield a reasonable compensation for the service rendered, the commission shall determine the just, reasonable, or sufficient rates, charges, regulations, practices, or contracts to be thereafter observed, and shall fix them by order. [1911 c 117 § 54, part; RRS § 10390, part.] 

1. Courts have jurisdiction to determine right of water company to charge fee for tapping main despite fact that Public Service Commission has exclusive jurisdiction to determine question involving reasonableness of rates. State ex rel. Hodgdon v. Hoquiam W. Co., 70 Wash. 682 (1912). 

2. Where a proposed change was contested on the complaint of third persons, it is not error for the Commission to rule that the company does not assume the burden of proving the reasonableness of the rates. 

Under this and other sections of the Public Service Act, the discretion of the Commission to enter upon an inquiry on its own motion, or to pursue an inquiry beyond the evidence brought before it by the complaining or defending parties, when no convincing evidence is offered by either party, cannot be controlled by the courts. State ex rel. Seattle v. Public Service 

Comm., 76 Wash. 492 (1913). 

3. Contracts whereby a water company acquired all water supply of a mill company and has agreed to furnish mill company with water free or at reduced rates for a stated period are neither void nor voidable.

Upon complaint of city as to water rates, Commission has jurisdiction to determine reasonableness of rates and adequacy of service as between city and water company, and under its discretionary power order contracts for free water terminated, and its order to that effect will be construed as a direction to the water company to institute proper legal proceedings to terminate such contracts.

The Commission may vacate order ordering contracts cancelled if it finds that a consideration passed at the time the contract was entered into. 

State ex rel. Raymond L. & W. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 83 Wash. 130 (1915). 

4. Regulation of rates of public service water company conferred on 

Commission. Raymond Lbr. Co. v. Raymond L. & W. Co., 92 Wash. 330 (1916). (The action in this case was the result of the Commission's order, upheld in the case of State ex rel. Raymond L. & W. Co. v. Public Service Comm., supra.) 

5. A contract by a public service corporation to furnish power for driving mill machinery, electric light for the mill plant, steam heat for its dry kilns, and make repairs to its machinery, in consideration for the transfer by the mill company of its steam plant which was to be used for generating electricity, does not constitute a public service contract, the contract being one for private service before the dedication of any service to the public. Sunset Shingle 

Co. v. Northwest Electric & Water Works, 118 Wash. 416 (1922). 

6. Water company furnishing service for protection against fire may charge extra for such service. North Coast P. Co. v. Pittock, etc., Co., 118 

Wash. 542 (1922). 

7. Public utility is entitled to earn a fair and reasonable return on its investment, and the Department may order a departure from franchise contract. State ex rel. Spokane v. Kuykendall, 119 Wash. 107 (1922). 

8. Finding that a power company's rates for supplying a certain city with electrical energy were unreasonable and discriminatory as compared to rates charged two other cities held not to justify Department's order to charge the former city the same rates as the others in absence of evidence that the latter rates were just, fair, reasonable, and sufficient. State ex rel. Puget Sound 

Power & Light Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 181 Wash. 105 (1935). 

9. Increasing franchise rates, Commission's power. 29 A.L.R. 356. 

80.28.030 Commission may order improved quality of commodity. Whenever the commission finds, after such hearing, that the illuminating or heating power, purity or pressure of gas, the efficiency of electric lamp supply, the voltage of the current supplied for light, heat, or power, or the purity, volume, and pressure of water, supplied by any gas, electrical, or water company is insufficient, impure, inadequate, or inefficient, it shall order such improvement in the manufacture, distribution, or supply of gas, in the manufacture, transmission, or supply of electricity, or in the storage, distribution, or supply of water, or in the methods employed by the company, as will in its judgment be efficient, adequate, just, and reasonable. [1911 c 117 § 54, part; RRS § 10390, part.] 

1. Assuming that the Department has jurisdiction to interfere with watermains laid in city streets in accordance with a franchise, held that no occasion therefor was shown by the evidence. State ex rel. Yakima v. Kuykendall, 117 Wash. 406 (1921). 

80.28.040 Commission may order improved service. Whenever the commission finds, after such hearing, that any rules, regulations, measurements, or the standard thereof, practices, acts, or services of any gas, electrical, or water company are unjust, unreasonable,

improper, insufficient, inefficient, or inadequate, or that any service which may be reasonably demanded is not furnished, it shall fix the reasonable rules, regulations, measurements, or the standard thereof, practices, acts, or service to be thereafter observed and furnished, and shall fix the same by order or rule. [1911 c 117 § 54, part; RRS 

§ 10390, part.] 

1. It is a reasonable regulation of a gas company to require that its customers owning apartment houses and desiring to install more than one meter shall provide a separate meter-room on each floor or in the basement where all meters may be installed.

The waiver by a gas company of its regulations as to buildings that had already been constructed does not estop the company from enforcing the regulations as to new buildings constructed with notice of the rules. State ex rel. Hallett v. Seattle Lighting Co., 60 Wash. 81; 30 L.R.A. (N.S.) 492 (1910). 

2. A shut-off of water from premises until the sum due is paid, held a reasonable and necessary provision for the collection of the charges, and runs against the premises served as well as against the tenant using the water. 

McCormacks v. Tacoma, 170 Wash. 103 (1932). 

3. The word "acts" denotes affirmative, voluntary action or performance as distinguished from mere omission. State ex rel. P.U.D. No. 1 of Okanogan 

County v. Dept. of Public Service, 21 Wn. (2d) 201 (1944). 

4. The statute does not authorize the department to require a utility company to furnish_schedules of rates for a new kind of service to persons or corporations or P.U.D.'s which have made no request for service, which have not shown themselves to be reasonably entitled to service, and which are in no position to accept the service if offered to them at a specified or any other rate. Id. 

5. Delegation to Commission of power to regulate issuance of securities by public utilities. 41 A.L.R. 889. 

6. Commission has no power to fix standards of thermal units per cubic foot of gas merely by issuing an order or making a rule but can promulgate such a rule only after due notice and hearing. Op. Atty. Gen. July 28, 1915. 

7. Commission has no jurisdiction to make a rule requiring a public service electrical company to accept warrants of an irrigation district as payment for electrical energy furnished such district. Op. Atty. Gen. May 6, 1921. 

8. Department has jurisdiction over contract for street lighting and fire protection service between utility and city of Longview. Op. Atty. Gen. 

August 15, 1932. 

80.28.050 Tariff schedules to be filed. Every such company shall file with the commission and shall print and keep open to public inspection schedules in such form as the commission may prescribe, showing all rates and charges made, established, or enforced, or to be charged or enforced, all forms of contract or agreement, all rules and regulations relating to rates, charges, or service, used or to be used, and all general privileges and facilities granted or allowed by the company. [1911 c 117 § 27; RRS § 10363.] 

Duty of electrical company to fix of public utility district: RCW 54.04rate for wholesale power on request .100. 

1. ". the proposed new rate of the lighting company, which was properly noticed and filed, becomes automatically effective, subject to be defeated by the Commission only in the manner already indicated." State ex rel. City of Seattle v. Public Service Comm., 76 Wash. 492 (1913). 

2. Compliance with this statute on the part of a company furnishing electrical energy will be assumed in the absence of a contrary showing. State ex rel. Model Water and Light Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 199 Wash. 24 (1939).

3. Under this section and that prohibiting any change in rates except on prescribed notice or on order of the Commission, a scheduled rate becomes the lawful rate during the time it is in force. Id. 

4. An electric power company is not required to file with the Department a schedule showing the rate at which it would furnish to PUD's the power required in the operation of their electrical properties, where the company has never furnished nor proposed to furnish such service and may never be called on to do so, and where no application for the service has ever been made. 

State ex rel. P.U.D. v. Dept. of Public Service, 21 Wn. (2d) 201 (1944). 

5. The words, or to be charged or enforced " have reference simply to those situations where the utility company proposes to change its established rates, or to inaugurate rates for types of service not previously furnished, or put into effect some new charge or rule of the company. Id. 66 6. A water company formed before passage of this act and furnishing water only to a certain few users all of whom had entered into water right contracts with the company prior to passage of the act, is nevertheless a public service company and must file its tariff schedule with the Commission. 

Op. Atty. Gen. November 25, 1911. 

80.28.060 Tariff changes-Statutory notice-Exception. Unless the commission otherwise orders, no change shall be made in any rate or charge or in any form of contract or agreement or in any rule or regulation relating to any rate, charge, or service, or in any general privilege or facility which has been filed and published by such a company except after thirty days' notice to the commission and publication for thirty days, which notice shall plainly state the changes proposed to be made in the schedule then in force and the time when the change will go into effect and all proposed changes shall be shown by printing, filing, and publishing new schedules, or shall be plainly indicated upon the schedules in force at the time and kept open to public inspection. The commission, for good cause shown, may allow changes without requiring the thirty days' notice by filing, in such manner as it may direct, an order specifying the changes so to be made and the time when it shall take effect. All such changes shall be immediately indicated upon its schedules by the company affected. When any change is made in any rate or charge, form of contract or agreement, or any rule or regulation relating to any rate or charge or service, or in any general privilege or facility, the effect of which is to increase any rate or charge then in existence, attention shall be directed on the copy filed with the commission to such increase by some character immediately preceding or following the item in such schedule, such character to be in form as designated by the commission. [1911 c 117 § 28; RRS 

§ 10364.] 

1. Under this section the new rates automatically go into effect upon the expiration of the thirty days, unless suspended or adjudged unreasonable by the public service commission in proceedings to determine the reasonableness of the rates. State ex rel. Seattle v. Public Service Comm., 76 Wash. 492 (1913). 

2. Rate contract entered after date of section need not be cancelled by affirmative action of commission, but is abrogated by the filing of a higher tariff, found to be reasonable after hearing. North Coast Power Co. v. Public 

Service Comm., 114 Wash. 102 (1921).

3. It must be assumed until the contrary is shown, that respondent complied with the statute. State ex rel. Model Water and Light Co. v. Dept. of 

Public Service, 199 Wash. 24 (1939). 

4. Power company filing a reduced rate for movie theaters, including electricity for both light and power, is filing a change in schedule, even though no such rate previously existed and must give 30-day notice. Op. Atty. 

Gen. March 1, 1916. 

5. Public service company abolishing a discount provision for the prompt payment of bills formerly contained in its tariff is changing its rates and must comply with laws requiring notice of change in rates and secure approval of the Department. Dept. Memo. August 7, 1946. 

80.28.070 Sliding scale of charges permitted. Nothing in this chapter shall prohibit a gas, electrical, or water company from establishing a sliding scale of charges, whereby a greater charge is made per unit for a lesser than a greater quantity for gas, electricity, or water, or any service rendered. [1911 c 117 § 32; RRS § 10368.] 

80.28.080 Published rates to be charged-Exceptions. No gas, electrical, or water company shall charge, demand, collect, or receive a greater or less or different compensation for any service rendered or to be rendered than the rates and charges applicable to such service as specified in its schedule in effect at the time, nor shall it directly or indirectly refund or remit in any manner or by any device any portion of the rates or charges so specified, or furnish its product at free or reduced rates except to its employees and their families, and its officers, attorneys, and agents; to hospitals, charitable, and eleemosynary institutions and persons engaged in charitable and eleemosynary work; to indigent and destitute persons; to national homes or state homes for disabled volunteer soldiers and soldiers' and sailors' homes.

The term "employees" as used in this section includes furloughed, pensioned, and superannuated employees and persons who have become disabled or infirm in the service of any such company. The term "families," includes the families of such "employees," also the families of persons killed, and the widows during widowhood, and minor children during minority, of persons who died while in the service of any such company. 

Water companies may furnish free or at reduced rates water for the use of the state, or for any project in which the state is interested.

No such company shall extend to any person any form of contract or agreement or any rule or regulation or any privilege or facility except such as are regularly and uniformly extended to all persons and corporations under like circumstances. [1911 c 117 § 29; 

RRS § 10365.] 

1. The word "contract" as used in this section has reference to contracts between the utility company and its customers and not to franchise contracts with the municipality. Monroe Water Co. v. Town of Monroe, 135 Wash. 355 (1925). Overruling 130 Wash. 351 (1924). 

2. Electrical companies may grant reduced rates to churches for lighting services. Ops. Atty. Gen. 1915-16, p. 377.

80.28.090 Unreasonable preferences prohibited. No gas, electrical, or water company shall grant any undue or unreasonable preference or advantage to any person or locality, or to any particular description of service, or subject any particular person or locality or any particular description of service, to any undue or unreasonable prejudice or disadvantage. [1911 c 117 § 30; RRS § 10366.] 

1. Failure of a town to charge for water to which the user has a right because of an exception or reservation in contract is not a violation of this section. Duus v. Town of Ephrata, 14 Wn. (2d) 426 (1942). 

80.28.100 Rate discrimination prohibited. No gas, electrical, or water company shall, directly or indirectly, or by any special rate, rebate, drawback, or other device, charge, demand, collect, or receive from any person a greater or less compensation for gas, electricity, or water, or for any service rendered or to be rendered in connection therewith, except as authorized by this chapter, than it charges, demands, collects, or receives from any other person for doing a like or contemporaneous service with respect thereto under the same or substantially similar circumstances. [1911 c 117 § 31; 

RRS § 10367.] 

1. Where owner of premises supplied by private water service petitions public service corporation to extend service to his property and the said company in extending its mains at great expense purchases the private system, the company may charge a fee for connecting its system so as to supply petitioner's property. State ex rel. Foley v. Hillyard W. Co., 49 Wash. 232 (1908). 

2. Party discriminated against must, before resort to the courts, ask the 

Commission to file the complaint, or apply to the city authorities to do so, or induce other customers to join in the complaint. State ex rel. Goss v. Metaline 

Falls L. & W. Co., 80 Wash. 652 (1914). 

3. For case upholding water company's right to furnish water at free or reduced rates under contract existing at the time of the passage of this act, see State ex rel. Raymond Light and Water Co. v. Public Service Comm., 83 

Wash. 130 (1915). 

4. Commission has exclusive jurisdiction to adjust and determine a matter of alleged discrimination in rates, etc.; but where matter complained of is an arbitrary, unjust and illegal demand for an excessive payment for water, not in fact used, courts will grant relief. Johnson v. Pacific P. & L. Co., 90 Wash. 492 (1916). 

5. A mere rate difference does not constitute an unlawful discrimination. 

State ex rel. Puget Sound Power & Light v. Dept. of Public Works, 181 Wash. 

105 (1935). 

6. This section must be read in connection with the following section providing for a sliding scale of charges per unit depending upon quantity. State ex rel. Model Water & Light Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 199 Wash. 24 

(1939). 

7. To establish a violation of this section, complainant must show that the services for which the alleged discriminatory rate was charged were rendered under the same or substantially similar circumstances or conditions or that the rates charged were not just, fair, reasonable, or sufficient. Id. 

8. A mere rate difference does not constitute an unlawful discrimination, though a comparison of rates may be persuasive or controlling provided conditions are comparable and the rates used for comparison are just, fair, reasonable, and sufficient. Id. 

9. A schedule of rates wherein a higher rate is imposed if payment is not made within a certain time after the bill is presented is not a violation of this

section but a valid provision, within reasonable limits, as a premium upon prompt payment of bills. Id. 

10. Credit, discrimination by public utility company in respect of extension of. 12 A.L.R. 964. 

11. Franchise provisions for free or reduced rates of public service corporation as within constitutional or statutory provision prohibiting discrimination. 15 A.L.R. 1200. 

12. Deposit or guaranty, discrimination between its patrons by public service corporation in regard to furnishing. 31 A.L.R. 1411. 

13. Resale, right of electric company to discriminate against a concern which desires service for. 33 A.L.R. 638. 

14. Graduating public utility rates according to liability assumed. 35 

A.L.R. 336. 

15. Variations of electric utility rates based on quantity used. 67 A.L.R. 821. 

80.28.110 Service to be furnished on demand. Every company engaged in the sale and distribution of gas, electricity, or water shall, upon reasonable notice, furnish to all persons who apply therefor and are reasonably entitled thereto, suitable facilities for furnishing and furnish all available gas, electricity, and water as demanded. 

[1911 c 117 § 33; RRS § 10369.] 

Duty of electrical company to fix of public utility district: RCW 54.04rate for wholesale power on request .100. 

1. An electric power company is not required to file with the Department a schedule showing the rate at which it would furnish to PUD's the power required in the operation of their electrical properties, where the company has never furnished nor proposed to furnish such service and may never be called on to do so, and where no application for the service has ever been made. State ex rel. P.U.D.'s v. Dept. of Public Service, 21 Wn. (2d) 201 

(1944). 

2. Electric company must make extensions to serve those in its territory not already served where the return would reasonably justify the making of such extensions. Op. Atty. Gen. January 4, 1932. 

80.28.120 Effect on existing contracts. Every company owning, operating, or managing a plant or system for the distribution and sale of gas, water, or electricity to the public for hire shall be a public service company as to such plant or system and as to all gas, water, or electricity distributed or furnished therefrom, whether such gas, water, or electricity is sold wholesale or retail or distributed wholly to the general public or in part as surplus gas, water, or electricity to manufacturing or industrial concerns or to other public service companies or municipalities for redistribution. Nothing herein shall prevent any such company from continuing to furnish its. product or the use of its lines, equipment, or service under any contract in force on June 7, 1911, at the rates fixed in such contract: 

Provided, That the commission may direct by order that such contract be terminated by the company party thereto and thereupon the contract shall be terminated as directed by such order. [1933 c 165 

§ 1; 1911 c 117 § 34; RRS § 10370.] 

1. No power given to Commission to grant, modify or abrogate franchises or contracts arising out of franchises, except in regard to rates and the regulation of service in respect to its safety, efficiency, and equality. Tacoma 

R. & P. Co. v. Tacoma, 79 Wash. 508 (1914).

2. Under its discretionary power, Commission may order contracts for free water terminated, and its order to that effect will be construed as a direction to the water company to institute proper legal action to terminate such contracts.

Commission may vacate order directing cancellation of contracts if it finds that a consideration passed at time of contract was entered into. State ex rel. 

Raymond L. & W. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 83 Wash. 130 (1915). 

3. Contracts may be terminated, even if prior to act if consideration not paid-police power-impairing contracts-due process. Raymond Lumber 

Co. v. Raymond L. & W. Co., 92 Wash. 330; L.R.A. 1917C 574 (1916). 

4. It is the policy of the public service law to permit a public utility to receive a fair and reasonable return upon the reasonable value of its property, and the Department has the right to order a departure from a franchise contract, which by this section is binding until the Department has ordered a departure therefrom. State ex rel. Spokane v. Kuykendall, 119 Wash. 107 (1922). 

5. Sweeping power to readjust rates contrary to franchise denied. Monroe Water Co. v. Monroe, 135 Wash. 355 (1925). 

6. See also: 6 A.L.R. 1659; 9 A.L.R. 1423; 10 A.L.R. 499; 14 A.L.R. 252; 

29 A.L.R. 356. 

7. Contract for services by public utility in consideration of conveyance of property as affected by Public Utility Acts. 41 A.L.R. 257. 

8. Under this section a subsidiary electric company selling its entire output wholesale to its parent company is not excused from paying fees based on its gross revenue. Dept. Memo. October 21, 1941. 

9. Provision in tariff of electrical company giving it power to refuse to sell energy wholesale to a distributor who proposes to resell to it in a territory already being served by the electrical company is a reasonable one. 

Dept. Memo. September 30, 1943. 

80.28.130 Repairs and improvements may be directed. Whenever the commission finds, after hearing had upon its own motion or upon complaint, that repairs or improvements to, or changes in, any gas or electric plant or water system ought to be made, or that any additions or extensions should reasonably be made thereto, in order to promote the security or convenience of the public or employees, or in order to secure adequate service or facilities for manufacturing, distributing, or supplying gas, electricity, or water, it may enter an order directing that such reasonable repairs, improvements, changes, additions, or extensions to such plant or system be made. [1911 c 117 

§ 70; RRS § 10406.] 

1. Dismissal of application by a city to the Commission to compel a water company to renew and relocate its mains before city paved streets, upheld without deciding question of Commission jurisdiction to order such changes. 

State ex rel. City of Yakima v. Kuykendall, 117 Wash. 406 (1921). 

2. Public Service Commission's power to require public utility to extend gas service into new territory. 31 A.L.R. 333. 

80.28.140 Inspection of gas and water meters. The commission may appoint inspectors of gas and water meters whose duty it shall be to inspect, examine, prove, and ascertain the accuracy of all gas and water meters used or intended to be used for measuring the quantity of gas for light, heat, or power, or the quantity of water furnished for any purpose by any public service company to or for the use of any person, and when found to be or made to be correct

such inspectors shall seal all meters with some suitable device to be prescribed by the commission. 

No public service company shall thereafter furnish, set, or put in use any gas or water meter which has not been inspected, proved, and sealed by an inspector under such rules and regulations as the commission may prescribe. [1911 c 117 § 74, part; RRS § 10410, part.] 

80.28.150 Inspection of electric meters. The commission may appoint inspectors of electric meters whose duty it shall be to inspect, examine, prove, and ascertain the accuracy of all electric meters used or intended to be used for measuring the quantity of electric current furnished for light, heat, or power by any public service company to or for the use of any person and to inspect, examine, and ascertain the accuracy of all apparatus for testing and proving the accuracy of electric meters, and when found to be correct the inspector shall mark all such meters and apparatus with some suitable device to be prescribed by the commission. No public service company shall furnish, set, or put in use any electric meters the type of which has not been approved by the commission. [1911 c 117 § 74, part; RRS § 10410, part.] 

80.28.160 Testing apparatus to be furnished. Every gas, electrical, and water company shall prepare and maintain such suitable premises, apparatus, and facilities as may be required and approved by the commission for testing and proving the accuracy of gas, electric, or water meters furnished for use by it by which apparatus every meter may be tested. [1911 c 117 § 74, part; RRS § 10410, part.] 

80.28.170 Testing at consumer's request. If any consumer to whom a meter has been furnished shall request the commission in writing to inspect such meter, the commission shall have it inspected and tested, and if it is found to be more than four percent, if an electric meter, or more than two percent, if a gas or water meter, incorrect to the prejudice of the consumer, the expense of inspection and test shall be borne by the company; otherwise the expense shall be borne by the consumer. [1911 c 117 § 74, part; RRS § 10410, part.] 

1. Department has no authority to prescribe allowable variation for gas, electric and water meters. Op. Atty. Gen. May 13, 1927. 

80.28.180 Rules and regulations. The commission shall prescribe such rules and regulations to carry into effect the above provisions concerning meters as it deems necessary, and shall fix the uniform and reasonable charges for the inspection and testing of meters upon complaint. [1911 c 117 § 74, part; RRS § 10410, part.]
