Op. Atty. Gen. Sept. 17, 1941. 

81.04.170 Review of orders. Any complainant or any public service company affected by any findings or order of the commission, and deeming them contrary to law, may, within thirty days after

the service of the findings or order upon him or it, apply to the superior court of Thurston county for a writ of review, for the purpose of having the reasonableness and lawfulness of the findings or order inquired into and determined. 

The writ shall be made returnable not later than thirty days from the date of its issuance, unless upon notice to all parties affected further time is allowed by the court, and shall direct the commission to certify its record in the case to the court. The cause shall be heard by the court without a jury, on the evidence and exhibits introduced before the commission and certified to by it. Upon the hearing the court shall enter judgment either affirming or setting aside or remanding for further action the findings or order of the commission.

The reasonable cost of preparing the transcript of testimony taken before the commission shall be assessable as part of the court costs, and the amount thereof, if collected by the commission, shall be deposited in the public service revolving fund. In case the findings or order are set aside, or reversed and remanded, the court shall make specific findings based upon evidence in the record indicating clearly all respects in which the commission's findings or order are erroneous. [1937 c 169 § 3; 1911 c 117 § 86; RRS § 10428.] 

1. Act is not unconstitutional because review is upon evidence taken before the Commission, as it also provides for a reversal with instructions to receive evidence offered and improperly rejected. 

Upon review of order of Commission, evidence used in other cases may be certified by reference thereto, under stipulation in the return to the writ. 

Manner of certifying record by Commission. State ex. rel. O. R. & N. Co. v. 

R. R. Comm., 52 Wash. 17 (1909). 

2. An order of the Railroad Commission reducing switch charges, based largely upon an old rate that had been continued in force for some time, and upon conflicting evidence as to the time consumed in the service and as to the other services performed by the switching crew, is aided by the presumption that a rate continued in force for some time is presumed to be remunerative and by the presumption that the findings of the Commission are correct; and the order will not be disturbed on appeal although the testimony is meager, indefinite, and unsatisfactory. Northern Pacific R. Co. v. Railroad Comm., 57 Wash. 134 (1910). 

3. The provisions in the Railway Commission law, giving to railroad companies the right of appeal from orders of the superior court on reviewing orders of the Railroad Commission, was not intended to restrict the right of appeal to railroad companies.

The Railroad Commission has such an interest in defending its orders concerning railroad service and facilities made in proceedings instituted by it, as to entitle it to appeal to the supreme court from orders of the superior court reversing the orders of the Commisison. State ex rel. Great Northern R. Co. v. Railroad Comm., 60 Wash. 218 (1910). 

4. The statute providing for appeals from orders of the superior court reviewing orders of the Railroad Commission reducing railroad rates, which the superior court in its discretion might "suspend" pending the hearing, and which requires that bonds "shall" be required in addition to the usual appeal bond, was mandatory and contemplated a compensatory bond to supersede the judgment pending the appeal to the supreme court; especially in view of the provision allowing appeal "as in other civil cases." State ex rel. Puget Sound 

Elec. R. v. Mitchell, 60 Wash. 660 (1910).

5. Order of Commission that railroad construct depot is final unless appealed from, and failure to comply therewith subjects railroad to penalties provided.

Commission may fix time within which construction shall be completed. 

Penalty for failure to comply attaches when time fixed in order expires.

No defense that it was finished before suit for penalty was brought. State ex rel. Comm. v. Great Northern R. Co., 68 Wash. 257 (1912). 

6. Company is bound by order requiring construction and maintenance of spur-track, when the spur was built and paid for, and the company took no appeal. Great Northern R. Co. v. Comm., 69 Wash. 579 (1912). 

7. Findings of the Commission upon expert evidence should not be disturbed unless they show evidence of arbitrary action and disregard of the material rights of the parties to the controversy. 

The review provided in this section is not limited to final orders and writ is properly quashed if not taken out within thirty days after "any" order. 

State ex rel. Great Northern Ry. Co. v. Comm., 76 Wash. 625 (1913). 

8. The reasonableness and lawfulness of an order of the Commission respecting train service to be rendered a town is reviewable, and the presumption that the Commission acted reasonably and lawfully must be clearly overthrown before the orders will be set aside. State ex rel. Great Northern Ry. 

Co. v. Public Service Comm., 81 Wash. 275 (1914). 

9. Under this section, certiorari lies to review Commission's order for the repayment by a carrier of an overcharge; and the carrier need not first assail the order in an action to recover the overcharge. State ex rel. Tacoma Eastern 

Ry. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 102 Wash. 589 (1918). 

10. A decision ordering a recovery for overcharges on complaint filed by a shipper, and judgment on certiorari affirming the same are not decisions on the merits reviewable on appeal; since they but give the shipper the right to sue on the award, and are merely prima facie evidence of the facts stated. 

State ex rel. Tacoma Eastern R. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 112 Wash. 629 

11. Order of Department reopening cause is an appealable order.

Northern 

Pacific Ry. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 122 Wash. 673 (1923). 

12. Question of admissibility of evidence not reviewable on appeal when not objected to either before Department's examiner or before superior court. 

Great Northern Ry. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 137 Wash. 548 (1926). 

13. Prohibition does not lie to prevent superior court from hearing a petition for a rehearing of a writ of review, when real party in interest was not notified of first hearing. State ex rel. Sumner-Tacoma Stage Co. v. Superior 

Court, 138 Wash. 376 (1926). 

14. Successful party before the Department is a necessary party in review proceedings.

Department is a necessary party in review proceedings. Department is keeper of record and it only could certify record. Thereafter, Department represents only the public interest.

Review proceedings are void as to persons not parties thereto. Sumner- 

Tacoma Stage Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 142 Wash. 594 (1927). 

15. Valuation order that is merely a step in a rate controversy must be reviewed in Thurston County.

In valuation proceeding that is to determine rates or service, all orders entered therein must be reviewed in Thurston County. State ex rel. P. P. & L. 

Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 143 Wash. 67 (1927). 

16. Prohibition does not lie to prevent erroneous exercise of jurisdiction when Department has jurisdiction over parties and subject matter and an appeal is provided by statute. State ex rel. Grays Harbor Ry. & L. Co. v. 

Denney, 150 Wash. 690 (1929). 

17. Under this section providing for a review by the courts to determine the reasonableness and lawfulness of any order of the Department relating to certificates of convenience, with power to either affirm or reverse the order or remand for further evidence, the courts have no original jurisdiction to decide any question not passed upon by the Department; and where the De-

partment, on application for a certificate of convenience for a ferry service, refused to consider the jurisdictional question as to whether the territory was "already served," the court has no power to determine the fact, but must remand the case to the Department for further findings of fact. Puget Sound 

Nav. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 152 Wash. 417 (1929). 

18. Where in a rate case against a carrier, the reduction of the company's tariff was not sustained by competent evidence, and neither party has had a fair trial on the issues presented, on reversal of the judgment the cause will be remanded for further consideration of such evidence as the parties submit. 

The power to fix the rates of a common carrier is a legislative power, and on review the courts are limited to the inquiry whether the Department's findings and conclusions are justified by the record, and the court will not make findings for the Department. Great Northern R. Co. v. Dept. of Public 

Works, 161 Wash. 29 (1931). 

19. Amendatory act L. '31, chapter 119, held invalid. North Bend Stage

Line, Inc. v. Dept. of Public Works, 170 Wash. 217 (1932). 

20. The supreme court having original common law certiorari jurisdiction will, in its discretion, retain and dispose of proceedings to review an order of the Department respecting a certificate of necessity and convenience sought to be appealed, where the time for taking an appeal to the superior court has passed, and the injured party has lost its right of appeal through no fault of its own. Kitsap County Transportation Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 170 Wash. 

396 (1932). 

21. The Supreme Court will, in its discretion, retain and dispose of proceedings to review an order of the Department respecting a certificate of convenience and necessity sought to be appealed under the act of 1931 where the time for taking an appeal to the superior court has passed and the injured party has lost its right of appeal through no fault of its own. North Bend 

Stage Line, Inc. v. Dept. of Public Works, 170 Wash. 217 (1932); Kitsap County Transportation Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 170 Wash. 396 (1932). 

22. Chapter 119, L. 1931, amending this section, held unconstitutional since it gave a right of appeal direct to the supreme court from orders of the Department respecting certificates of public convenience and necessity; and the real nature of the review proceedings is an appeal granted to the injured party as a matter of right rather than a statutory writ of error or certiorari, which is a matter of discretion. Id. 

23. Commission may consider the result of its own inspection of a physical situation and its findings will be judged accordingly on review. State ex rel. 

Country Club of Seattle v. Dept. of Public Service, 198 Wash. 37 (1939). 

24. In determining reasonableness of rate fixed by the Department, the court may inquire whether facts found are sufficient to justify the order and whether findings are based on substantial evidence. State ex rel. Bohon v. 

Dept. of Public Service, 6 Wn. (2d) 676 (1940). 

25. Court's power in rate-making review is limited to inquiry whether findings and conclusions of the Department are justified by the record. Id. 

26. The Department possesses exclusive and original jurisdiction and courts may review and determine only those questions actually passed upon by it. State ex rel. Northeast Transportation Co. v. Abel, 10 Wn. (2d) 349 (1941). 

27. Where court reviewing order of the Department adopted its findings as its own, assignment of error that court erred in making certain findings could be considered. Manlowe Transfer & Distributing Co., Inc. v. Dept. of Public 

Service, 18 Wn. (2d) 754; 155 A.L.R. 928 (1943). 

28. RCW 81.80.360 makes this section and RCW 81.04.410, relating to the conclusiveness of orders and rules applicable to persons within the purview of the motor carrier act with the result that where no party seeks relief against the Department's rate schedule for motor carriers, the rates must stand unless inapplicable for some reason other than that they are unreasonable. Trudeau v. Pacific States Box & Basket Co., 20 Wn. (2d) 561 (1944). 

29. The Department was entitled to costs for preparation of transcript of the testimony taken before it, notwithstanding that the transcript was prepared by

the Department for its own convenience before decision on the application.

Taylor-Edwards W. & T. Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 22 Wn. (2d) 565 (1945). 

30. The provision in this section for allowance of costs of preparing transcript of testimony is not void on the ground that the title to the act gives no notice that it contains a provision as to costs since the act is amendatory to a general act relating to public service companies and providing additional super vision and regulation. Dept. of Public Service ex rel. Anacortes Chamber of 

Commerce v. Shelton, 23 Wn. (2d) 526 (1945). 

31. When, in spite of asserted abandonment of certificate, the operations of a common carrier were brought before the Department, and the Department after proceedings under RCW 81.04.130, entered its order after the close of the hearing, the carrier was a public service company, subject to the jurisdiction of the Department, and had a right to have the order reviewed by the superior court and under RCW 81.04.190 to appeal from that judgment to the 

Supreme Court. State ex rel. Puget Sound Navigation Co. v. Dept. of Trans portation, 33 Wn. (2d) 448 (1949). 

32. A common carrier who, prior to rendition of a final judgment by the superior court in proceedings under this section, gave notice of its intention to abandon intrastate operations and surrender its certificate of convenience and necessity, and who thereafter resisted injunction proceedings by the Department against discontinuance of the intrastate business, did not thereby acquiesce or impliedly recognize the validity of the judgment as subsequently entered by the superior court and did not thereby waive and was not estopped to appeal to the Supreme Court therefrom, nor had the questions involved therein become moot. Id. 

33. Findings of the Department are to be given the same weight accorded those of any impartial tribunal and may not be overturned unless the clear weight of the evidence is against them or unless the Department has mistaken the law applicable to the matter adjudicated. Id. 

34. Where it was agreed that the Department would consider the report of the Federal Wage Arbitrator before issuing its order establishing common carrier rates and subsequently the Department's order was issued on the same day the Arbitrator issued his report, the carrier had been denied a full and fair hearing and the judgment of the superior court affirming the Department's order was reversed with instructions to remand the cause to the Department for further proceeding. Id. 

35. Under this section, the Department can recover, in cases where it is entitled to costs, only the cost of preparing the original transcript and the ordinary attorney and court fees. Op. Atty. Gen. Aug. 25, 1941. 

81.04.180 Supersedeas. The pendency of writ of review shall not of itself stay or suspend the operation of the order of the commission, but the court may restrain or suspend, in whole or in part, the operation of the order pending the final hearing and determination of the cause.

No order restraining or suspending an order of the commission relating to rates, fares, or charges, or rules or regulations, practices, classifications, or contracts affecting them, shall be made by the court otherwise than upon three days' notice and after hearing, and if a supersedeas is granted, the order granting it shall contain a specific finding, based upon evidence submitted to the court, and identified by reference thereto, that great or irreparable damage would otherwise result to the petitioner, and specifying the nature of the damage.

In case the order of the commission under review is superseded, the court shall require a bond, with good and sufficient surety, con-

དྲ་ 1 ditioned that the company petitioning for review shall answer for all damages caused by the delay in the enforcement of the order of the commission, and all compensation for whatever sums for transportation, any person shall be compelled to pay pending the review proceedings in excess of the sum such person would have been compelled to pay if the order of the commission had not been suspended.

The court, in addition to, or in lieu of, the bond herein provided for, may require such other or further security for the payment of the excess charges or damages as it may deem proper. [1933 c 165 

§ 6; 1911 c 117 § 87; RRS § 10429.] 

1. This section is mandatory and contemplates compensatory bond to supersede the judgment pending the appeal to the supreme court. State ex rel. P. S. Elec. Ry. Co. v. Superior Court, 60 Wash. 660 (1910). 

2. Railroad company is bound by the order of the Commission where it built a spur track pursuant to the Commission's order and took no appeal. 

G. N. Ry. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 69 Wash. 579 (1912). 

3.

Supersedeas necessary to stay action by shipper on refund order of 

Department. Tacoma Grain Co. v. Northern Pacific Ry. Co., 123 Wash. 664 

(1923). 

81.04.190 Appeal to supreme court. The commission, any company, or any complainant may, within twenty days after the entry of judgment in the superior court in any action of review, prosecute an appeal to the supreme court. Such appeal shall be taken by giving a notice of appeal in open court at the time of the rendition of judgment, or by the service and filing of a notice of appeal within twenty days from and after the entry of the judgment. 

The appellant shall have fifty days after the entry of judgment in which to serve and file his opening brief, and the respondent shall have thirty days after the service of the opening brief in which to answer. The appellant shall have twenty days after the service of respondent's brief in which to reply. After the filing of the briefs, or the expiration of the time for filing briefs, the cause shall be assigned for hearing at the earliest motion day of the court, or at such other time as the court shall fix, and the clerk of the court shall notify the attorneys for the respective parties of the date set for the hearing in time to permit them to participate in the hearing. 

The original transcript of the record and testimony filed in the superior court in any action to review an order of the commission together with a transcript of the proceedings in the superior court, shall constitute the record on appeal. 

No appeal shall be effective, when taken by a public service company or a complainant, unless a cost bond on appeal in the sum of two hundred dollars is filed within five days after the service of the notice of appeal.

The superior court may suspend its judgment pending the hearing in the supreme court, upon the filing of a bond, with good and sufficient surety, conditioned as provided for bonds upon actions for

review, or upon such other terms and conditions as it may deem proper.

The general laws relating to appeals to the supreme court shall, so far as applicable and not in conflict with the provisions of this chapter, apply to appeals taken under the provisions hereof. [1911 

c 117 § 88; RRS § 10430.] 

1. If Commission is an aggrieved party by the order of the superior court, it may appeal to supreme court. Commission has an interest in defending its orders.

Presumption is that orders of Commission are reasonable. 

Great Northern Ry. Co. v. R. R. Comm., 60 Wash. 218 (1910). 

State ex rel. 

2. Jurisdiction of the superior court on review proceedings under this section. State ex rel. Sumner-Tacoma Stage Co. v. Superior Court, 138 Wash. 

376 (1926). 

3. While a mandamus proceeding with no other issue involved or not merely ancillary to jurisdiction already vested is not as a rule removable to the federal court, yet it is removable where its substantial purpose is to nullify an order of the Interstate Commerce Commission, in a matter affecting the public interest within the exclusive jurisdiction of the federal court. State ex rel. Dept. Public Works v. N. P. Ry. Co., 172 Wash. 37 (1933). 

4. Filing of briefs within 50 days is not mandatory and jurisdictional, and that time may be extended. United Truck Lines, Inc. v. Dept. of Public Works, 181 Wash. 318 (1935). 

5. Appeal and error Decisions reviewable- Amount in controversy.

Where an action for less than $200 does not involve the validity of a statute, but simply the construction of a tariff rule promulgated pursuant to statute, the supreme court has no jurisdiction, and the appeal will be dismissed. State ex rel. Home Tel. & Tel. Co. v. Hurn, 106 Wash. 362 (1919). 

6. Commission may consider the results of its own inspection of a physical situation and its findings will be judged accordingly on review. State ex rel. 

Country Club v. Dept. of Public Service, 198 Wash. 37 (1939). 

7. The provisions of this section with regard to the taking of an appeal within 20 days are mandatory notwithstanding use of the term "may." State ex rel. Dept. of Public Service v. Northern Pacific Railway Company, 200 

Wash. 663 (1939). 

8. The requirement of this section that appeal be taken within 20 days after entry of judgment is not dispensed with by Constitution, Article IV, sec. 

4, giving the supreme court original jurisdiction in habeas corpus, quo warranto and mandamus as to all state officers, there being nothing in that article authorizing a review of a judgment of the superior court in a case in which the supreme court has no jurisdiction because of failure to give timely notice of appeal. Id. 

9. The Department possesses exclusive and original jurisdiction and courts may review and determine only those questions actually passed upon by the 

Department. State ex rel. Northeast Transportation Co. v. Abel, 10 Wn. (2d) 

81.04.200 Rehearings before commission. Any public service company affected by any order of the commission, and deeming itself aggrieved, may, after the expiration of two years from the effective date of the order, petition the commission for a rehearing upon the matters involved in the order, setting forth the grounds and reasons for a rehearing, which may consist of changed conditions since the issuance of the order, or the showing of a result injuriously affecting the petitioner which was not considered at the former hearing, or that the effect of the order has been such as was not contemplated

by the commission or the petitioner, or for any good and sufficient cause which for any reason was not considered and determined in such former hearing.

Upon the filing of the petition, such proceedings shall be had thereon as are provided for hearings upon complaint, and the order may be reviewed as are other orders of the commission. No order superseding an order denying a rehearing shall be granted by the court pending the review.

In case any order shall not be reviewed, but shall be complied with by the company, a petition for rehearing may be filed within six months from the effective date of the order, and the proceedings thereon shall be as in this section provided. The commission may permit the filing of a petition for rehearing at any time. No order of the commission upon a rehearing shall affect any right of action or penalty accruing under the original order unless so ordered by the commission. [1911 c 117 § 89; RRS § 10431.] 

1. Rehearings intended only to correct defects and mistakes existing or inherent in the order at time of its entry and is not the proper procedure to establish a tariff increasing rates which was filed since the hearing. Op. Atty. 

Gen. Nov. 4, 1920. 

2. Purpose of this section is to provide for changed conditions since the issuance of the order or injurious result not anticipated in the order. P. S. 

Elec. Co. v. Lee, 207 Fed. 860. 

3. Carrier secured modification of order requiring station without changed conditions. Dryden Commercial Club v. Dept. of Public Works, 142 Wash. 

317 (1927). 

4. Order of an administrative tribunal is not invalidated by admission of matter not admissible under rules of evidence or for mere error in reasoning. 

Northern Pac. Ry. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 268 U. S. 39, 69 L. Ed. 837. 

Reversing (on a different point) 125 Wash. 584 (1923). 

81.04.210 Commission may change orders. The commission may at any time, upon notice to the company affected, and after opportunity to be heard as provided in the case of complaints, rescind, alter, or amend any order or rule made, issued, or promulgated by it, and any order or rule rescinding, altering, or amending any prior order or rule shall, when served upon the company affected, have the same effect as original orders and rules. [1911 c 117 § 90; RRS § 10432.] 

1. Department cannot reopen proceeding after final order of dismissal, when the injured party sought no writ of review, and when four years had passed and the statute of limitation for its presentment has run. Northern 

Pacific Railway Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 122 Wash. 673 (1923). 

2. Department on its own motion changed former order and ordered telechronometers out of Everett. State ex rel. American Telechronometer Co. v. 

West Coast Telephone Co., 164 Wash. 483 (1931). 

3. Reservation of jurisdiction for possible future action by the Department was immaterial in view of this section. State ex rel. O.-W. R. & N. Co. v. 

Walla Walla County, 5 Wn. (2d) 95 (1940). 

4. For other cases on this point, see: State ex rel. Bingen v. Dept.

of Public 

Works, 164 Wash. 563 (1931); N. P. Railroad Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 122 

Wash. 673 (1923).

81.04.220 Reparations. When complaint has been made to the commission concerning the reasonableness of any rate, fare, or charge for any service performed by any public service company, and it has been investigated by the commission, and the commission has determined that the company has charged an excessive or exorbitant amount for the service, and that any party complainant is entitled to an award of damages, the commission shall order that the company pay to the complainant the excess amount found to have been charged, whether it was charged and collected before or after the filing of the complaint, with interest from the date of collection of the excess amount. [1943 c 258 § 1; 1937 c 29 § 1; Rem. Supp. 1943 § 10433.] 

1. It is a condition precedent to an action to recover overcharges that the 

Commission determine the amount, under this section; hence an order of the 

Commission does not furnish a sufficient basis for action where it does not designate the individual shippers entitled to recover nor fix the amount due to each.

Where a railroad company invoked the aid of the Commission to cancel charges which were admitted to be unjust and discriminatory, and undertook with the Commission to make a refund to all claimants, it cannot invoke the statute of limitations provided by this section. 

The Commission has jurisdiction to investigate a charge of discrimination growing out of the violation of the long and short haul provision, under comprehensive provisions of this section. Belcher v. Tacoma Eastern R. Co., 99 

Wash. 34 (1917). 

2. Under section authorizing an appeal from "any order," certiorari lies to review an order for the repayment of an overcharge. State ex rel. Tacoma 

Eastern R. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 102 Wash. 589 (1918). 

3. Undercharge inadvertently made recovered. O.-W. R. & N. Co. v. Seattle 

Grain Co., 106 Wash. 1 (1919). 

4. Public Works Department has jurisdiction of overcharges on logs carried between points in this state from private road. Campbell River Mills 

Co. v. Chicago M. St. P. & P. R. R. Co., 42 F. (2d) 775. 

5. The superior court has jurisdiction, although the action relates to refund of excess freight charges prior to the passage of this section. State ex rel. 

Tacoma Eastern R. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 112 Wash. 629 (1920). 

6. Where, on a prior appeal, the law of the case has been decided as involving the measure of recovery for a violation by a carrier of the long and short haul provisions of the statute, the carrier cannot interpose an objection in an action to recover upon an award made by the Commission, that the shipper's case is one based upon discriminatory charges, thereby requiring a different measure of recovery. Belcher v. Tacoma Eastern R. Co., 117 Wash. 

512 (1921). 

7. Where a carrier has agreed that a preferential tariff on carriage of freight shall apply to other shippers of the same commodity between points named, its published tariff is in effect replaced by a lower one, and a shipper who has been compelled to pay freightage according to the published tariff is damaged to the extent of the difference between the published tariff and the preferential rate accorded the other shippers. Tacoma Eastern R. Co. v. 

Public Service Comm., 117 Wash. 609 (1921). 

8. A railroad company did not make legal delivery of cars, and could not recover demurrage charges where, owing to unprecedented floods, cars of logs consigned to defendant, could not be placed at the usual unloading place or near the plant or unloading spur so that they could be unloaded. Oregon- 

Wash. Railroad & Navigation Co. v. McGoldrick Lumber Co., 119 Wash. 119 (1922).

01 9. This section is not a mere statute of limitations but is a jurisdictional bar creating a limit beyond which no cause of action will lie for overcharges. 

Northern Pac. R. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 122 Wash. 673 (1923). 

10. If refund order not superseded, action may be brought though writ of review has issued.

Under this section, notwithstanding the only method of testing the reasonableness and lawfulness of the order is by writ of review, the mere pendency of a writ of review does not defeat the jurisdiction or right of action. Tacoma 

Grain Co. v. Northern Pac. R. Co., 123 Wash. 664 (1923). 

11. Where a railroad company assessed a shipper for demurrage charges on cars held under constructive placement and the shipper voluntarily paid the same without protest, after reaching a complete and satisfactory adjustment, the payments were voluntary and cannot be recovered back. N. P. Ry. Co. v. 

Dept. of Public Works, 125 Wash. 428 (1923). 

12. Reparation cannot be granted for freight charges which were assessed in compliance with the orders of the Department. N. P. Ry. Co. v. Dept. of 

Public Works, 136 Wash. 389 (1925). 

13. A carrier's discrimination in favor of shippers at one point, by charging less than the regular tariff, is not ground for the recovery of reparation by competing shippers at another point, who were charged the regular tariff; since the remedy for a rebate to one is by disciplinary measures, and not by reparation to all others charged only the lawful rate. Oregon-Wash. R. & 

Nav. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 151 Wash. 142 (1929). 

14. In an action by a shipper of logs to recover for overcharges, due to the railroad company's adopting a rule for scaling logs not mentioned in the tariff and making less deductions than the rule employed by the scaling bureau, it is not error to exclude evidence that the lumber cut from the logs scaled would be greater than indicated by the bureau's scale. Northern Pac. 

Co. v. Sauk River Lumber Co., 160 Wash. 691; 56 F. (2d) 656 (1931). 

15. In a rate case involving the shipment of oil, a finding of unreasonableness is not sustained by evidence of similar tariff rates for the same commodity between other places on the railway company's lines lower than the amount charged, where there was no traffic moving in such places; nor by evidence of lower rates in another state, where it was not shown that they were based on hauls made under like or similar conditions to the one in question. G. N. Ry. 

Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 161 Wash. 29 (1931). 

16. The Department has the duty to protect the traveling public from overcharges by a common carrier, and, at the same time, to maintain tariffs that will assure the carrier a fair and adequate return for the service rendered. 

State ex rel. Puget Sound Navigation v. Dept. of Transportation, 33 Wn. (2d) 

448 (1949). 

17. The Department is vested by law in proper cases with authority to direct payment of refunds by an order entered after a hearing. Id. 

18. Assuming that this section does not empower the Department to direct future refunds, it does not follow that it did not have authority to direct refunds in an order as actually entered. Id. 

19. Claims for refund of overcharges, validity of statute requiring submission of, to Public Service Commission. 3 A.L.R. 203. 

20. Attorney's fees, statutory provisions for, as a penalty for violation of statutory rates of transportation. 11 A.L.R. 892. 

21. Who may maintain action to recover back excessive freight charges. 

13 A.L.R. 289. 

22. Transportation Act as affecting power of Public Service Commission to award reparation for excessive rates. 52 A.L.R. 326. 

23. Liability of carrier for overcharging passenger. 56 A.L.R. 771. 

24. Power of Public Service Commission as to individual party's charge for telephone service in excess of that fixed by public utility. 73 A.L.R. 1194. 

25. Joinder or representation of several claimants in action against carrier or utility to recover overcharge. 1 A.L.R. (2d) 160.

26. When Commission has determined that a joint rate never authorized by the Commission was excessive and exorbitant, a refund ordered by the Commission shall be shared by the carriers in proportion to their share in the joint rate. Op. Atty. Gen. Jan. 16, 1917. 

27. Ordering of payment of interest on amount of refund determined is discretionary with Commission. Op. Atty. Gen. Feb. 24, 1925. 

81.04.230 Overcharges-Refund of. When complaint has been made to the commission that a public service company has charged an amount for any service rendered in excess of the lawful rate in force at the time the charge was made, and it has been investigated and the commission has determined that the overcharge allegation is true, the commission may order that the company pay to the complainant the amount of the overcharge so found, whether made before or after the filing of the complaint, with interest from the date of collection of the overcharge. [1937 c 29 § 2; RRS § 10433-1.] 

1. When Commission has determined that a joint rate never authorized by the Commission was excessive and exorbitant, a refund ordered by the Commission shall be shared by the carriers in proportion to their share in the joint rate. Op. Atty. Gen. Jan. 16, 1917. 

2. Ordering of payment of interest on amount of refund determined is discretionary with Commission. Op. Atty. Gen. Feb. 24, 1925. 

81.04.240 Action in court on reparations and overcharges. If the public service company does not comply with the order of the commission for the payment of the overcharge within the time limited in the order, action may be brought in any superior court where service may be had upon the company to recover the amount of the overcharge with interest. The commission shall certify and file its record in the case, including all exhibits, with the clerk of the court within thirty days after such action is started and the action shall be heard on the evidence and exhibits introduced before the commission and certified to by it. 

If the complainant shall prevail in the action, the court shall enter judgment for the amount of the overcharge with interest and shall allow complainant a reasonable attorney's fee, and the cost of preparing and certifying the record for the benefit of and to be paid to the commission by complainant, and deposited by the commission in the public service revolving fund, said sums to be fixed and collected as a part of the costs of the action. 

If the order of the commission is found contrary to law or erroneous by reason of the rejection of testimony properly offered, the court shall remand the cause to the commission with instructions to receive the testimony so proffered and rejected and enter a new order based upon the evidence theretofore taken and such as it is directed to receive.

The court may remand any action which is reversed by it to the commission for further action.

Appeals to the supreme court shall lie as in other civil cases. All complaints concerning overcharges shall be filed with the commis-

sion within six months, in cases involving the collection of unreasonable rates, and within two years, in cases involving the collection of more than lawful rates, from the time the cause of action accrues, and the action to recover the overcharge shall be filed in the superior court within one year from the date of the order of the commission.

The procedure provided in this section is exclusive, and neither the supreme court nor any superior court shall have jurisdiction save in the manner hereinbefore provided. [1943 c 258 § 2; 1937 c 29 § 3; 

Rem. Supp. 1943 § 10433-2.] 

1. Two-year period of limitation held valid. Hewitt Logging Co. v. 

Northern Pacific Ry. Co., 97 Wash. 597 (1917). 

2. Under this section certiorari lies to review the Commission's order for the repayment by a carrier of an overcharge; and the carrier need not first await the order in an action by the shipper to recover the overcharge. State ex rel. Tacoma Eastern R. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 102 Wash. 589 (1918). 

3. Assuming that this section does not empower the Department to direct future refunds, it does not follow that it did not have authority to direct refunds in an order as actually entered. State ex rel. Puget Sound Navigation 

Co. v. Dept. of Transportation, 33 Wn. (2d) 448 (1949). 

4. It has been held that filing of a claim with the regulatory body is a condition precedent to right to sue in courts. Op. Atty. Gen. Feb. 1, 1922. 

81.04.250 Determination of rates. The commission shall have the power upon complaint or upon its own motion to prescribe and authorize just and reasonable rates for the transportation of persons or property by carriers, and shall exercise such power whenever and as often as it shall deem necessary or proper. The commission shall, before any hearing is had upon such complaint or motion, notify the complainants and the carrier concerned of the time and place of such hearing by giving at least ten days' written notice thereof, specifying that at the time and place designated a hearing will be held for the purpose of prescribing and authorizing such rates, which notice shall be sufficient to authorize the commission to inquire into and pass upon the matters designated in this section. 

In exercising its aforesaid power the commission may use any standard, formula, method or theory of valuation reasonably calculated to arrive at the objective of prescribing and authorizing just and reasonable rates.

In the exercise of said power the commission may in its discretion give consideration in lieu of other factors to the following: (1) To the effect of such rates upon movement of traffic by such carriers; (2) To the public need for adequate transportation facilities, equipment and service at the lowest level of charges consistent with the provision, maintenance and renewal of such facilities, equipment and service; and (3) To the carrier need for revenue of a level which under honest, efficient and economical management is sufficient to cover the cost

(including all operating expenses, depreciation accruals, rents and taxes of every kind) of providing adequate transportation service, plus an amount equal to such percentage of said cost as shall be reasonably necessary for the provision, maintenance and renewal of said transportation facilities or equipment and a reasonable profit to the carrier. The relation of carrier expenses to carrier revenues may be deemed the proper test of a reasonable profit. [1951 c 75 § 1, last am'ds 1911 c 117 § 92; formerly RRS § 10441.] 

1. In fixing rates of interurban railroad, the annual renewal fund should be allowed upon which to draw for necessary replacement, but where company has failed to provide fund for back years, traffic of future years should not be made to bear deterioration of past years. Puget Sound Elec. Ry. Co. v. 

R. R. Comm., 65 Wash. 75 (1911). 

2. Pendency of review or appeal does not affect conclusiveness of findings. 

Title of public service law sufficiently broad to include power of Commission to value operating property of railroad for taxation purposes. Spokane & I. E. 

Ry. Co. v. Spokane County, 75 Wash. 72 (1913). 

3. Certiorari will not lie to review equalization by State Board of Equali zation. State ex rel. Spokane & I. E. Ry. Co. v. State Board, 75 Wash. 90 (1913). 

4. Valuation made by Commission is only conclusive as of the date stated and under the conditions thereunder stated, and action of taxing boards in accepting it a year after it was made is arbitrary. Spokane & I. E. Ry. Co. v. 

Spokane County, 82 Wash. 24 (1914). 

5. Under this section, "good will" may be considered as an asset in determining the value of a company's lines and property. Northern Pac. R. Co. v. 

State, 84 Wash. 510; Ann. Cas. 1916E 1166 (1915). 

6. Railway operating property is taxable. Canadian Pac. R. Co. v. King 

County, 90 Wash. 38 (1916). 

7. Public Service Commission act gave Commission substantially same powers with respect to valuation of railroad property as that possessed by railroad commission.

Commission to classify and value railway property and its finding is final except as to value for taxation purposes. 

Classification of railway "operating property" by Public Service Commission. N. P. Ry. Co. v. King County, 93 Wash. 89; G. N. Ry. Co. v. King County, 93 Wash. 697 (1916). 

8. Extended discussion of methods of valuation by Tax Commission and 

Public Service Commission. O.-W. R. & N. Co. v. Thurston County, 98 Wash. 

218 (1917). 

9. Utility is basis of valuation of railroad for taxation if it does not pay on actual or necessary cost. Inland Empire Ry. v. Whitman County, 128 Wash. 

358 (1924). 

10. "Prudent Investment Theory" sustained. Rates yielding return of 10% held not confiscatory. Department justified in excluding from rate base warehouses not in use and out of repair. Pac. Coast Elev. Co. v. Dept. of Public 

Works, 130 Wash. 620 (1924). 

11. Prohibition does not lie to prevent Department from making a valuation by an erroneous method, where the Department has jurisdiction of the parties and subject-matter and an appeal is granted by statute. Hardship caused by exercise of jurisdiction is not ground for prohibition. State ex rel. 

Grays Harbor Ry. & L. Co. v. Denney, 150 Wash. 690 (1929). 

12. Properties which are reasonably necessary to the proper operation of railroads and without which such railroads could not successfully operate their lines, and could not properly serve the public, should be included in the valuation upon which rates are based. Op. Atty. Gen. April 8, 1908. 

13. Where light company owned and operated a short street railway, the 

Commission in prescribing rates was not required to hold hearing and could

consider for valuation either the railway alone or the company's entire properties so long as rate established was reasonable. Op. Atty. Gen. April 21, 1915. 

81.04.260 Summary proceedings. Whenever the commission is of opinion that any public service company is failing or omitting, or about to fail or omit, to do anything required of it by law, or by order, direction, or requirement of the commission, or is doing anything, or about to do anything, or permitting anything, or about to permit anything to be done contrary to or in violation of law or of any order, direction, or requirement of the commission, it shall direct the attorney general to commence an action or proceeding in the superior court of Thurston county or in the superior court of any county in which such company is doing business, in the name of the state on the relation of the commission, for the purpose of having such violations or threatened violations stopped and prevented, either by mandamus or injunction.

The attorney general shall thereupon begin such action or proceeding by petition to such superior court, alleging the violation complained of, and praying for appropriate relief by way of mandamus or injunction. The court shall specify a time, not exceeding twenty days after the service of the copy of the petition, within which the public service company complained of must answer the petition.

In case of default in answer or after answer, the court shall immediately inquire into the facts and circumstances in such manner as it shall direct, without other or formal pleadings, and without respect to any technical requirement. 

Such persons or corporations as the court deems necessary or proper to be joined as parties, in order to make its judgment, order, or writ effective, may be joined as parties. 

The final judgment in any such action or proceeding shall either dismiss the action or proceeding or direct that the writ of mandamus or injunction, or both, issue as prayed for in the petition, or in such other modified form as the court may determine will afford appropriate relief.

An appeal may be taken to the supreme court from such final judgment in the same manner and with the same effect as appeals from judgments of the superior court in actions to review orders of the commission. All provisions of this chapter relating to the time of appeal, the manner of perfecting the same, the filing of briefs, hearings, and supersedeas, shall apply to appeals to the supreme court under the provisions of this section. [1911 c 117 § 93; RRS § 10442.] 

1. Under the statute, county commissioners are authorized to grant franchises to railroads outside limits of incorporated cities, but they have no authority to consent to abandonment.

Common carrier having accepted provisions of a permissive franchise cannot, against the will of the state, abandon the enterprise to the prejudice of the public necessity.

Injunction does not lie to prevent carrier from abandoning part of line between two stations served by a competing road lying closely parallel merely because abandonment deprives persons of competitive service. Day v. Tacoma Ry. & P. Co., 80 Wash. 161 (1914). 

2. In the absence of any special showing, the remedy by appeal is exclusive, and certiorari does not lie to review final judgment in summary proceedings brought by the Department against a common carrier for violation of a rate schedule, under this section which expressly provides for an appeal to the supreme court from such final judgment. State ex rel. Dept. of Public Works v. Superior Court, 155 Wash. 296 (1930). 

3. This section applies and provides a remedy in those cases only where a present or threatened specific violation of the act exists and cannot be used as a correction for past abuses nor to enjoin a future general course of conduct. 

State ex rel. Dept. of Public Works v. Skagit River Navigation & Trading Co., 

181 Wash. 642 (1935). 

4. Commission may proceed by injunction to restrain electrical and telephone companies from so constructing or maintaining their lines as to endanger public safety. Ops. Atty. Gen. 1911-12, p. 223. 

5. Commission may direct Attorney General to commence an action either in mandamus or injunction to compel a railroad company to comply with the act relating to the fencing of its right of way. Ops. Atty. Gen. 1911-12, p. 291. 

6. If it can first be determined in a particular case that it is the duty of a railroad to continue operations and that it is failing or about to fail to fulfill its obligations as a carrier, the Commission may, under this section, maintain mandamus or other proceedings to compel that company to perform its public duties. Ops. Atty. Gen. 1911-12, p. 320. 

7. Department has no authority to authorize affirmatively the abandonment of service by a public service company. Op. Atty. Gen. March 27, 1926; also March 31, 1926. 

81.04.270 Merchandise accounts to be kept separate. Any public service company engaging in the sale of merchandise or appliances or equipment shall keep separate accounts, as prescribed by the commission, of its capital employed in such business and of its revenues. therefrom and operating expenses thereof. The capital employed in such business shall not constitute a part of the fair value of the company's property for rate making purposes, nor shall the revenues or operating expenses of such business constitute a part of the operating revenues and expenses of the company as a public service company. 

[1933 c 165 § 8; RRS § 10458-2.] 

81.04.280 Purchase and sale of stock by employees. No public service company shall permit an employee to sell, offer for sale, or solicit the purchase of any security of any other person during the hours such employee is engaged to perform any duty of such company; nor shall a public service company by any device require an employee to purchase or contract to purchase any of its securities or those of any other person, or require an employee to permit the deduction from his wages or salary of any sum to be applied in payment of any purchase or contract to purchase any security of such company or of any other person. [1933 c 165 § 9; RRS § 10458-3.]

81.04.290 Sales of stock to employees and patrons. A public service company may sell to its employees and patrons any increase of its capital stock, or part thereof, without first offering it to existing stockholders: Provided, That such sale is approved by the holders of a majority of the capital stock, at a regular or special meeting held after notice given as to the time, place, and object thereof as provided by law and the bylaws of the company. Such sales shall be at prices and in amounts for each purchaser and upon terms and conditions as set forth in the resolution passed at the stockholders' meeting, or in a resolution passed at a subsequent meeting of the board of trustees if the resolution passed at the stockholders' meeting shall authorize the board to determine prices, amounts, terms, and conditions, except that in either event a minimum price for the stock must be fixed in the resolution passed at the stockholders' meeting. [1923 c 110 § 1; RRS § 10344-1.] 

1. Constitutionality of statute as to employee's purchase of stock. 63 

A.L.R. 841. 

81.04.300 Budgets to be filed by companies. The commission may regulate, restrict, and control the budgets of expenditures of public service companies. On or before the first day of November of each year each company shall prepare a budget showing the amount of money which, in its judgment, will be needed during the ensuing year for maintenance, operation, and construction, classified by accounts as prescribed by the commission, and shall file it with the commission for its investigation and approval or rejection. When a budget has been filed with the commission it shall examine into and investigate it to determine whether the expenditures therein proposed are fair and reasonable and not contrary to public interest. 

Adjustments or additions to budget expenditures may be made from time to time during the year by filing a supplementary budget with the commission for its investigation and approval or rejection. 

[1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

81.04.310 Commission's control over budgets. The commission may, both as to original and supplementary budgets, prior to the making or contracting for the expenditure of any item therein, and after notice to the company and a hearing thereon, reject any item of the budget. The commission may require any company to furnish further information, data, or detail as to any proposed item of expenditure.

Failure of the commission to object to any item of expenditure within sixty days of the filing of any original budget or within thirty days of the filing of any supplementary budget shall constitute authority to the company to proceed with the making of or contracting for such expenditure, but such authority may be terminated at any time by objection made thereto by the commission prior to the making of or contracting for such expenditure.

Examination, investigation, and determination of the budget by the commission shall not bar or estop it from later determining whether any of the expenditures made thereunder are fair, reasonable, and commensurate with the service, material, supplies, or equipment received. [1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

81.04.320 Budget rules and regulations. The commission may prescribe the necessary rules and regulations to place RCW 81.04.300 to 81.04.330 in operation. It may by general order, exempt in whole or in part from the operation thereof companies whose gross operating revenues are less than twenty-five thousand dollars a year. The commission may upon request of any company withhold from publication during such time as the commission may deem advisable, any portion of any original or supplementary budget relating to proposed capital expenditures. [1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

81.04.330 Effect of unauthorized expenditures - Emergencies.

Any public service company may make or contract for any rejected item of expenditure, but in such case the same shall not be allowed as an operating expense, or as to items of construction, as a part of the fair value of the company's property used and useful in serving the public: Provided, That such items of construction may at any time thereafter be so allowed in whole or in part upon proof that they are used and useful. Any company may upon the happening of any emergency caused by fire, flood, explosion, storm, earthquake, riot, or insurrection, or for the immediate preservation or restoration to condition of usefulness of any of its property, the usefulness of which has been destroyed by accident, make the necessary expenditure therefor free from the operation of these sections. 

Any finding and order entered by the commission shall be in effect until vacated and set aside in proper proceedings for review thereof. [1933 c 165 § 10, part; RRS § 10458-4, part.] 

81.04.340 Dividends, control of. No public service company engaged in intrastate business in this state shall pay any dividend upon its common stock until: (1) Its earnings and earned surplus are sufficient to declare and pay the same after provisions are made for reasonable and proper reserves. (2) The dividend proposed to be paid can reasonably be paid without impairing the ability of the company to render reasonable and adequate service at reasonable rates. 

Before any common stock dividend is paid, the company shall make application to the commission for approval, and shall furnish to the commission such information and data relating thereto as the commission shall require. 

If the commission finds after notice and an opportunity to be heard that the payment of the dividend will not be violation of the

provisions of this section it shall approve the declaration and payment thereof, otherwise it shall disapprove it. No dividend so disapproved shall be paid unless the findings and order of the commission with respect thereto have been vacated and set aside in proper proceeding for review thereof.

If at any time the commission finds that the capital of any company is impaired, it may, after due notice, investigation, and hearing, issue an order directing the company to cease paying dividends on its common stock until reasonable proof has been made to the commission that such impairment has been made good, and the status of the company has become such that common stock dividends may reasonably and properly be paid in full compliance with this section. 

[1933 c 165 § 11; RRS § 10458-5.] 

1. Commission has no jurisdiction to regulate payment of dividends by common carrier engaged in both interstate and intrastate business. Op. Atty. 

Gen. May 10, 1950. 

81.04.350 Depreciation and retirement accounts. The commission may, after hearing, require any public service company to carry proper and adequate depreciation or retirement accounts in accordance with such rules, regulations, and forms of accounts as the commission may prescribe. The commission may from time to time. ascertain and by order fix the proper and adequate rates of depreciation or retirement of the several classes of property of each company. Each company shall conform its depreciation or retirement accounts to the rates so prescribed. In fixing the rate of the annual depreciation or retirement charge, the commission may consider the rate and amount theretofore charged by the company for depreciation or retirement.

The commission shall exercise like authority over all other reserve accounts of such companies. [1937 c 169 § 4; 1933 c 165 § 13; 

RRS § 10458-7.] 

81.04.360 Excessive earnings to reserve fund. If any public service company earns in any year a net utility operating income in excess of a reasonable rate of return upon the fair value of its property used and useful in the public service, such excess shall be placed in a reserve fund. Such reserve fund shall from time to time and under rules and regulations prescribed by the commission and upon its order, be applied in whole or in part as earnings of the company in any subsequent year or years in which it does not earn a reasonable rate of return, or in establishing, replenishing, or maintaining amortization, depreciation, or other contingent funds, or for any other purpose beneficial to the consumers of the company. [1933 

c 165 § 14; RRS § 10458-8.] 

81.04.370 Manner of serving papers. All notices, applications, complaints, findings of fact, opinions, and orders required by this title to be served may be served by mail and service shall be deemed

complete when a true copy thereof is deposited in the post office properly addressed and stamped. [1933 c 165 § 7; RRS § 10458-1.] 

81.04.380 Penalty for violations by companies. Every public service company and every corporation, whether or not a public service company, which violates or fails to comply with any provision of this title or any order, rule, or requirement of the commission, shall be subject to a penalty of not to exceed one thousand dollars for each offense, and if the offense is a continuing one, each day's continuance thereof shall constitute a separate offense. [(i) 1911 c 117 § 94; RRS 

§ 10443. (ii) 1911 c 117 § 96; RRS § 10445.] 

1. Penalty for failure to obey an order to construct a depot within a specified time attaches at the expiration of the period. 

When order is definite as to time, and the company has acted upon it except as to time, complaint cannot be made that it was indefinite as to size and location of building.

No appeal being taken from order, it cannot be contended that the time allowed was unreasonably short. 

Delay by floods is not a defense, where floods occurred after expiration of time limit. State ex rel. R. R. Comm. v. G. N. Ry. Co., 68 Wash 257 (1912). 

2. Proceedings to compel operation-Individuals living along the line of a street railway cannot maintain an action to prevent the company from abandoning the route merely because they, "and many other residents similarly situated," are dependent on the line for railway service, in the absence of any allegation as to the number of persons affected who are too remote to use a new parallel route, or the manner in which the public convenience will be affected.

Injunction does not lie to prevent a street railway company from abandoning that part of its road lying between two stations served by a competing road lying closely parallel, merely because the abandonment necessarily results in depriving all persons to whom both roads are accessible of competitive service; since the whole theory of the public service law is opposed to the idea that the public will be better served with two closely parallel lines of road where one road will amply suffice. Day v. Tacoma R. & Power Co., 80 Wash. 161; L.R.A. 1915B 547 (1914). 

3. "Penalty" as used here is compensation in the nature of liquidated damages for failure to furnish cars. 

So-called penalty prescribed by Commission to be paid by a railroad company for failure to furnish cars is valid. 

Where legislative or judicial power is merely incidental to the exercise of administrative power for which Commission was created, courts will not declare delegation unconstitutional. State ex rel. C., M. & St. P. R. Co. v. 

Public Service Comm., 94 Wash. 274 (1917). 

81.04.390 Penalty for violations by individuals. Every officer, agent, or employee of a public service company and every person, who, as an individual or as an officer or agent of a corporation, violates or fails to comply with, or who procures, aids, or abets another, or his company, in the violation of, or noncompliance with, any provision of this title or any order, rule, or requirement of the commission, shall be guilty of a gross misdemeanor. [(i) 1911 c 117 

§ 95; RRS § 10444. (ii) 1911 c 117 § 97; RRS § 10446.] 

81.04.400 Actions to recover penalties. Actions to recover penalties under this title shall be brought in the name of the state in the superior court of Thurston county, or the superior court of any

county in which the public service company does business. In all such actions the procedure and rules of evidence shall be the same as in ordinary civil actions, except as otherwise herein provided. 

All fines and penalties recovered shall be paid into the state treasury. 

[1911 c 117 § 98; RRS § 10447.] 

1. Jurisdiction of state court of action for penalty under state law in relation to interstate shipment. 64 A.L.R. 361. 

81.04.410 Orders and rules conclusive. In all actions between private parties and public service companies involving any rule or order of the commission and in all actions for the recovery of penalties, or for the enforcement of the orders or rules of the commission, the orders and rules shall be conclusive unless set aside or annulled on review. [1911 c 117 § 99; RRS § 10448.] 

R.C.W 81.80.360 makes this section and RCW 81.04.170 relating to the conclusiveness of orders and rules applicable to persons within the purview of the motor vehicle act with the result that where no party seeks relief against the Department's rate schedule for motor carriers, the rates must stand unless inapplicable for some reason other than that they are unreasonable. Trudeau v. Pacific States Box & Basket Co., 20 Wn. (2d) 561 (1944). 

81.04.420 Commission intervention where order or rule is involved. In all court actions involving any rule or order of the commission where it has not been made a party, the commission shall be served with a copy of all pleadings, and shall be entitled to intervene.

Where the fact that the action involves a rule or order of the commission does not appear until the time of trial, the court shall immediately direct the clerk to notify the commission of the pendency of such action, and shall permit the commission to intervene therein.

The failure to comply with the provisions of this section shall render void and of no effect any judgment in such action, where the effect of such judgment is to modify or nullify any rule or order of the commission. [1943 c 67 § 1; Rem. Supp. 1943 § 10448-1.] 

81.04.430 Findings of department prima facie correct. Whenever the commission has issued or promulgated any order or rule, in any writ of review brought by a public service company to determine the reasonableness of such order or rule, the findings of fact made by the commission shall be prima facie correct, and the burden shall be upon the public service company to establish the order or rule to be unreasonable or unlawful. [1911 c 117 § 100; RRS § 10449.] 

1. Order of Commission reducing switching charges is aided by presumption that findings of Commission are correct, and will not be disturbed though evidence is meager, indefinite and unsatisfactory. N. P. Ry. Co. v. 

Railroad Comm., 57 Wash. 134 (1910). 

2. Findings of Commission on reasonableness of rate should not be disturbed unless made arbitrarily and without a full consideration of facts. 

Puget Sound Elec. R. R. Co. v. Railroad Comm., 65 Wash. 75 (1911). 

3. Upon review, the Commission will be presumed to have acted reasonably and lawfully, and such presumption must be clearly overthrown before

the order will be set aside. State ex rel. G. N. Ry. Co. v. Public Service Comm., 81 Wash. 275 (1914). 

4. Findings of the Commission are controlling unless they have been reached arbitrarily without due consideration of all the facts, or have been reached on a manifestly wrong basis. N. P. Ry. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 125 Wash. 584 (1923). 

5. Findings of Department are entitled to same presumption of verity accorded to findings of regular judicial tribunal and will be sustained unless against the clear weight of the evidence. 

The fact that complaint is filed by Department does not change the rule. 

Department may make its order retroactive to the date jurisdiction was acquired. Pacific Coast Elev. Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 130 Wash. 620 

(1924). 

6. Findings of Department will not be disturbed unless arbitrary. North 

Bend Stage Lines v. Denney, 153 Wash. 439 (1929). Affirmed: 283 U. S. 786, 

75 L. Ed. 1414 (1931). 

7. Findings of Department on question of fact will not be disturbed by court unless arbitrarily reached and without full and due consideration of all controlling facts. State ex rel. Winlock Water Co. v. Dept. of Public Works, 

180 Wash. 278 (1934). 

8. Even if this section did not direct it, findings of the Department would be taken as prima facie correct on review where made by experts with long familiarity and great personal knowledge of the subject. State ex rel. West 

Side Improvement Club v. Dept. of Public Service, 186 Wash. 378 (1936). 

9. The Department's findings are given the same weight as those of any other tribunal, and are final unless against the clear weight of the evidence. 

State ex rel. O.-W. R. & N. Co. v. Walla Walla County, 5 Wn. (2d) 95 (1940). 

10. The court must await findings by the Department informing of the ultimate facts which are considered prima facie correct. State ex rel. Bohon v. 

Dept. of Public Service, 6 Wn. (2d) 676 (1940). 

11. Department's findings must be given the same weight as those of any impartial tribunal and may not be overturned unless the clear weight of evidence is against them, or the Department mistook the law applicable, or unless the findings show arbitrariness and disregard of material rights of the parties. 

Manlowe Transfer and Dist. Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 18 Wn. (2d) 754; 

155 A.L.R. 928 (1943). 

12. If the clear weight of the evidence is against the findings of the Department, they will be set aside on review even though supported by substantial evidence. Id. 

81.04.440 Liability for damages. Any public service company which does or causes or permits to be done, or which fails to do any act, prohibited or required by law, or order or rule of the commission, shall be liable for all loss, damage, or injury resulting therefrom. 

In case of recovery, if the court finds that the act or omission was wilful, it may allow a reasonable attorney's fee as part of the costs. 

[1911 c 117 § 102; RRS § 10451.] 

81.04.450 Certified copies of orders, rules, etc.-Evidentiary effect. Upon the application of any person, the commission shall furnish certified copies of any classification, rate, rule, regulation, or order established by it, and the printed copies published by its authority, or any certified copy of any such classification, rate, rule, regulation, or order, with seal affixed, shall be admissible in evidence in any action or proceeding, and shall be sufficient to establish the fact that the charge, rate, rule, order, or classification therein contained is the official act of the commission.

TE TE When copies of any classification, rate, rule, regulation, or order not contained in the printed reports, or copies of papers, accounts, or records of public service companies filed with the commission are demanded for proper use, the commission shall charge a reasonable compensation therefor. [1911 c 117 § 103; RRS § 10452.] 

1. Employees of Commission may not keep money received for certified copies of documents made by said employee after working hours. Dept. Memo. 

Aug. 14, 1924. 

2. Department has no power or duty to furnish a private individual a copy of a labor agreement between a public service company and a union. Op. 

Atty. Gen. Sept. 10, 1941. 

81.04.460 Commission to enforce public service laws. The commission shall enforce the provisions of this title and all other acts affecting public service companies, the enforcement of which is not specifically vested in some other officer or tribunal. [1911 c 117 § 101; 

RRS § 10450.] 

1. Regulation of municipally owned water system denied to Commission.

State ex rel. Country Club of Seattle v. Dept. of Public Service, 198 Wash. 

37 (1939). 

2. Statute making it incumbent on school buses and explosives trucks to stop at railroad crossings and providing that failure so to do is a misdemeanor is not for the Department to enforce with regard to intracity bus operations. 

Op. Atty. Gen. Jan. 14, 1946. 

81.04.470 Rights of action not released. This title shall not release or waive any right of action by the state or any person for any right, penalty, or forfeiture under any law. [1911 c 117 § 104, part; 

RRS & 10453, part.] 

81.04.480 Penalties cumulative. All penalties arising under this title shall be cumulative and an action to recover one penalty is no bar to the recovery of any other penalty. [1911 c 117 § 104, part; 

RRS § 10453, part.] 

81.04.490 Application to municipal utilities. The commission shall make or enforce no order affecting the rates, fares, charges, contracts, or services, or the safety or adequacy of the facilities of, or the reasonableness of the rules affecting, any public service company owned and operated by a city. All other provisions herein shall apply to companies so owned and operated. [1911 c 117 § 105; RRS § 10454.]

Chapter 81.08

SECURITIES 

"Evidence of indebtedness"-Limitation of term.
