81.08.010 Definition. The term "public service company," as used in this chapter, shall mean every company now or hereafter engaged in business in this state as a public utility and subject to regulation as to rates and service by the public service commission: Provided, That it shall not include any such company the issuance of stocks and securities of which is subject to regulation by the Interstate 

Commerce Commission. [1933 c 151 § 1; RRS § 10439-1.] 

81.08.012 "Evidence of indebtedness"-Limitation of term. The term "evidence of indebtedness," as used in this chapter shall not include conditional sales contracts or purchase money chattel mortgages. [1951 c 227 § 2.] 

81.08.020 Control vested in state. The power of public service companies to issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, and bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness and to create liens on their property situated within this state, is a special privilege, the right of supervision, regulation, restriction, and control of which is vested in the state, and such power shall be exercised as provided by law and under such rules and regulations as the commission may prescribe. [1933 c 151 § 2; RRS § 10439-2.] 

1. Regulating issuance of securities by public utilities through Public 

Service Commissions-Annotations-41 A.L.R. 889; 39 A.L.R. 1517; 5 A.L.R. 36. 

2. State has control of issuance of stock certificates to stockholders of a public service company who subscribed before this act became effective. Op. 

Atty. Gen. Dec. 13, 1938.

1 

81.08.030 Authority to issue. A public service company may issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness payable on demand or at periods of more than twelve months after the date thereof, for the following purposes only: The acquisition of property, or construction, completion, extension, or improvement of its facilities, or the improvement or maintenance of its service, or discharge or refunding of its obligations, or the reimbursement of moneys actually expended from income or from any other moneys in the treasury of the company not secured by or obtained from the issue of stock or stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness of the company for any of the aforesaid purposes except maintenance of service, in cases where the applicant keeps its accounts and vouchers for such expenditures in such manner as to enable the commission to ascertain the amount of money so expended and the purpose for which the expenditure was made. [1937 c 30 § 1; 1933 c 151 § 3; RRS § 10439-3.] 

1. Issuance of stock dividend by public service company should be treated as any other issuance of securities and application must be made to Department containing full information on proposal. If Department is not satified with application, it should hold a hearing on the matter. Op. Atty. Gen. Oct. 

18, 1941. This opinion subsequently modified. Op. Atty. Gen. May 20, 1952. 

3. Conditional sales contract for purchase of equipment entered into by common carrier with bank is not "other evidence of indebtedness," and carrier is not required to apply to the Commission for permission to make such a contract. Op. Atty. Gen. May 10, 1950. 

81.08.040 Application for authority-Hearing-Joint action. Application for authority to issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness shall be made to the commission, stating the amount, character, terms, and purpose of each proposed issue, and stating such other pertinent details as the commission may require. 

To enable it to determine whether it will grant the application, the commission may hold a hearing and may make such additional investigation, and examine such witnesses, books, papers, documents, and contracts, and require the filing of such data as it may deem of assistance. The commission may by its order grant permission for the issuance of the stock or stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, in the amount applied for, or in a lesser amount, or not at all, and may attach to the exercise of its permission such conditions as it may deem reasonable and necessary. 

If an agency is empowered by another state to regulate and control the amount and character of securities to be issued by any public service company within such other state, the commission may agree with such agency on the issuance of stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes

or other evidence of indebtedness, by a public service company owning or operating a public utility both in such state and in this state, and may approve such issue jointly with such other agency and issue a joint order of approval: Provided, That no joint approval shall be required in order to express the approval of such issue by this state if the issue is separately approved by the commission. 

The company making the application may have the order of the commission reviewed in the courts in the same manner and by the same procedure as any other order of the commission. [1933 c 151 

§ 4; RRS § 10439-4.] 

81.08.050 Use of proceeds limited. No public service company shall, without the consent of the commission, apply the issue of any stock certificate or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bond, note or other evidence of indebtedness, or any part thereof, or any proceeds thereof, to any purpose not specified in the commission's order, or to any purpose specified in the commission's order in excess of the amount authorized for such purpose, or issue or dispose of them on any terms less favorable than those specified in the order, or a modification thereof. [1933 c 151 § 5; RRS § 10439-5.] 

81.08.060 Short term notes excepted. A public service company may issue notes, except demand notes, for proper purposes and not in violation of any provision of this chapter, or any other law, payable at periods of not more than twelve months after the date of issuance, without the consent of the commission, but no such note shall, in whole or in part, be refunded by any issue of stock or stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bonds, notes, or other evidence of indebtedness, without the consent of the commission. [1937 c 30 § 2, part; 1933 c 151 § 6; RRS § 10439-6, part.] 

81.08.070 Fee schedule. Each public service company making application to the commission for authority to issue stock and stock certificates or other evidence of interest or ownership and bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, shall pay to the commission the following fees: For each order authorizing an issue of bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness, one dollar for each one thousand dollars of the principal amount of the authorized issue or fraction thereof up to one million dollars and fifty cents for each one thousand dollars over one million dollars and up to ten million dollars, and ten cents for each one thousand dollars over ten million dollars, with a minimum fee in any case of ten dollars; for each order authorizing an issue of stock, stock certificates, or other evidence of interest or ownership, one dollar for each one thousand dollars of the par or stated value of the authorized issue or fraction thereof up to one million dollars, and fifty cents for each one thousand dollars over one million dollars and up to ten million dollars, and ten cents for each one thousand dollars over ten million dollars,

20 Der with a minimum fee in any case of ten dollars: Provided, That only twenty-five percent of the specified fees need be paid on any issue or on such portion thereof as may be used to guarantee, take over, refund, or discharge any stock issue or stock certificates, bonds, notes, or other evidence of interest, ownership, or indebtedness on which a fee has theretofore been paid or on authorizations of notes issued to banks, loaning institutions, or affiliated interests: Provided further, That if the commission modifies the amount of the issue requested and the applicant elects not to avail itself of the authorization, no fee need be paid. All fees collected under this section shall be paid at least once each month to the state treasurer and deposited in the public service revolving fund. [1937 c 30 § 2, part; 1933 c 151 § 6; 

RRS § 10439-6, part.] 

1. Loans from agency of Federal Government (REA) are covered by this act if public service company gives notes for the indebtedness. Op. Atty. Gen. 

July 1, 1937. 

2. Public service company which paid fee prescribed on application for authority to refund securities and was then unable to do so, cannot be credited on subsequent application with fees paid on former application-yet company need pay only 25% of fee in second application on all securities for which authority was given in prior application and fee paid. Op. Atty. Gen. Jan. 4, 1940. 

3. Bus company mortgaging its certificate of convenience and necessity must, on securing order of approval from Department, pay fee under this section. Op. Atty. Gen. July 7, 1942. 

4. Where a public service company received authorization for issuance of bonds and then secured a supplemental order authorizing change in interest, and finally an order cancelling the original order and authorizing issuance of notes to banks, the Department should not charge the statutory fee again for the supplemental order. Department also need not refund any of the fee because of the 25% proviso as to notes issued to banks. Op. Atty. Gen. April 

5, 1943. 

5. Where a public service company, with the approval of the Department, issues a note to an insurance company in return for a loan, and the insurance company's business is such that it may be considered a "loaning institution," the proviso of this section allowing payment of only 25% of the fee specified in the schedule applies. Op. Atty. Gen. Aug. 29, 1949. 

6. Loan to a public service company by a mutual life insurance company comes within the first proviso, and public service company need pay only 25% of the fee prescribed. Op. Atty. Gen. Aug. 29, 1949. 

81.08.080 Capitalization of franchises or merger contracts prohibited. The commission shall not authorize the capitalization of the right to be a corporation, or the capitalization of any franchise or permit whatsoever, or the right to own, operate, or enjoy any such franchise or permit, in excess of the amount, exclusive of any tax or annual charge, actually paid to the state or to a political subdivision thereof as the consideration for the grant of the franchise, permit, or right; nor shall any contract for consolidation or lease be capitalized, nor shall any public service company hereafter issue any bonds, notes or other evidence of indebtedness against or as a lien upon any contract for consolidation or merger. [1933 c 151 § 7; RRS § 10439-7.] 

1. This section does not prohibit mortgaging of bus certificate of convenience and necessity for value in excess of $25 (amount of fee paid for cer-

tificate application) even though mortgage includes no other property. Op. 

Atty. Gen. July 7, 1942. 

81.08.090 Accounting for disposition of proceeds. The commission may require public service companies to account for the disposition of the proceeds of all sales of evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, in such form and detail as it deems advisable, and establish such rules and regulations as it deems reasonable and necessary to insure the disposition of the proceeds for the purposes specified in its order. [1933 c 151 § 8; RRS § 10439-8.] 

81.08.100 Unauthorized and nonconforming issues void. Every evidence of interest or ownership, and every evidence of indebtedness, of a public service company, issued without an authorizing order of the commission shall be void, and every evidence of interest or ownership, and every evidence of indebtedness, of a company, comply with the terms of the order and no defect in, or in connection issued with the authorization of the commission, but not conforming with, the application for or issuance of such order shall render void in substance in its provisions to the requirements of the order of authorization, shall be void; but no failure in any other respect to any such evidence of interest or ownership, or any such evidence of indebtedness, except as to a person taking it otherwise than in good faith and for value and without actual notice. [1933 c 151 § 9; RRS § 10439-9.] 

81.08.110 Penalty against companies. Every public service company which issues or causes to be issued any evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, in nonconformity with the order of the commission authorizing it, or contrary to the provisions of this chapter, or which applies any part of the proceeds from the sale thereof to any purpose other than the purposes specified in the order, or to any purpose specified in the order in excess of the amount authorized for such purpose, shall be subject to a penalty of not more than one thousand dollars for each offense. Every violation of any order, rule, or requirement of the commission, or of any provision of this chapter, shall be a separate offense and in case of a continuing violation every day's continuance thereof shall be a separate offense.

The act, omission, or failure of any officer, agent, or employee of any company acting within the scope of his official duties or employment shall be the act, omission, or failure of the company. [1933 

c 151 § 11; RRS § 10439-11.] 

81.08.120 Penalty against individuals. Every officer, agent, or employee of a public service company, and every other person who knowingly authorizes, directs, aids in, issues or executes, or causes to be issued or executed, any evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, in nonconformity with the order of the

commission authorizing it, or contrary to the provisions of this chapter, or who, in any proceedings before the commission, knowingly makes any false statement or representation or with knowledge of its falsity files or causes to be filed with the commission any false statement or representation, which may tend to influence the commission to make an order authorizing the issuance of any such evidence of interest or ownership, or any such evidence of indebtedness, or which results in procuring from the commission any such order, or who, with knowledge that any false statement or representation was made to the commission in any proceedings tending in any way to influence the commission to make such order, issues or executes or negotiates, or causes to be issued, executed, or negotiated any such evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, or who, directly or indirectly, knowingly applies, or causes or assists to be applied the proceeds or any part thereof, from the sale of any such evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, to any purpose not specified in the commission's order, or to any purpose specified in the commission's order in excess of the amount authorized for such purpose, or who, with knowledge that any such evidence of interest or ownership, or evidence of indebtedness, has been issued or executed in violation of any of the provisions of this chapter, negotiates, or causes it to be negotiated, shall be guilty of a gross misdemeanor. [1933 c 151 § 12; RRS § 10439-12.] 

81.08.130 Permit to assume liability as guarantor, etc. No public service company shall henceforth assume any obligation or liability as guarantor, endorser, surety, or otherwise, in respect to the securities of any other person when such securities are payable at periods of more than twelve months after the date thereof, without having first secured from the commission an order authorizing it so to do. 

Every such assumption made other than in accordance with the order of the commission is void. [1933 c 151 § 13; RRS § 10439-13.] 

81.08.140 State not obligated. No provision of this chapter, and no deed or act done or performed under or in connection therewith, shall obligate the state to pay or guarantee any stock or stock certificate or other evidence of interest or ownership, or bond, note or other evidence of indebtedness, authorized, issued, or executed un der the provisions of this chapter. [1933 c 151 § 14; RRS § 10439-14.]

Sections Chapter 81.12 TRANSFERS OF PROPERTY 

81.12.010

Definition. 

81.12.020 Order required to sell, merge, etc.

81.12.010 Definition. The term "public service company," as used in this chapter, shall mean every company now or hereafter engaged in business in this state as a public utility and subject to regulation as to rates and service by the public service commission: Provided, That it shall not include common carriers subject to regulation by the Interstate Commerce Commission. [1941 c 159 § 1; Rem. Supp. 

1941 § 10440a.] 

81.12.020 Order required to sell, merge, etc. No public service company shall sell, lease, assign, or otherwise dispose of the whole or any part of its franchises, properties, or facilities, which are necessary or useful in the performance of its duties to the public, and no public service company shall, by any means whatsoever, directly or indirectly, merge or consolidate any of its franchises, properties, or facilities with any other public service company without having secured from the commission an order authorizing it so to do. [1945 

c 75 § 1; 1941 c 159 § 2; Rem. Supp. 1945 § 10440b.] 

1. Public service company purchasing all capital stock of a non-public service company need not apply for permission of Department. Op. Atty. 

Gen. Nov. 6, 1942. 

2. Stockholder of public service company wishing to transfer his stock to another person is not required to obtain authorization of the Commission to make the transfer. Op. Atty. Gen. Nov. 25, 1949. 

81.12.030 Disposal without authorization void. Any such sale, lease, assignment, or other disposal, merger or consolidation, made without authority of the commission is void. [1941 c 159 § 3; Rem. 

Supp. 1941 § 10440c.] 

81.12.040 Authority required to acquire property or securities of company. No public service company shall, directly or indirectly, purchase, acquire, or become the owner of any of the franchises, properties, facilities, capital stocks, or bonds of any other public service company unless authorized so to do by the commission. Nothing contained in this chapter shall prevent the holding of stocks or other securities heretofore lawfully acquired or prohibit, upon the sur-

render or exchange of said stocks or other securities pursuant to a reorganization plan, the purchase, acquisition, taking, or holding by the owner of a proportionate amount of the stocks or other securities of any new corporation organized to take over at foreclosure or other sale, the property of the corporation the stocks or securities of which have been thus surrendered or exchanged. Any contract by any public service company for the purchase, acquisition, assignment, or transfer to it of any of the stocks or other securities of any other public service company, directly or indirectly, without the approval of the commission shall be void and of no effect. [1941 

c 159 § 4; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440d.] 

81.12.050 Rules and regulations. The commission may promulgate rules and regulations to make effective the provisions of this chapter. [1941 c 159 § 5; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440e.] 

81.12.060 Penalty. A violation of, or failure to comply with, any provision of this chapter, or any order or rules issued by the commission hereunder, by any public service company or its officers, agents, or employees is a gross misdemeanor and each day's continuance of such violation or failure to comply shall constitute a separate offense. 

[1941 c 159 § 6; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440f.]

Chapter 81.16

AFFILIATED INTERESTS 

81.16.010 Definitions. 

81.16.020 Dealings with affiliated interests must be approved. 

81.16.030 Payments to affiliated interest disallowed if not reasonable. 

81.16.040 Satisfactory proof, what constitutes. 

81.16.050 Commission's control is continuing. 

81.16.060 Summary order on nonapproved payments. 

81.16.070 Summary order on payments after disallowance. 

81.16.080 Court action to enforce orders. 81.16.090 

Review of orders. 

81.16.010 Definitions. As used in this chapter, the term "public service company" shall include every corporation engaged in business as a public utility and subject to regulation as to rates and service by the public service commission. 

When used in this chapter, the term "affiliated interest" means: 

Every corporation and person owning or holding directly or indirectly five percent or more of the voting securities of any public service company engaged in any intrastate business in this state; 

Every corporation and person, other than those above specified, in

any chain of successive ownership of five percent or more of voting securities, the chain beginning with the holder of the voting securities of such public service company; 

Every corporation five percent or more of whose voting securities are owned by any person or corporation owning five percent or more of the voting securities of such public service company or by any person or corporation in any such chain of successive ownership of five percent or more of voting securities;

Every corporation or person with which the public service company has a management or service contract; and 

Every person who is an officer or director of such public service company or of any corporation in any chain of successive ownership of five percent or more of voting securities. [1933 c 152 § 1, part;

RRS § 10440-1, part.] 

81.16.020 Dealings with affiliated interests must be approved.

No contract or arrangement providing for the furnishing of management, supervisory, construction, engineering, accounting, legal, financial, or similar services, and no contract or arrangement for the purchase, sale, lease, or exchange of any property, right, or thing, or for the furnishing of any service, property, right, or thing, other than those above enumerated made or entered into between a public service company and any affiliated interest, including open account advances from or to such affiliated interest, except open account advances from or to a common carrier subject to the provisions of part one of the interstate commerce act, shall be valid until the contract or arrangement has received the approval of the commission. Every public service company shall file with the commission a verified copy or a verified summary of any such unwritten contract or arrangement. The commission shall approve such contract or arrangement only if it clearly appears upon investigation that it is reasonable and consistent with the public interest. The commission shall not be required to approve any such contract or arrangement unless satisfactory proof is submitted to it of the cost to the affiliated interest of rendering the services or of furnishing the property or service described herein. [1941 c 160 § 1, part; 1933 c 152 

§ 2; Rem. Supp. 1941 § 10440-2, part.] 

81.16.030 Payments to affiliated interest disallowed if not reasonable. In any proceeding involving the rates or practices of any public service company, the commission may exclude from the accounts of the company any payment to an affiliated interest for any services rendered or property or service furnished, as above described, under existing contracts or arrangements with the affiliated interest, unless the company establishes the reasonableness of such payment. The commission shall disallow such payment, in whole or in part, in the absence of satisfactory proof that it is reasonable

in amount, or unless satisfactory proof is submitted of the cost to the affiliated interest of rendering the service or furnishing the property or service. [1933 c 152 § 3; RRS § 10440-3.] 

1. In fixing a telephone company's rates, the Department acted arbitrarily in disallowing all payments by the company under a license contract with an affiliated company, and, as a matter of law, committed error. While the Department was not required to approve the company's agreement to pay a certain percentage to its parent company in license fees, it should have allowed such portion thereof as appears to be reasonable payment for the services rendered. State ex rel. Pacific Tel. & Tel. Co. v. Dept. of Public Service, 19 

Wn. (2d) 200 (1944). 

81.16.040 Satisfactory proof, what constitutes. No proof shall be satisfactory, within the meaning of RCW 81.16.010 to 81.16.030, unless it includes the original or verified copies of the relevant cost records and other relevant accounts of the affiliated interest, or such abstract thereof, as the commission may deem adequate, properly identified and authenticated: Provided, That the commission may, where reasonable, approve or disapprove such contracts or arrangements without the submission of cost records or accounts. [1933 c 152 § 4; RRS § 10440-4.] 

81.16.050 Commission's control is continuing. The commission shall have continuing supervisory control over the terms and conditions of such contracts and arrangements in so far as necessary to protect and promote the public interest. It shall have the same jurisdiction over the modification or amendment of such contracts or arrangements as it has over original contracts or arrangements. 

The fact that the commission has approved entry into a contract or arrangement shall not preclude disallowance of payments made pursuant thereto if upon actual experience under such contract or arrangement, it appears that the payments provided for or made are unreasonable. Every order of the commission approving any such contract or arrangement shall be conditioned upon the reserved power of the commission to revise and amend the terms thereof, if, and when necessary to protect and promote the public interest. [1933 

c 152 § 5; RRS § 10440-5.] 

81.16.060 Summary order on nonapproved payments. When the commission finds that any public service company is giving effect to any such contract or arrangement without its having received the commission's approval the commission may issue a summary order prohibiting the public service company from treating any payments made under the terms of such contract or arrangement as operating expenses or as capital expenditures for rate or valuation purposes, until such payments have received the approval of the commission. 

[1933 c 152 § 6; RRS § 10440-6.] 

81.16.070 Summary order on payments after disallowance. When the commission finds that any public service company is making payments to an affiliated interest, notwithstanding such payments

have been disallowed by the commission in a proceeding involving the company's rates or practices, the commission shall issue a summary order prohibiting the company from treating the payments as operating expenses or capital expenditures for rate or valuation purposes, until they have received the approval of the commission. 

[1933 c 152 § 7; RRS § 10440-7.] 

81.16.080 Court action to enforce orders. The superior court of 

Thurston county may enforce such orders to cease and desist by appropriate process, including the issuance of a preliminary injunction upon the suit of the commission. [1933 c 152 § 8; RRS § 10440-8.] 

81.16.090 Review of orders. Any public service company or affiliated interest deeming any decision or order of the commission to be in any respect improper, unjust or unreasonable may have the same reviewed in court in the same manner and by the same procedure as is provided for review of any other order of the commis sion. [1933 c 152 § 9; RRS § 10440-9.] 

Chapter 81.20

INVESTIGATION OF PUBLIC SERVICE COMPANIES Sections 

81.20.010 Definition. 

81.20.020 Cost of investigation may be assessed against company. 

81.20.030 Interest on unpaid assessment-Action to collect. 

81.20.040 Commission's determination of necessity as evidence. 

81.20.050 Order of commission not subject to review. 

81.20.060 Limitation on frequency of investigations. 

81.20.010 Definition. As used in this chapter, the term "public service company" means any person, firm, association, or corporation, whether public or private, operating a utility or public service enterprise subject in any respect to regulation by the public service commission. [1939 c 203 § 1; RRS § 10458-6.] 

81.20.020 Cost of investigation may be assessed against company.

Whenever the commission in any proceeding deems it necessary in order to carry out the duties imposed upon it by law to investigate the books, accounts, practices, and activities of, or make any valuation or appraisal of the property of any public service company, or to investigate or appraise any phase of its operations, or to render any engineering or accounting service to or in connection with any such company, and the cost thereof to the commission exceeds in amount the ordinary regulatory fees paid by such company during the preceding calendar year or estimated to be paid during the current year, whichever is more, such company shall pay the expenses reasonably attributable and allocable to such investigation, valua-

1 tion, appraisal, or services. The commission shall ascertain such expenses, and, after giving notice and an opportunity to be heard, shall render a bill therefor by registered mail to the company, either at the conclusion of the investigation, valuation, appraisal, or services, or from time to time during its progress. Within thirty days after a bill has been mailed, the company shall pay to the commission the amount of the bill, and the commission shall transmit the payment to the state treasurer who shall credit it to the public service revolving fund. The total amount which any public service company shall be required to pay in any calendar year shall not exceed one percent of the gross operating revenues derived by such company from its intrastate operations during the last preceding calendar year. If the company did not operate during all of the preceding year the calculations shall be based upon estimated gross revenue for the current year. [1939 c 203 § 2(a); RRS § 10458-6a (a).] 

1. Equal protection clauses of both federal and state constitutions are violated by section 12, chapter 165, Laws of 1933, which provided for investigation of public service companies by the Department with power to charge the cost of investigation in whole or in part to the companies without any fixed standards as to limits on amounts chargeable or frequency of investigation, thus permitting and inviting discrimination and inequality. State v. Northwestern Electric Co., 183 Wash. 184 (1935). 

2. This section relates only to expenses of investigation by the Department and not to costs of defending the Department in court proceedings resulting from a Departmental order; hence, legal fees and other expenses of the Attorney General cannot be recovered from the public service company. 

State v. Pacific Tel. & Tel. Co., 27 Wn. (2d) 893 (1947). 

3. Department has authority to conduct investigation to see if donations by public service company have been improperly recorded as operating expenses, although otherwise what such company does with its profit is immaterial to the Department. Op. Atty. Gen. Sept. 20, 1940. 

4. Whenever there is a complete and extraordinary investigation of a public service company as contemplated by chapter 203, Laws of 1939, and the cost of that investigation exceeds the amount of fees paid by the company during the preceding year under chapter 123, Laws of 1939, or estimated to be paid during the current year, the entire cost of such investigation should be charged directly against the company and the amount of annual fees paid by that company cannot be deducted from the charges accruing under this section. 

Op. Atty. Gen. May 6, 1941. 

5. An investigation by the Department of the books and plant of a public service company, unconnected with "any proceeding" and unaccompanied by any formal notice to the company, cannot be recovered from the utility under this section. Op. Atty. Gen. April 28, 1942. 

6. Cost of hiring special attorney to assist Department in litigation is properly payable out of Public Service Revolving Fund. Op. Atty. Gen. 

July 7, 1944. 

81.20.030 Interest on unpaid assessment - Action to collect.

Amounts so assessed against any public service company not paid within thirty days after mailing of the bill therefor, shall draw interest at the rate of six percent per year from the date of mailing of the bill. Upon failure of the company to pay the bill, the attorney general shall proceed against the company by civil action in the name of the state in the superior court of Thurston county to collect

the amount due, together with interest and costs of suit.

[1939 c 203 

§ 2(b); RRS § 10458-6a (b).] 

81.20.040 Commission's determination of necessity as evidence. 

In such action the commission's determination of the necessity of the investigation, valuation, appraisal, or services shall be conclusive evidence of such necessity, and its findings and determination of facts expressed in bills rendered pursuant to this chapter or in any proceedings determinative of such bills shall be prima facie evidence of such facts. [1939 c 203 § 2(c); RRS § 10458-6a (c).] 

81.20.050 Order of commission not subject to review. In view of the civil action provided for in this chapter any order made by the commission in determining the amount of such bill shall not be reviewable in court, but the mere absence of such right of review shall not prejudice the rights of defendants in the civil action. [1939 c 203 

§ 2(d); RRS § 10458-6a (d).] 

81.20.060 Limitation on frequency of investigations. Expenses of a complete valuation, rate, and service investigation shall not be assessed against a public service company if it has been subjected to and has paid the expenses of a complete valuation, rate, and service investigation during the preceding five years, unless the properties or operations of the company have materially changed or there has been a substantial change in its value for rate making purposes or in other circumstances and conditions affecting rates and services. 

[1939 c 203 § 2(e); RRS § 10458-6a (e).] Sections 

81.24.010 

Chapter 81.24

REGULATORY FEES 

Companies to file reports of gross revenue and pay fees -General. 

81.24.020 Fees of auto transportation companies. 

81.24.030 Fees of steamboat companies, wharfingers or warehousemen. 

81.24.040 Fees of storage warehousemen. 

81.24.050 Fees to approximate reasonable cost of regulation. 

81.24.060 Regulatory cost records to be kept by commission. 

81.24.070 Disposition of fees. 

81.24.080 Penalty for failure to pay fees. 

81.24.010 Companies to file reports of gross revenue and pay fees -General. Every company subject to regulation by the commission, except auto transportation companies, steamboat companies, wharfingers or warehousemen, motor freight carriers, and storage warehousemen shall, on or before the first day of April of each year, file

with the commission a statement on oath showing its gross operating revenue from intrastate operations for the preceding calendar year, or portion thereof, and pay to the commission a fee equal to onetenth of one percent of the first fifty thousand dollars of gross operating revenue, plus two-tenths of one percent of any gross operating revenue in excess of fifty thousand dollars: Provided, That the fee shall in no case be less than one dollar. 

The percentage rates of gross operating revenue to be paid in any year may be decreased by the commission for any class of companies subject to the payment of such fees, by general order entered before March 1st of such year, and for such purpose such companies shall be classified as follows: Railroad, street railroad, express, sleeping car, and toll bridge companies shall constitute class one. Every other company subject to regulation by the commission, for which regulatory fees are not otherwise fixed by law shall pay fees as herein provided and shall constitute additional classes according to kinds of businesses engaged in. [1939 c 123 § 1, part; 1937 c 158 § 1; RRS § 10417, part.]

Annual license fee of public service companies: RCW 23.28.050.

Gross weight fees of motor freight carriers: RCW 81.80.320. 

Mileage fees on auto stages: RCW 46.16.125. 

1. Fees based on gross operating revenues not an unconstitutional burden on interstate commerce provided amount of fees reasonably agrees with cost of supervision, but where charges against fund were not properly segregated, burden of proving reasonableness shifts to state. Great Northern Ry. Co. v. 

State, 184 Wash. 648. Reversed on facts in 300 U. S. 154, 57 Sup. Ct. 397, 81 

L. Ed. 573 (1935). 

2. Overpayment of filing fee cannot be credited upon next year's payment. 

Op. Atty. Gen. April 1, 1933. 

3. Department not obligated to collect gross revenue fees from intracity bus companies for years prior to 1937 even though Supreme Court held in 

Spokane United Railways v. Dept. of Public Service, 191 Wash. 595, that such companies were common carriers subject to regulation under Laws of 1911, since Department, on advice of Attorney General in 1926, never regulated such companies and therefore incurred no expenses incident to regulation. 

Op. Atty. Gen. Oct. 21, 1937. 

4. Where one group of utilities out of a class established by law for purposes of regulation does not pay fees sufficient to cover cost of regulation, the Department must continue its statutory duties of regulation, and may in its discretion either await reduction of the deficit by future years' fees or apply to the next legislature for an increase in the fees. Op. Atty. Gen. April 15, 1940. 

5. This section was not intended to operate retroactively. The fee paid by a company based on its gross revenue the preceding year is for the Department's expenses during the current year. So where a company went out of business in May, it was not required to pay in the next year any fees based on revenues up to May. The Department is not required to refund any portion of the last fee paid. Op. Atty. Gen. June 28, 1945. 

81.24.020 Fees of auto transportation companies. Every auto transportation company shall, between the first and fifteenth days of January, April, July and October of each year, file with the commission a statement showing its gross operating revenue from intrastate operations for the preceding three months, or portion thereof, and

pay to the commission a fee of two-fifths of one percent of the amount of gross operating revenue: Provided, That the fee paid shall in no case be less than two dollars and fifty cents. 

The percentage rate of gross operating revenue to be paid in any period may be decreased by the commission by general order entered before the fifteenth day of the month preceding the month in which such fees are due. [1937 c 158 § 2, part; RRS § 10417-1, part.] 

1. Question of whether Department should collect minimum fee from a company which has not closed its books or earned any revenues for three years is one of fact (whether company is actually operating) for the Department to determine. Op. Atty. Gen. May 6, 1924. 

2. Bus companies need not pay fees on revenues derived from interstate operations, but only on revenues from intrastate operation. Op. Atty. Gen. 

June 15, 1939. 

81.24.030 Fees of steamboat companies, wharfingers or warehousemen. Every steamboat company and every wharfinger or warehouseman shall, on or before the first day of April of each year, file with the commission a statement on oath showing its gross operating revenue from intrastate operations for the preceding calendar year, or portion thereof, and pay to the commission a fee of two-fifths of one percent of the amount of gross operating revenue: 

Provided, That the fee so paid shall in no case be less than five dollars. The percentage rate of gross operating revenue to be paid in any year may be decreased by the commission by general order entered before March 1st of such year. [1939 c 123 § 3, part; 1937 

c 158 § 4; RRS § 10417-3, part.] 

1. Warehouse near docks used as storage for water shipments is within the jurisdiction of the Commission. Op. Atty. Gen. 1917-18, p. 281. 

2. Act is not applicable to interstate commerce and revenue derived from interstate commerce is not subject to the fee. 

A dock may be a facility of interstate commerce, though the service rendered is entirely within the state. Op. Atty. Gen. Jan. 5, 1922. 

81.24.040 Fees of storage warehousemen. Every storage warehouseman shall, on or before the thirty-first day of March, 1950, and of each year thereafter, file with the commission an annual report under oath, on forms to be provided by the commission, showing his gross operating revenue from intrastate operations for the preceding calendar year ending December 31st, or portion thereof, and pay to the commission one percent of such gross operating revenue: Provided, That the fee so paid shall in no case be less than ten dollars: 

Provided further, That for the year 1950 the amount yet remaining due shall be computed to give credit for amounts paid during that year. The percentage rate of gross operating revenue to be paid in any year may be decreased by the commission by general order entered before March 1st of such year. [1949 c 124 § 1, part; 1939 

c 123 § 2, part; 1937 c 158 § 3; Rem. Supp. 1949 § 10417-2, part.] 

1. Department may waive in toto for a definite period of time the fee of a storage warehouseman. Op. Atty. Gen. June 26, 1940.

81.24.050 Fees to approximate reasonable cost of regulation. In fixing the percentage rates of gross operating revenue to be paid by companies under RCW 81.24.010, 81.24.020, 81.24.030 and 81.24.040, the commission shall consider all moneys then in the public service revolving fund and the fees currently to be paid into such fund, to the end that the fees collected from the companies, or classes of companies, covered by each respective section shall be approximately the same as the reasonable cost of supervising and regulating such companies, or classes of companies, respectively. [(i) 1939 c 123 § 1; 

1937 c 158 § 1; RRS § 10417, part. (ii) 1937 c 158 § 2, part; RRS § 10417-1, part. (iii) 1939 c 123 § 3, part; 1937 c 158 § 4; RRS § 10417-3, part. (iv) 1939 c 123 § 2, part; 1937 c 158 § 3; RRS § 10417-2, part. 

(v) 1949 c 124 § 1, part; Rem. Supp. 1949 § 10417-2, part.] 

81.24.060 Regulatory cost records to be kept by commission. The commission shall keep accurate records of the costs incurred in regulating and supervising the several groups of companies subject to regulation or supervision and such records shall be open to inspection by all interested parties. The records and data upon which the commission's determination is made shall be considered prima facie correct in any proceeding instituted to challenge the reasonableness or correctness of any order of the commission fixing fees and distrib uting regulatory expenses. [1937 c 158 § 7; RRS § 10417-5.] 

1. Where one kind of utility out of a class established by law for purposes of regulation does not pay fees sufficient to cover cost of regulation, the Department must continue its statutory duties of regulation and may, in its discretion, either await reduction of the particular deficit by future years' fees or apply to next legislature for increase in amount of fees. Op. Atty. Gen. 

April 15, 1940. 

2. See Great Northern Ry. v. State, 300 U. S. 154, 81 L. Ed. 573 (1937). 

3. If the allocation of general departmental administrative overhead costs on the basis of gross operating revenues derived from intrastate operation produces a result which, in the language of the statute, is reasonable and does, in fact, approximate the cost of regulating any given utility or class of utilities, such basis would be proper and legal. Op. Atty. Gen. June 29, 1943. 

81.24.070 Disposition of fees. All moneys collected under the provisions of this chapter shall within thirty days after their receipt be paid to the state treasurer who shall deposit them in a special fund in the state treasury, hereby created, to be designated the public service revolving fund. [(i) 1949 c 117 § 11; Rem. Supp. 1949 § 10964115-11. (ii) 1937 c 158 § 6; RRS § 10417-4. (iii) 1945 c 267 § 10, part; 

Rem. Supp. 1945 § 10459-10, part.] 

Public service revolving fund: RCW 43.53.090. 

81.24.080 Penalty for failure to pay fees. Every company, or the officers, agents, or employees thereof, failing or neglecting to pay the fees herein required shall be guilty of a misdemeanor, and in addition thereto shall be subject to a penalty of twenty-five dollars for each and every day that the fee remains unpaid after it becomes

due; the penalty to be collected by the commission in a civil action. 

All fines and penalties collected under the provisions of this chapter shall be deposited in the public service revolving fund of the state treasury. [1923 c 107 § 2; 1921 c 113 § 3; RRS § 10419.] STAGE MILEAGE FEE COLLECTION 

46.16.125 Mileage fees on stages. In addition to the fees required by RCW 46.16.120, operators of auto stages with seating capacity over six shall pay quarterly, at the time they file gross earning returns with the public service commission, the sum of fifteen cents for each one hundred vehicle miles operated by each auto stage over the public highways of this state: Provided, That in the case of each auto stage propelled by steam, electricity, natural gas, diesel oil, butane or propane, the payment required hereunder shall be twenty cents per one hundred miles of such operation. The commission shall transmit all such sums so collected to the state treasurer, who shall deposit the same in the motor vehicle fund. Any person failing to make any payment required by this section shall be subject to a penalty of one hundred per cent of the payment due hereunder, in addition to any penalty provided for failure to submit a quarterly report. Any penalties so collected shall be credited to the public service revolving fund. [1951 c 269 § 14.]
