81.44.010 Commission may order improved facilities. Whenever the commission, after hearing had upon its own motion or upon complaint, finds that additional tracks, switches, terminals, terminal facilities, stations, motive power or any other property, apparatus, equipment, facilities, or devices for use by any common carrier in, or in connection with the transportation of person or property, ought reasonably to be provided, or any repairs or improvements to, or changes in, any theretofore in use ought reasonably to be made, or any additions or changes in construction should reasonably be made thereto, in order to promote the security or convenience of the public or employees, or in order to secure adequate service or facilities for the transportation of passengers or property, it may make and serve an order directing such repairs, improvements, changes, or additions to be made. [1911 c 117 § 64; RRS § 10400.] 

1. Order of district court affirmed by territorial Supreme Court for a peremptory mandamus compelling a railroad company to construct a station at 

Yakima―reversed. Northern Pac. Ry. Co. v. Territory, 142 U. S. 492, 35 L.

Ed. 1092 (1892). 

2. Order requiring railroad to furnish depot in small town with running water for drinking and flush toilets is unreasonable. 

Order requiring depot to be moved five hundred feet nearer center of small town, held unreasonable. State ex rel. G. N. Ry. Co. v. Railroad Comm., 60 

Wash. 218 (1910). 

3. Under this act, the power to fix the time within which a depot must be completed is conferred by necessary implication. State ex rel. Railroad Comm. v. Great Northern R. Co., 68 Wash. 257 (1912). 

4. Under this and other sections, the Commission was not authorized to change city street grades or exercise the power of eminent domain for the purpose of eliminating railroad grade crossings in a city. Spokane v. Spokane & I. E. R. Co., 75 Wash. 651 (1913). 

5. Injunction does not lie to prevent a street railway company from abandoning that part of its road lying between two stations served by a competing road lying closely parallel, merely because the abandonment necessarily results in depriving all persons to whom both roads are accessible of competitive service; since the whole theory of the public service law is opposed to the idea that the public will be better served with two closely parallel lines of road where one road will amply suffice. Day v. Tacoma R. & Power Co., 80 Wash. 

161; L.R.A. 1915B 547 (1914). 

6. An order of the Department changing the name of a railway station serving two nearby villages or towns, so as to incorporate the names of both, hyphenated, is proper when that appears to best serve the interests of both communities, eliminates confusions, and offers the most equitable and just solution of the controversy; the railroad company standing neutral. State ex rel. Bingen v. Dept. of Public Works, 164 Wash. 563 (1931). 

7. Extension of line or building new line to new territory, power of Public 

Service Commission as to. 30 A.L.R. 73. 

8. Shops, power of Public Service Commission to regulate location and maintenance of. 36 A.L.R. 1495. 

9. Jurisdiction of Public Service Commission with respect to crossings in case of intersecting railroads by virtue of statute. 40 A.L.R. 742. 

10. Alteration or extension of passenger service, power of Public Service 

Commission in respect to. 70 A.L.R. 841.

11. Commission can, under a showing of certain circumstances, order a railroad company to change the name of one of its stations. Op. Atty. Gen. Oct. 

11, 1916. 

12. Duty of carrier and shipper in connection with cleaning and cooperage of cars for grain carriage. Op. Atty. Gen. Aug. 22, 1927. 

13. Commission has no power to order unification of railroad terminals in large city since expenses involved would amount to a burden on interstate commerce. Op. Atty. Gen. Dec. 12, 1928. 

14. Department of Public Service and the Department of Labor and Industries have overlapping jurisdiction in some instances relating to the safety of railroad equipment, but where the safety sought is for the traveling public, the jurisdiction is the Department of Public Service. Op. Atty. Gen. 

Feb. 17, 1947. 

81.44.020 Correction of unsafe or defective conditions. If the commission finds that the equipment, or appliances in connection therewith, or the apparatus, tracks, bridges, or other structures of any common carrier are defective, and that the operation thereof is dangerous to the employees of the carrier or to the public, it shall immediately give notice to the superintendent or other officer of the carrier of the repairs or reconstruction necessary to place the same in a safe condition, and may also prescribe the rate of speed for trains or cars passing over the dangerous or defective track, bridge, or other structure until the repairs or reconstruction required are made, and may also prescribe the time within which they shall be made. If, in its opinion, it is needful or proper, the commission may forbid the running of trains or cars over any defective track, bridge, or structure until it is repaired and placed in a safe condition. 

There shall be no appeal from or action to review any order of the commission made under the provisions of this section. [1911 

c 117 § 65; RRS § 10401.] 81.44.030 Safety appliances-Locomotives. Each railroad loco motive shall be equipped with power driving wheel brakes and appliances for operating the train brake system, so equipped that the engineer can control its speed, without requiring brakeman to use the common hand brakes for that purpose; with couplers coupling automatically by impact, which can be coupled or uncoupled without the necessity of men going between the locomotive and the locomotive or car to which the same is being coupled or from which it is being uncoupled; with proper flanges, sill steps and grab irons, or uncoupling levers in lieu of such grab irons; and, excepting such as may be assigned to daylight runs or switching service exclusively, with electric headlights of approved design and capacity, except that locomotives may be operated without such headlight upon order of the commission; with proper cocks, valves, pistons, valve stems, and appliances which will prevent the escape of steam in such volume as to obstruct the view of the engineman; and, in the case of locomotives used in the switching service, with proper foot boards and toe boards, and with headlight on each end, and with such other appli-

AAB ances, apparatus, and machinery necessary for safe operation of the locomotive or the train to which it is attached, as the commission may prescribe: Provided, That in case of emergency the commission may permit the use of road engines in switching service. [1911 c 117 

§ 66, part; RRS § 10402, part.] 

81.44.040 Safety appliances-Cars. Each car shall be equipped with couplers coupling automatically, which can be coupled or uncoupled without the necessity of men going between the ends of the cars; with power brakes; with proper hand brakes, sill steps and grab irons, and, where secure ladders and running boards are required, with such ladders and running boards, and all cars having ladders shall also be equipped with secure hand holds or grab irons. on their roofs at the tops of such ladders, and with such other appliances necessary for the safe operation of the cars, and the trains containing them, as may be prescribed by the commission: Provided, That in the loading and hauling of long commodities requiring more than one car, hand brakes may be omitted from all save one of the cars, while they are thus combined for such purpose: Provided further, That in the operation of trains, not less than eighty-five percent of the cars in the train, which are associated together, shall have their power brakes used and operated by the engineer. [1911 c 117 § 66, part; RRS § 10402, part.] 

81.44.050 Power of commission as to appliances. The commission shall designate the number, dimensions, location, and manner of application of the appliances of such locomotives and cars, or such as may be prescribed by it, and shall give notice of such designation to all railroad companies subject to the provisions hereof, by such means as it deems proper, and thereafter the number, dimensions, location, and manner of application so designated shall remain as the standards of equipment to be used on all cars and locomotives subject to the provisions hereof. The commission may change or modify said standards of equipment at any time or provide different standards under different circumstances and conditions. It shall be unlawful to use any car, motor, or locomotive which does not comply with the standards so prescribed: Provided, That when any car, motor, or locomotive has been properly equipped, and such equipment has become defective or insecure while being used, the car, motor, or locomotive may be hauled from the place where the equipment was first discovered to be defective or insecure to the nearest available point where such car, motor, or locomotive can be repaired, without liability for the penalties imposed herein if such movement is necessary to make such repairs, and they cannot reasonably be made except at such repair point. Nothing herein shall be construed to permit the hauling of defective cars by means of chains instead of drawbars in revenue trains, or in association with other cars that

are commercially used, unless such defective cars contain livestock or perishable freight. [1911 c 117 § 66, part; RRS § 10402, part.] 

81.44.060 Penalty. It shall be unlawful for any railroad company to use or operate any car, motor, locomotive, or train that is defective, or any car, motor, locomotive, or train upon which any appliance, machinery, or attachment thereto belonging is defective, or to knowingly operate its train over any defective track, bridge, or other structure, excepting in cases of emergency and under proper precautions: Provided, That this section shall not apply to boarding and outfit cars when moved as work trains, or to trains consisting wholly of logging trucks or of logging trucks and a passenger car or caboose at the rear end thereof, or of logging trucks and not to exceed five freight cars at the rear end thereof. [1911 c 117 § 66, part; RRS § 10402, part.] 

81.44.070 Duties of inspector as to railroads. The inspector of tracks, bridges, structures, and equipment, and his deputies shall inspect all equipment, and appliances connected therewith, and all apparatus, tracks, bridges, and structures, depots and facilities and accommodations connected therewith, and facilities and accommodations furnished for the use of employees, and make such reports of his inspection to the commission as may be required. He shall, on discovering any defective equipment or appliances connected therewith, rendering the use of such equipment dangerous, immediately report the same to the superintendent of the road on which it is found, and to the proper official at the nearest point where such defect is discovered, describing the defect. The inspector may, on the discovery of any defect rendering the use of any car, motor, or locomotive dangerous, condemn it and order it out of service until repaired and put in good working order. He shall, on discovering any track, bridge, or structure defective or unsafe in any particular, report such condition to the commission, and, in addition thereto, report it to the official in charge of the division of the railroad upon which the defect is found. In case any track, bridge, or structure is found so defective as to be dangerous to the employees or public for a train to be operated over the same, the inspector may condemn such track, bridge, or structure and notify the commission and the office in charge of the division of the railroad where the defect is found, of his action concerning the same, reporting in detail the defect complained of, and the work or improvements necessary to repair it. He shall also report to the commission the violation of any law affecting the conduct of public service companies in this state. 

The inspector, or his deputies may ride on any locomotive, or on the caboose of any freight train, for the purpose of inspecting the track of any railroad in the state: Provided, That the engineer or conductor in charge of any such locomotive or caboose may require

​330 the inspector to produce his authority, under the seal of the commission, showing that he is such inspector or deputy inspector. [1911 c 117 § 67, part; RRS § 10403, part.] 

81.44.080 Additional duties may be required. The inspector, or his deputies, when required by the commission, shall inspect any street railroad, gas plant, electrical plant, water system, telephone line, or telegraph line, and upon discovering any defective or dangerous track, bridge, structure, equipment, apparatus, machinery, appliance, facility, instrumentality, or building, rendering the use of it dangerous to the public or to the employees of the company owning or operating it, report the same to the commission and to the official in charge of the road, plant, system, or line. [1911 c 117 § 67, part; 

RRS § 10403, part.] 

81.44.085 First aid kits and drinking water. (1) Every person operating a common carrier railroad in this state shall equip each locomotive and caboose used in train or yard switching service, and every car used in passenger service with a first aid kit of a type to be approved by the director of labor and industries: Provided, That such kits shall not be required on equipment used exclusively in yard or switching service where such kits are maintained in the yard or terminal.

Each locomotive and caboose shall also be furnished with sanitary cups and sanitary ice-cooled drinking water. (2) For the purpose of this section a "locomotive" shall include all railroad engines propelled by any form of energy and used in rail line haul or yard switching service. (3) Any person violating any provision of this section shall be guilty of a misdemeanor. [1951 c 66 §§ 1, 2, 3.] 

81.44.090 Cabooses-Size-Equipment. It shall be unlawful for any person operating any railroad in this state to require or permit the use of any caboose cars, unless they are at least twenty-four feet in length, exclusive of platforms, and are provided with a door in each end thereof, and with suitable waterclosets, cupolas, platforms, guard rails, grab irons and steps for the safety of persons in alighting or getting on, and they shall be equipped with at least two four wheel trucks and an operative hand brake on each end: Provided,

That this section shall not apply to logging railways upon which passengers are not regularly carried for hire. [1909 c 31 § 1; RRS 

§ 10483.] 

81.44.100 Penalty. Any person violating any provisions of RCW 

81.44.090, shall, upon conviction thereof, be fined not less than five hundred dollars nor more than one thousand dollars for each offense. 

[1909 c 31 § 2; RRS § 10484.]

81.44.101 Track motor cars-Windshield and canopy required.

Every person, firm or corporation operating or controlling any railroad running through or within this state as a common carrier shall, on or before January 1, 1952, equip each of its track motor cars with: (1) A windshield and a device for wiping rain, snow and other moisture therefrom, which device shall be maintained in good order and so constructed as to be controlled or operated by the operator of said track motor car; (2) A canopy or top of such construction as to adequately protect the occupants thereof from the rays of the sun, rain, snow or other inclement weather. [1951 c 42 § 1.] 

81.44.102 -Absence of windshield or canopy unlawful. It shall be unlawful after January 1, 1952, for any person, firm or corporation, operating or controlling any common carrier railroad running through or within this state to operate or use any track motor car which is not equipped with a windshield and canopy or top as provided in RCW 81.44.101. [1951 c 42 § 2.] 81.44.103 -Head and tail lights required. Every person, firm or corporation operating or controlling any railroad running as a common carrier through or within the state shall, on or before January 1, 1952, equip each of its track motor cars used during the period from thirty minutes before sunset to thirty minutes after sunrise, with an electric headlight of such construction and with sufficient candle power to render plainly visible at a distance of not less than three hundred feet in advance of such track motor car, any track obstruction, landmark, warning sign or grade crossing, and further shall equip such track motor car with a red rear electric light of such construction and with sufficient candle power as to be plainly visible at a distance of three hundred feet. [1951 c 42 § 3.] 81.44.104 -Absence of lights unlawful. It shall be unlawful after January 1, 1952, for any person, firm or corporation operating or controlling any railroad running as a common carrier through or within this state to operate or use any track motor car from thirty minutes before sunset to thirty minutes after sunrise, which is not equipped with lights of the candle power, construction and utility described in RCW 81.44.103. [1951 c 42 § 4.] 81.44.105 -Penalty for violation. Every violation of RCW 

81.44.101 to 81.44.105 is a misdemeanor and shall be punishable by a fine of not more than one hundred dollars. [1951 c 42 § 5.] 

81.44.110 Equipment is part of cars-Tare weight. The stakes, standards, supports, stays, railings, and other equipment necessary to effectually and suitably equip and supply all flat cars, and cars belonging to a railroad company, or person engaged in the business of carrying for hire, shall be considered part and parcel of the cars, and

the weight thereof shall be added to the weight of the cars, and shall be deducted from the weight of the cargo, commodity, or product shipped on any and all such flat car or cars so that the freight charges shall be charged by the carrier only on the cargo, commodity, or product carried. [1907 c 218 § 1; RRS § 10470.] 

81.44.120 Reimbursement of shipper for supplying equipment.

If a railroad company, or person in the business of carrying for hire, furnishes flat cars, or cars, without stakes, standards, supports, stays, railings, or other equipment necessary to load and transport the cargo, and the shipper provides such stakes, standards, supports, stays, railings, or other necessary equipment, the carrier shall reimburse the shipper for the cost and expense thereof. [1907 c 218 

§ 2; RRS § 10473.] 

81.44.130 Frogs, switches and guard rails. Every railroad and street railroad company shall securely guard the frogs, switches, and guard rails on its tracks so as to protect and prevent the feet of any person being caught therein. [(i) 1911 c 117 § 68; RRS 

§ 10404. (ii) 1907 c 138 § 1, part; 1899 c 35 § 1; RRS § 10480, part.] 

1. Custom as affecting liability for injuries from unblocked frogs, etc. 68 

A.L.R. 1420. 

81.44.140 Liability for damage. Any person owning or operating a railroad shall be liable for any damage received from the failure to comply with the provisions of RCW 81.44.130. [1899 c 35 § 2; RRS § 10481.] 

81.44.150 Track scale-Testing. All railroads operating in this state shall provide suitable facilities for the testing of all track scales used by them. The commission after a hearing may upon its own motion and after notice to the railroads may order a suitable car, or other device, provided by the companies, to be used in testing the track scales used by them, the expense of providing such car, or device to be equitably and reasonably apportioned among the different railroad companies by the commission. The car or device shall be used by the commission to test the accuracy of all track scales, and the different railroad companies shall transport and move such car or device without charge, to the different places designated by the commission under such reasonable rules and regulations as the commission may prescribe. The car or device may be used in adjoining states to test the scales of railroad companies and for that purpose may be taken beyond the limits of the state under such reasonable rules and regulations for the due care and return thereof as the commission may prescribe. The commission may prescribe and collect a reasonable fee sufficient to

cover the cost and expenses connected therewith for the inspection and testing of all scales. [1911 c 117 § 19; RRS § 10355.] 

1. Under this section the Commission has no authority to use its scale testing equipment to test scales owned by private persons or corporations. Op. 

Atty. Gen. May 13, 1912. 

81.44.160 Regulations for weighing. The commission may enforce reasonable regulations for the weighing of cars and freight offered for shipment over any line of railroad, and test the weights made by any railroad and scales used in weighing freight on cars. 

[1911 c 117 § 60; RRS § 10396.] 

Duties of railroad relative to sidings, weighing facilities and police protection for handling agricultural commodities: Chapter 22.08. 

1. Duty of railway company to install stock scales at station. 10 A.L.R. 1339. 

Chapter 81.48

RAILROADS-OPERATING REQUIREMENTS AND Sections REGULATIONS

Excessive steam in boilers, penalty: 

RCW 70.54.080. 

81.48.010 Failure to ring bell-Penalty. Every engineer driving a locomotive on any railway who fails to ring the bell or sound the whistle upon such locomotive, or cause the same to be rung or sounded, at least eighty rods from any place where such railway crosses a traveled road or street on the same level, except in cities, or to continue the ringing of such bell or sounding of such whistle until such locomotive has crossed such road or street, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor. [1909 c 249 § 276; RRS § 2528.] 

81.48.020 Obstructing or delaying train-Penalty. Every person who wilfully obstructs, hinders, or delays the passage of any car lawfully operated upon any railway, shall be guilty of a misde meanor. [1909 c 249 § 278; RRS § 2530.] 

81.48.030 Speed within cities may be regulated. The right to fix and regulate the speed of railway trains within the limits of cities of the second, third, and fourth classes is vested exclusively in the commission: Provided, That this section shall not apply to street railways operated within said cities. [1943 c 228 § 1; Rem. Supp. 1943 § 10547-1.] 

1. This section revokes the power of cities and towns of second and third class to regulate speed of trains within their limits where the Commission has

prescribed limitations. Where Commission has not acted, ordinances in force before this act was passed may not be changed without approval of Commission. Ordinances passed subsequent to this section are of no effect. Op. Atty. 

Gen. June 12, 1946. 

81.48.040 Procedure to fix speed limits-Change in limits. After due investigation, the commission shall issue an order fixing the speed of railway trains within the limits of such cities. The commission may fix different rates of speed for different cities, commensurate with the hazard presented and the practical operation of the trains. It may, from time to time, as conditions change, either increase or decrease such speed limits. [1943 c 228 § 2; Rem. Supp. 1943 § 10547-2.] 

81.48.050 Trains to stop at railroad crossings. All railroads and street railroads, shall cause their trains and cars to come to a full stop at a distance not greater than five hundred feet before crossing the tracks of another railroad crossing at grade, excepting at crossings where there are established signal towers and signal men, interlocking plants, or gates. [1911 c 117 § 69; RRS § 10405.] 

1. Interlocking device is safety appliance which may be required by Com mission. State ex rel. P. S. & Willapa Harbor Ry. Co. v. N. P. Ry. Co., 94 Wash. 

10 (1916). 

2. Under this section, an order of the Department allowing a grade crossing and providing that the company shall "reduce" its speed so as to pass the crossing under control is invalid. State ex rel. Spokane International R. Co. v. 

Kuykendall, 128 Wash. 88 (1924). 

3. Validity and construction of Railroad Stop Statute. 2 A.L.R. 156. 

81.48.060 Penalty for violation of duty endangering safety. 

Every engineer, motorman, gripman, conductor, brakeman, switchtender, train dispatcher or other officer, agent, or servant of any railway company, who is guilty of any wilful violation or omission of his duty as such officer, agent, or servant, by which human life or safety is endangered, for which no punishment is specially prescribed, shall be guilty of a misdemeanor. [1909 c 249 § 277; RRS § 2529.]
